# korbat sub-project 3c: moderation cases

Date: 2026-10-03. Status: draft r8, written without the user under the standing instruction of 2026-09-23 (keep going; leave only account-dependent steps). It has been through six reviews (`korbat-scratch/research-3c/review-r1.md` to `review-r6.md`), the architecture pass (`simplify-r5.md`) and the pass on the path for suspected child sexual abuse material (`csam-r8.md`, with its lab `lab-gate-k`). The architecture pass restructured draft r4 by nine accepted simplification proposals, P1 to P9, each applied with the conditions its three judges set (`korbat-scratch/research-3c/simplify-r5.md` records them, their votes and what was removed or moved): restriction state is derived from a ledger of measures (P1); every restricting answer owns its measures (P2); deletion and moderation write disjoint columns (P3); suspicion of child sexual abuse material follows the material (P4); appeals have one shape, the author's, and notifiers get re-examination (P5); authorities' orders (P6) and strikes with handle retirement (P7) move to 3e; retention waits only for each kind of data's own users (P8); and each rule is stated once (P9). The path for suspected child sexual abuse material was rewritten in r8 to one model (6.1, the freeze; `korbat-scratch/research-3c/csam-r8.md` records the pass): P4's aim stands, and its mechanism is replaced. The decisions in section 2 were taken here and are flagged for review. It builds on the 3a design (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-28-staff-console-3a-design.md`, its section 15 included) and on the repository at `6897902`. Research: `korbat-scratch/research-3c/` (`repo-3c.md`, a map of the code after 3a; `law-3c.md`, the law with its obligations table; `practice-3c.md`, how working systems moderate; `threats-3c.md`, threats T39 to T62 and controls C1 to C40; ten gap answers `gap-G1.md` to `gap-G10.md`, each with lab measurements on PostgreSQL 18.6; and the review records `review-r1.md` to `review-r6.md`), and the earlier `korbat-scratch/research-3/{practice,threats}.md`. Where a gap answer contradicts an earlier file, the gap answer is followed unless the code says otherwise; every such case is named in section 3.

This design splits the 3c scope of the main design's amendment of 2026-10-01 into two designs (section 2, first row). This one, 3c, is designed in full: notices and reports, cases, decisions with statements of reasons on posts, images and profile fields, evidence, the author's appeal, the notifier's and reporter's re-examination, the author's and the notifier's screens and mails, the console's case screens, the export and the terms. The second, 3e (account sanctions and authorities' orders: strikes, suspensions and handle retirement, Explore's formula version 2, the authorities' orders path with the one-hour removal orders, and the alert channel), is described as scope only, in section 16.

## 1. Context

3a gave staff a console of their own, keys that open it, a step-up that confirms each action with the session's own key, and an audit log that is a hash chain. It gave them nothing to moderate. The trust-and-safety tables of migration 0000 (`cases`, `reports`, `appeals`, `strikes`, `transparency_log`, `packages/db/src/schema/safety.ts:11-96`) have no reader or writer in any app (`repo-3c.md` section 1.7), and they cannot hold what the law now asks: a `reports` row needs a reporter and a rule (`safety.ts:36,39`), so a notice from someone without an account, or about content illegal under a national law but outside R1 to R6, has nowhere to go (Digital Services Act Art. 16, `law-3c.md` section 1). Nothing in the code sets `withheld` or `removed`; every read already honours them (`repo-3c.md` section 2). An author can delete a withheld or removed post and its images today, and an account deletion blanks every post and deletes every image (`apps/api/src/social/repo.ts:480-497,1079-1121`), so the evidence of a case would vanish before anyone decided it.

korbat's promise is that content limits are narrow and public, that every removal cites a public rule, shows the author exactly what happened and can be appealed to a different human, and that there is no silent reach reduction (main design, Context). 3c is where that promise is built or broken. It must also meet the law that binds a micro platform now: Art. 16 notices from anyone, Art. 17 statements of reasons for every restriction, Art. 14 terms that describe the procedure, the TCO Regulation's terrorist-content policy in the terms (`law-3c.md` key findings 1 to 4, 8), Art. 18 and TCO Art. 14(5), which oblige korbat to tell the authorities promptly when it learns of a suspected offence threatening someone's life or safety (met in 3c by the runbook and `staff.js account-facts`; 3e records the notifications, `law-3c.md` 3), and the GDPR for notifiers, reporters and the people reported. And it must not give one or two tired moderators, or someone who stole their key, more power than the record can account for (`threats-3c.md` sections 3.4 and 6).

This design delivers: an intake that takes reports from signed-in users and Art. 16 notices from anyone into one model of intakes and items, merged into one open case per target as a database fact; a queue ordered by severity and age, with no weighting; decisions on posts, single images and profile fields, each carrying a statement of reasons whose every Art. 17(3) element is data, confirmed by the deciding person's staff key without any free text entering the chain; a ledger of measures from which every post's and image's restriction is derived by the database; evidence copied or held at the first report and protected from every destructive path; the author's appeal of a final restriction, reviewed by a different person, which the database enforces, and re-examination of "no action" for notifiers and reporters; the console's queue, case and appeal screens; the app's report form, notice status page and case screens; the mails; the export's moderation section; and the moderation parts of the draft terms and the rulebook.

## 2. Decisions taken without the user

Each row gives the decision, the reasons and flags that belong to it, and the alternatives rejected. The mechanics are stated once, in sections 6 and 7, and the rows point to them (P9).

| Topic | Decision | Alternatives rejected |
|---|---|---|
| Splitting 3c | Two designs. **3c, moderation cases** (this design, 12 tasks): intake, cases, decisions on posts, images and profile fields, the ledger of measures, evidence, the author's appeal, re-examination, statements, the screens and mails of authors, notifiers and reporters, the console's case work, the export, the terms and the rulebook. **3e, account sanctions and authorities' orders** (section 16, scope only, about 9 tasks): strikes and handle retirement, suspensions with their notice and reply, terminations and narrower restrictions with their enforcement on every write path, Explore's formula version 2 (G10), the whole path for authorities' orders (TCO removal orders within one hour, DSA Art. 9 and 10 orders, their records, clock, preservation and information duties), Art. 18 notification records, the self-hosted alert channel (G8) and `reverse-window`. Against 3a's task size (15 to 55 files each) the whole is about 21 tasks, and the seam is clean: 3e needs 3c's account cases, decisions, ledger and holds; 3c needs nothing of 3e. Order: 3c, 3e, 3d, 3b, 2d, 4, 5 (3d's automatic R2 suspension needs 3e's suspension). **Hard gate** (P6): korbat is not opened to the public and sign-up is not opened to people the operator does not know until 3e's order path and sub-project 3d's handling of sealed images (section 17) have both shipped, and no authorities' contact point (DSA Art. 11, TCO Art. 15) is published until 3e's order path ships. The TCO Regulation applies to a host insofar as it disseminates information to the public (Art. 1(2), `law-3c.md` 8.1), with removal orders executed within one hour (Art. 3), and DSA Art. 9 and 10 orders presuppose a service offered in the Union; a private korbat disseminates nothing to the public, and the runbook of 7.14 answers a stray order. The README states the gate and its reason (Task 12); any reordering that puts sub-project 4 before 3e reopens the move of the orders path, and one that puts it before 3d reopens what section 17 leaves to 3d. Flagged: the letter e is only a name; the owner may prefer 3c-1 and 3c-2. Flagged: until 3e there are no strikes and no handle retirement (a display name can still be reset), no order is given effect except by its author's deletion or an ordinary decision under a korbat rule, R2 and R5 cases alert staff by mail rather than a phone alert, sealed images are handed to an authority only by the operator's hand (runbook, Task 12(a)), nothing is produced under an order to provide information, Art. 18 and TCO Art. 14(5) notifications are made by the runbook and kept in the operator's own records, and a restored backup undoes every decision, review and seal confirmed after it, with their audit rows, while nothing on the server names them (C37's off-server copy is 3e's). Flagged (P7): the main design's line 185 moves wholly to 3e. | One design of about 21 tasks; splitting by layer, which leaves nothing testable end to end until the second half; keeping the orders path and strikes in 3c (in r4 every case rule needed an order exception for a path 3c could not run within the hour, and strikes restricted nothing in 3c yet took part in every outcome, reversal, appeal and telling rule; classes C8 and C9 of `simplify-r5.md`). |
| One model for every intake | An **intake** is one report, one Art. 16 notice or one moderator's own finding, with one item per target it names (a notice up to 10, recital 50); on the path for child sexual abuse material korbat adds an item for the post a named post quotes (6.1, the freeze). Each item lands in exactly one **case**, one open case per target, enforced by the database under concurrency (6.1; G3, measured in section 3). A decision answers the items up to the sequence number the moderator saw (on a case that holds an unanswered suspect item, up to the newest of those, 7.5 step 1) and is refused only when another decision landed since, so a brigade's new reports never block a decision and are never swallowed (G3 key finding 5). The case holds no rule: its lane is the most severe of its unanswered items (T51(f)). Each intake and item keeps its own row, ground, receipt and outcome (practice-3c key finding 1); reports and notices share one table with a channel (G9 3.1), and the app calls the control "Report". Flagged: G3 named the table `intakes`, G9 `notices`; `intakes` was chosen because own-initiative findings are intakes too. | A `reports` and a `notices` table (two receipts, two outcome paths, two ways to merge); merging reports into one row (each notifier needs its own decision, Art. 16(5)); refusing a decision whenever a new report arrives (G3: 121 closes out of 2,556 attempts on a hot target). |
| What a report or notice holds | One form with two grounds side by side, "breaks a korbat rule" (R1 to R6) and "is illegal" (an Art. 16 notice under EU or national law), neither under "other" (G9 3.4, Coimisiun na Mean's Art. 25 concern), and a third choice for the offences of Articles 3 to 7 of Directive 2011/93/EU that DSA Art. 16(2)(c) names (the CSAM path), which asks no name or email (EDPB Guidelines 3/2025 para 29) and never attaches a signed-in account (Coimisiun na Mean's investigations read Art. 16(2)(c) as a right to report CSAM anonymously, G9 2.1). Items are korbat links only, parsed on the server; no attachments (T45); the illegal ground asks for the four Art. 16(2) elements and one of the Transparency Database's 16 categories (read 2026-10-02, G9 2.5) (7.1, section 10). On the CSAM path a notice names at most one account (`CSAM_NOTICE_MAX_ACCOUNTS`), since one such target reaches every image on the account's posts (6.1, the freeze). Flagged (owner): that number. R2 is offered nowhere as a rule to report, and the contracts and the database refuse a user's intake citing it (`intakes_r2_finding_only`), so no report of child sexual abuse material carries an account; only a moderator's finding cites R2. Signed out, the form needs Turnstile, whose failure names the users' contact address because Turnstile fails closed (`apps/api/src/auth/turnstile.ts:2`); a report mailed there is entered through the form itself from the mail's text, never as a finding, which would state "found by a moderator" (Art. 17(3)(b)) and send no receipt or decision, and its attachments and links are never opened (the runbook, Task 12(a)). Staff report only as their own finding (C27), citing a rule; a finding never alleges illegality (`intakes_finding_legal`), so the rulebook's and the terms' "told in a notice" holds in the database. Flagged for counsel: content a moderator sees to be illegal on the face of it, but outside R1 to R6, stays up until a notice arrives, which can cost the Art. 6 shield for that item (`law-3c.md` 1.4); DSA Art. 7 would allow acting on it. Flagged for counsel: Turnstile on an Art. 16 form, attachments refused, and whether a signed-in person may attach their account to a CSAM notice voluntarily (G9 section 5). | A form only for signed-in users (Art. 16(1) binds for anyone); an email-only notice address (no receipt, no per-item decision); free-text URLs to other sites (moderators would follow them, T39, T45). |
| Where a notifier's name and address live | In the auth schema, encrypted (`auth.notifier_contacts`, under a new versioned key `NOTIFIER_ENC_KEY` backed up like `TOTP_ENC_KEY`, never re-encrypted), with a keyed hash of the address for caps and suppression; the public intake row holds only an opaque `contact_id`, and `korbat_api` cannot reach any of it (`0001_grants.sql:4`; 6.1, section 5). A signed-in notifier's contact is their account. Staff never see a notifier's address; a reveal names a signed-out notifier only by the name they gave. The contact is forgotten six months after the last day korbat still owed it something, or 13 months after the notice if nothing moved that date (G9 3.2, extended; 7.8 step 3(b)). Flagged: staff can never write to a notifier except through korbat's fixed templates. | The contact in the public `intakes` row, encrypted (still in `korbat_api`'s reach, against main design section 3b); showing addresses to staff (3a's rule that no staff answer carries an email, `apps/api/test/staff/route-table.test.ts:183`). |
| Mails to notifiers and reporters | A receipt at once to every notice with an address, or an account behind an illegal-ground claim; decisions in one daily dispatch at a fixed hour, one `notice_update` per address or account (7.8). Nothing is withheld for want of a confirmed address (Coimisiun na Mean: hearing nothing back "is against the rules", G9 2.2); an address unconfirmed for 30 days gets at most one receipt a day. A recipient the relay says does not exist, and a line unsent after five days (RFC 5321 section 4.5.4.1), are recorded `failed`. "Stop" is honoured, and its notices are still processed (Art. 16(6)); confirm and stop need the contact's mail token and a POST (T42, W52; G9 3.2), so typing another person's address neither lifts the cap for it nor stops korbat's mail to it. Rule-only reports are answered in the app's list at the same dispatch, in the same words. Mails carry only korbat-generated fields, never a report's words or the content (T43), and the daily batch hides the minute a decision landed from the reported author (T47). Flagged for counsel: whether a decision up to about 24 hours after it was made is "without undue delay" (Art. 16(5)), suppression, and the 30-day confirmation window (G9 section 5). | Holding decisions back from unconfirmed addresses (no support in any regulator's text); mailing each decision the minute it lands (it times the reporter against the author); mailing rule-only reports (no in-app notifications until 2d). |
| Queue order and weight | Nothing is weighted. The queue orders open cases by lane (0: R2, R5, the CSAM path, violence, public security, self-harm; 1: R3, R4, privacy, cyber violence, illegal speech, minors, notices whose category is "not sure"; 2: R1, R6, everything else; `laneFor`, section 10), then by the oldest unanswered item (7.3). Reporter standing, report counts and the badge are never keys (G9 3.8: weighting by standing makes the legal path slower for strangers by design, rewards aged brigades, and practice that works without auto-hiding orders by severity). A published emergency switch (`MODERATION_DEFER_UNPROVEN`) sorts cases whose open items all come from unproven sources after the others of their lane, never below a less severe lane, and in lane 0 only signed-out intakes count as unproven, so a new account reporting a threat against itself is never deferred (7.3). 3c drops `profiles.reputation`; write limits stay on account age (`apps/api/src/social/tiers.ts`), and nothing a decision records feeds a limit or a weight (T53, C28). Staff see a reporter as a summary computed on read, never a stored score, in bands, never exact counts, because the summary is the same on every case the person reported and must rarely single them out across cases (7.3). Flagged: this reverses the main design's report weighting from reputation (lines 146, 183-184) and the 2a and 2b designs' "sub-project 3 replaces account age with reputation". | Weight from reputation (main design line 146); Discourse's trust-level weights (they exist to auto-hide, which korbat forbids); deadline-first ordering (G9 T14 put an order due tomorrow above a one-hour-old R5 threat). |
| Case targets and measures | Two target kinds: a **post** and an **account** (its profile fields; in 3e, its standing); one image is a scope inside a post case, so a report about an image and one about the text merge (G3 key finding 1). Measures: on a post, `withhold` (interim only) and `remove`; on the images a case reaches, `seal_media` (while deciding or for good, on a post case or an account case; 6.2); on one image, `withhold_media` (interim only) and `remove_media`; on an account, `reset_display_name` and `reset_bio`; each a row of the ledger, and each reversal a row naming exactly one measure (6.1 `action_kind`, 6.2). A decision may hold several measures with one statement (TDB's own example combines four kinds, G3 3), at most one of them a post measure. **Every restricting answer owns its measures** (P2): a final decision that finds a breach records `restricted` with measures of its own, whatever the target's level or lifecycle (a removal of a post already removed or deleted, a second seal, an interim withhold of a post not visible); each stays in force on its own should another be reversed, and its statement, the notifiers' line and the console say when it changed nothing anyone sees now (`no_effect`, 6.2). No outcome closes an item by pointing at another case: a final decision answers `restricted`, `no_violation` or `not_evident`, and `not_available` is only the automatic answer to a link that names nothing live (7.1 step 3). A field reset writes its placeholder once, at its decision, and holds nothing afterwards. Its author may set the field again at any time. What they set is their own edit: it is judged on its own when someone reports it (the report takes a new snapshot of the account, 7.1 step 4), and a new decision resets it again, while deciding too (6.2 step 5). Holding a field against its author's edits would be a partial suspension of the service, which korbat imposes only as a measure with a statement of its own: 3e's narrower restrictions (G3 7.1). Flagged (owner): until 3e an author can set a reset field again at once, and only a new decision resets it again. Flagged: one image or one field instead of the post or the account is the least restrictive measure the model makes possible (Art. 14(4)); it costs `post_media.moderation_state` and a placeholder for others. Flagged (owner): an author may now receive several statements about one post that draws reports over time, and a moderator judges every later report about a removed or deleted post on its merits. A notice or report naming an account is answered for its display name and bio, and its notifier reads `ACCOUNT_ITEM_SCOPE` with the outcome (section 10). What the account's posts and images showed is decided in cases of their own. A notice or report naming a post that quotes another is answered for that post's own text and images, and its notifier reads `QUOTE_ITEM_SCOPE` with the outcome (section 10). The quoted post its card shows is decided in a case of its own. Flagged for counsel: a notifier who named an account, or a post whose card showed the post it quotes, is not told what korbat's own findings decided about the account's posts or the quoted post. Counsel should say whether Art. 16(5)'s "decision in respect of the information to which the notice relates" requires it. On the path for child sexual abuse material both differ (6.1, the freeze): a suspect item about an account is answered in a case that reaches every image on the account's posts, and its notifier reads `ACCOUNT_SUSPECT_SCOPE` with the outcome, as does the sender of any other item about the account that a decision holding `seal_media` answers, since that answer does concern the images on the account's posts; a suspect item about a post that quotes another brings an item on the quoted post, so its notifier reads an answer about each post and no `QUOTE_ITEM_SCOPE`. Flagged for counsel: on that path a notifier who named an account is not told what korbat decided, in cases of their own, about single posts of the account or about what its posts say (Art. 16(5)). | A target kind per image (two cases for one post); detaching a removed image (the sweeper deletes unattached uploads, `apps/api/src/media/repo.ts:157-160`); a hidden-field state on profiles (G3 7.1); r4's outcomes "Already restricted" and a moderator's "Not available", which closed items on a measure the case did not own, so another case's reversal republished content an order, a notice or an admin's R5 judgment concerned, and which needed refusal lists keyed on grounds (r3-8, r4-4, r4-5; class C2); strikes and handle retirement in 3c (row Splitting 3c). |
| Decisions and statements | A decision is the statement of reasons: every Art. 17(3) element is a column (the measures and their durations, the territorial scope, the facts, the source, automation, the legal or contractual ground with its explanation, the redress), in the Transparency Database's shape (`decision_ground`, `category`, `source_type`, the automated flags, a random `puid`) without personal data in any enumerated field, so 3d can publish it (`law-3c.md` 7.3; 6.1). The rulebook is versioned (`RULES_VERSION` 2; version 1 kept) and every decision records the rules and redress versions it was made under, so an old statement renders exactly as sent (practice-3c 11.4); one pure function, `statementParagraphs`, renders it for the author's screen, the console's preview and the mail, so what the moderator previews is what the author gets (practice-3c 10.4; section 10). The statement names the source category only, never a count, a reporter or a report's words (T47). `REDRESS_VERSION` 1 holds three texts: korbat's appeal, for a final restriction; the interim text, for a decision taken while deciding; and the text for a decision its author cannot appeal (section 10); 3e adds an order's. The author is told, in the app at once and by mail, of every decision that restricts, reverses, or ends or lifts a measure taken while deciding, and of every review (6.2 step 10); an interim measure on a target already deleted, which can never take effect, is recorded without a mail. Legal-ground restrictions state their territorial scope as "everywhere": korbat cannot restrict by country (`law-3c.md` 1.5 option (ii)). Flagged: telling authors of no-action decisions (T46 suggests it for legal-ground notices) was not adopted, because it would tell them that someone reported them. Flagged: worldwide scope is more restrictive than a single state's law requires, and the statement says so. Flagged for counsel: whether a final decision on content its author had already deleted is mailed to them; Art. 17 does not require it, and the mail discloses that a report arrived and may tip off a suspect in an Art. 18 case; 3c mails it. | A free `public_reason` text only; rendering old statements with the current rule (Mastodon's flaw); storing every rendered statement as text (personal text kept twice, and the versions make re-rendering exact). |
| Free text under the step-up | G1's option (a), "draft first, then confirm". Every free text a decision, a review or a reveal carries is saved first, without a step-up, into the erasable table `staff_texts`, salted and digested by the console; the server refuses, never changes, a malformed text or one that quotes the case's reports, notices, re-examinations or appeal statements (C19; 7.4). The confirming params carry only ids, enums, integers and digests, so 3a's step-up, `checkStepUpOptions`, `audit-verify` and the chain's canonical text do not change, and the chain never holds readable personal text (T56); erasure nulls the text and keeps the digest; snapshots get a salted digest made by the server at capture. Three writing staff routes therefore stand without a step-up, by name: saving a text, taking a fresh snapshot, and opening a case on the moderator's own finding; none changes anything a user sees (a saved `seal_declined` text ends an urgent repeat and is listed on the home screen, 7.12), and each enters the record only through a confirmed decision that names its digest or its case. Opening a finding still has lasting effects (holds, possibly lane 0; citing R2, it freezes what its case reaches (6.1, the freeze) and makes the final decision an admin's), so findings are limited per staff account and UTC day and listed on the home screen (7.1, 7.3). Flagged: the three exceptions to 3a's rule that every changing staff route asks for the key (3a section 2, Confirming an action); a route-table test lists them. | G1's option (b), the text beside the params (it changes 3a's "the body is the params" rule and wastes a key touch on every refused text); option (c), the text in the params (unerasable for ever, T56); unsalted digests (a short reason or a guessed address could be confirmed by hashing guesses, EDPB Guidelines 02/2025 para 52). |
| The confirmed transaction on the API pool | G2's design: a migrator-owned definer function, `append_confirmed_audit`, EXECUTE to `korbat_api` only, is the last statement of every 3c step-up transaction, so nothing commits after a revocation, a take-down or the challenge's expiry (6.2; G2 measured in section 3). The lock-wait bound moves to `apps/api/src/lib/lock-bounds.ts`, shared by the auth module, the command line and the moderation module (7.5); it only aims to end the transaction before the expiry, which the function itself guarantees. `korbat_api` keeps no USAGE on `auth` (`0001_grants.sql:4`). Flagged: `korbat_api` gains the power to hold a staff account's row `FOR SHARE` for as long as it keeps a transaction open, which can stall `staff.js revoke` until its 10-second timeout, the class of stall 3a already accepts for the audit head (G2 3.6). | An auth-pool transaction around the API-pool one (two connections per decision, a wait the deadlock detector cannot see, G2 4.5); `GRANT USAGE ON SCHEMA auth TO korbat_api` (undoes 0001 and makes "revoke PUBLIC on every auth function" a standing rule). |
| No moderation state without a decision | A post's and an image's restriction is never set; the database derives it (P1). Every measure and every reversal is a row of the append-only ledger `decision_actions`, inserted only in the transaction of its own confirmed decision, which the audit link binds to a staff key (6.2). A measure is in force while no row reverses it and, for an interim measure, while its case is open, so interim measures end by themselves when the case's final decision closes it (a seal taken while deciding ends with an admin's answer to the suspect items instead, 6.1 `measures_in_force`). One migrator-owned function, `moderation_apply`, is the only writer of the moderation columns: under the post's lock it takes the strongest measure in force over every case, and under the image rows' locks each image's state (6.2). The result does not depend on the order in which cases, measures and reversals arrive, no case's effect reasons about another's, a new kind of measure only joins the maximum, and an auditor can recompute it (`staff.js text-verify` does). No reversal writes content. A plain trigger on `posts` and column grants keep every other writer off (6.1). Flagged (owner): a lifted or ended field reset does not give the old value back. The person types it again and, when korbat still keeps the case's copy at that moment, reads it on the screen of the decision that reset it for at least 30 days (6.4, 6.6), unless an R4 redaction erased it first. A lift after the copy was erased (a correction after the restriction became final, or after an R4 redaction of the account) finds none, and its statement then makes no such promise (section 10). Keeping the resetting decision's snapshot for as long as a final reset stands was not adopted: it would keep content found to break a rule, for a lift that may never come (GDPR 5(1)(c), (e); P8). Flagged: the 18 places in 7 test files that set a post's state directly (G4 6) move to a fixture that makes a real confirmed decision. | r4's imperative effects, in which each trigger moved `posts.state` and a restore moved a post back only from the state the reversed action set and only when no other removal stood: every round found an ordering of cases, reversals and states the effects missed, the source of most high findings of rounds 2 to 4 (r2-14, r3-9, r3-33, r4-8, r4-14; class C1); G4's deferred "xact" guard on `posts` (it proved only that a matching row was inserted, and needed a time pairing against logical restores, r1-19); G4's `case_events` table (decisions and their actions already are the audited events); case events as `audit_log` rows (a second large index and every event inside the head's lock, G4 4); a second chain head (3,510 deadlocks in 20 s once one path took the heads in the other order, G4 3). |
| Lock order | One order for every transaction that touches moderation: profile rows first (sorted by id, each in the strongest mode the transaction will need), then cases (in their targets' order), then, inside a decision's triggers, the deciding account's and the subject's `auth.users` rows `FOR SHARE` and the cap's advisory lock, then the case's own post, then image rows in id order, then inserts, the audit row and the deferred checks (6.2). A decision locks the subject's profile `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` first, so two decisions about one account serialise; the `FOR KEY SHARE` of likes and follows (`lockLive`, `apps/api/src/social/repo.ts:1160-1163`) never waits for it, and a follow of the subject, a post by the subject or a like of the decided post waits only at its counter update (`repo.ts:609`, `471`, `539`), with no cycle. The author's deletion of a top-level post, which today updates the post and then the profile's `post_count` (`repo.ts:480-497`), takes the profile first (G5 4.2: that one change removed every deletion cycle). An intake takes its cases in their targets' order, then its post targets `FOR SHARE` in id order, then every image row it reaches in one global id order `FOR KEY SHARE` (7.1); it never locks a post it reaches only through an account. The R4 redaction takes the case, then the post, then the post's images in id order (6.4). A seal takes image rows only, in id order; no decision locks a post other than its own case's (6.2). The author's deletion and the purge delete image rows in id order, as `dropDisposable` does (7.10), so nothing locks image rows in another order than an intake that reaches them through an account, which takes no lock on their posts. Flagged: the account purge still updates posts before other accounts' profiles (G5 4.3); a decision that meets it rarely deadlocks, and the decision's one retry covers it. The purge takes its own posts in id order (7.10). Flagged: the intake, the redaction, a seal and a closing decision against `moderation_apply` are measured in the contention lab (section 12). Flagged (owner): measured under load (section 3, lab `lab-gate-k`), the purge of an account with 5,000 image rows takes 2 to 4 s and with 20,000 rows 7 to 16 s, the keep trigger reading the freeze once for each row, and an account's seal takes 1.5 s at 5,000 image rows and 5.9 s at 20,000. Task 2's lab measures both again on a quiet machine. | C8's "case, post, profile" (it deadlocked with likes and replies, G5); taking `FOR UPDATE` on the profile (blocks every like and follow of the account for the decision's length; 3e needs it for suspensions, G5). |
| Recusal and who may decide | A staff account cannot decide where it is a party (the case's subject and the signed-in reporters of the items the decision considers) or where a personal account the operator linked to it (`staff.js link`, `auth.staff_personal_accounts`, readable by no API role) is a party or acted towards one: follows, blocks, mutes, a reply in the last 90 days, or the judged post replying to or quoting it (C27, Wikipedia's "involved" rule, practice-3c 2.7). Only the linked account's own acts count, since anyone can follow or reply to a staff member's posting account alone. The same check on the case's subject refuses opening a case, its images, snapshots and texts, and every reveal in it, and it does not look at reporters, so a refused read says nothing about who reported (6.2 `staff_case_conflict`; 7.3). No final decision may restrict a staff account (`staff_target`, G5 6): staff are disciplined by demotion through grant-role, while an interim measure applies to them as to anyone, so a threat posted from a staff account stops being public at once, and images on a staff account's posts that are reported as child sexual abuse material can be sealed while deciding. Whether a decision needs an admin is a stored fact, `decisions.needs_admin`, forced by the triggers and read by everything that asks (P4; 6.2 step 6, `decision_actions_check`): final decisions that answer a suspect item (6.1, the freeze) or consider an item citing R5, or that cite R2 or R5; every lifting of a seal; corrections of such decisions; and reviews of a decision that sealed images or cites R2 (`review_needs_admin`, 6.3), a review of an R5 restriction staying any eligible moderator's (round 1 decision 11). Interim decisions never need one, so whoever is on duty can seal reported images and withhold a threat while the admin is away, and anyone eligible may lift a measure taken while deciding, except a seal, which only an admin lifts (7.7). Flagged (owner): "senior" becomes "admin"; with one admin, final R2 and R5 decisions and every answer to a suspect item wait for them. Flagged (owner): the first seal is taken while deciding, by any moderator, on a report nobody can check, and hides the images from their author too (r7 showed a post withheld while deciding to its author). Its author cannot appeal it. It lasts until an admin who is not a party answers: with one admin, days, and with that admin recused, without limit; the queue-age mail counts it after 24 hours. The owner may choose r7's form, in which the author loses sight only when an admin seals for good; korbat then serves a file reported as child sexual abuse material to its uploader for as long as the admin is away. Flagged (owner): one moderator's decision while deciding seals every image of an account, also on a finding that moderator opened, and only an admin lifts it. In r7 such a decision hid one post. The decision caps allow 60 decisions an hour; the home screen lists every seal taken while deciding that is in force (7.3). Flagged (owner): reviews inheriting every `needs_admin` (R5 included) was proposed and not adopted, because with one admin those appeals would wait for good (T60); the owner may choose it. Flagged: no "obvious case" exception for a recused moderator (practice-3c 10.20 allows one with a recorded justification); with one moderator the case waits. Flagged: the link names a staff member's posting account inside the auth schema; an API under an attacker's control can still test whether an account is linked or close to one (section 11). | A device-hash comparison between parties and staff (a linkage fact about staff and users, T48's optional item); trusting staff to recuse themselves; computing the admin requirement at each check from the case's composition and from history across cases (r2-13, r3-17). |
| Evidence | Text by copy, images by reference (G6). The first report, notice or finding on a target, and every fresh snapshot, copies the post's or profile's content into an append-only snapshot with a server-made salt and digest, under the post's `FOR SHARE` lock, so an author's deletion comes after it or leaves a tombstone the copy records (G6 E3c); a capture of erased content copies none of it (6.1, T52). Images are held on their own row, which every delete path skips (G6 E3 to E5b); the images and the snapshots of a frozen post are kept by two gates of their own, whatever case holds them and whether a hold stands or not (6.1, the freeze; the keep trigger measured on PostgreSQL 18.6, section 3); one disposal predicate deletes what nothing retains (7.9). The decision names the snapshot and the digest the console computed from what it displayed (T52). Retention follows each kind of data's own users (P8, 6.4, 6.6). Flagged: the snapshot's digest enters the chain only with the decision that names it, not at capture (an intake by anyone would otherwise write a chained row, and an API compromised before the report can forge consistently anyway). Flagged (owner): snapshots of a no-action case go about a day after it closes instead of six months after the notifiers' release (a privacy gain, published in the terms), so a re-examination judges the target as it then stands, and an intake's texts may outlive their case's snapshots by up to six months and 30 days. Flagged (owner): a report or finding of child sexual abuse material, a false one included, keeps every image its case reaches that its author deletes meanwhile, those of a purged account included, unserved, until an admin answers (T41). Flagged: a suspect item about an account copies the account's fields, not its posts' texts. The body of a post its author deletes afterwards is lost unless some case had copied it; the alt texts stay on the kept rows. Flagged for counsel: rows 2, 4, 7, 10, 11, 15 and 17 (backups) of G6's table, and: how long korbat may and must keep sealed images, and on what basis, given that 3c's software has no function to erase them (Directive 2011/93/EU Art. 5 and recital 17; national law); on which basis it keeps, on an unverified report, images their author deleted and the images of a purged account until an admin answers, and the snapshots of a frozen post in other cases past their own periods (GDPR Art. 5(1)(e), 6(1)(c) or (f), 17(3)(b) or (e)); that an account case's own evidence goes by its ordinary due rules after a seal for good while the images are kept; and whether korbat may keep the account facts it printed for a report to the police until the admin answers. Flagged (owner): backups are deleted 30 days after they were taken (`RETENTION.backupDays`, 6.6), a period the terms publish and sub-project 4's backup job must keep. | Snapshots of media ids alone (the files are swept within the hour, G6 E1); copying image files (a new worker protocol, disk); keeping evidence only for decided cases (the author deletes first, T18); a foreign key from evidence to the image row (it made the author's deletion fail, G6); a separate holds table (it left holds pointing at nothing, G6); one case-wide due time over every decision, appeal, item and mail of a case and its intakes' other cases (r4's `moderation_due`, which gained a term for each late path and still made evidence due too early or never, r3-22, r4-17; class C7). |
| What the author sees of removed content | Ordinary removals (R1, R3, R5, R6, and the law): the author keeps seeing the body and images in their own view and export while they keep the post (main design line 191: never silently gone; GDPR Art. 15(3)). R4: as written until the restriction is final (for a frozen post, until the freeze ends), then redacted (6.4), with the chip "Removed: R4" and "Content erased when the R4 decision became final" on the decision screens (Art. 15(4), EDPB 01/2022 paras 172-173). Images reported as child sexual abuse material: once sealed (6.2), from the first seal taken while deciding, they reach no reader, their author included (Directive 2011/93/EU Art. 5(5)): each is a placeholder in every view, "Sealed: not shown to anyone, you included" for its author and the placeholder of a hidden image for anyone else, and the export holds none of them (7.10, 7.11). Text is never sealed: the post keeps its text and its place in its author's views and export, and a text that itself breaks a rule is restricted by the ordinary measures under that rule, which leave it with its author as any removal does. A withheld or removed image goes to its author with its state and to others as a placeholder that does not say which; a sealed one, and one an R4 redaction erased, to nobody. Flagged (owner): text is never sealed. The author of a post whose images are sealed keeps seeing its text, and so do others, unless a moderator restricts the post under a rule or the law (the runbook says to withhold the post as well when its text links to or offers such material); r7's hidden sealed post and its amendment of main design line 191 are withdrawn. Flagged (owner): others see "Image hidden by korbat moderation" on each sealed image of a post that stays public; after an account's seal that is every image post of the profile. r7's withhold while deciding took the post out of others' sight without a mark. Flagged for counsel: Directive 2011/93/EU Art. 2(c) defines the material as visual (read 2026-10-03), but it sets a minimum. Counsel says whether the home Member State's law counts words (a written depiction, instructions) as such material, and what the recast's adopted definition says; a summary of the Commission's proposal has it cover instructions whatever their form. If words can be the material, a text removed under R2 cannot stay in its author's view and export, and the seal needs a form for text; that an author may keep their own words is this design's reading. Flagged for counsel: the author's own sealed images are left out of the export and of an access answer on a report nobody checked (GDPR Art. 15(3) and 20): on which basis (Art. 15(4), Art. 23 and national law), and what the answer says; korbat's copy may be the only one. Flagged for counsel: the R4 redaction of a frozen post waits for the admin's answer, so a doxxed address there outlives the restriction's finality until then. Flagged for counsel: whether removed R3 or R5 bodies naming a third person may stay with their author without end (G6 row 2). | Hiding every removed post's content from its author (breaks "never silently gone"); serving sealed images to their uploader (making them available); sealing the post with its text, as r7 did (a post state every reader of text had to learn, snapshots withheld from the staff who had just read them, captures without content, an export rule, and a report to the police that had to be written before the seal because nothing showed the text afterwards; the Directive's definition is visual). |
| Deletion and cases | The author's deletion and the purge write only lifecycle columns and the author's own content, and delete only image rows nothing retains; they never write a moderation column or row. Moderation never writes a lifecycle column: on the one shared column, `posts.state`, deletion only moves it to `deleted_by_author` and moderation never moves it to or from that value, for every role (6.1 `posts_guard`). Moderation writes content only two ways, each one-way: a field reset at its decision (nothing on a deleted profile) and the R4 redaction (6.4). So cases about deleted targets go on and are decided on their snapshots, measures on them are recorded and in force, nothing writes into a row the purge anonymised, and the redaction acts whatever the post's lifecycle (P3). Liveness is one rule for every route (7.1 step 3): a suspect item's target is live while its row exists, a deleted post and a purged account included, so no report of child sexual abuse material about anything that exists is answered automatically, an account also under a handle it has left, which `handle_history` keeps reserved for it; for every other ground a deleted post or account is a target with nothing live. While a post is frozen, no delete path takes its images and no erasure its snapshots (6.1, the freeze). The retention job's `dropDisposable` is the one deleter of images a case kept: its predicate chooses the candidates (7.9), and gate K (`post_media_keep`) decides for every delete (6.1, the freeze). Flagged (owner): a report of child sexual abuse material about a post its author deleted, or about a purged account, is not answered automatically: it waits for an admin, and what is left of the post's images is kept until then (T41: a false report can keep deleted images that long, and costs an admin a decision). Flagged (owner): deletion no longer stops a case from producing a decision. | A deleted post with a held image live for every ground (repeated reports would keep content its author deleted and put deleted images before staff for no protective purpose, T41, GDPR 5(1)(e)); r7's liveness for this path, which read whether an image was still held and so needed an exception for each way a deleted post could still show images (r6-1, r6-3); liveness from `posts.state` with a ground-keyed exception (r4-1, r4-3); reversals that write content back into deleted or anonymised rows (r1-33, r2-24, r4-7); four image deleters (r1-10, r2-22; class C4). |
| Suspected child sexual abuse material | Nobody at korbat opens it. The mechanics are stated once, in 6.1 (the freeze) and 6.2 (the seal); this row gives the decision and its flags. A report on the CSAM path, or a moderator's finding citing R2, is a **suspect item**. While it is unanswered, every post its case reaches is **frozen**: a post case reaches its post, an account case every post of the account, and a report about a post that quotes another is taken in as two items, one for each post, so no case reaches another author's post. Frozen means three things, each enforced at one gate that every path of its kind must pass: no image row of the post is deleted and no file of it requested (a trigger), no snapshot of it is erased (the one function that erases snapshots), and no staff member is served its images, on any case, open or closed (the one function that opens an image for staff). A report hides nothing by itself. A person does, with one measure, the **seal**: it seals every image the case reaches, for everyone, their author included; whoever is on duty takes it while deciding, and an admin takes it for good or answers without it. Only images are sealed, never text. Every suspect item about something that exists waits for an admin, who answers all suspect items of a case at once, and only an admin lifts a seal, a seal for good only on the answer of the authority the images were reported to (6.2, the seal; 7.7). On a seal for good the operator reports to the national police or the authority its Member State names, never to a hotline, whose analyst assesses what an address shows while a sealed image answers 404 to everyone (`research-3/practice.md` INHOPE paragraph), and an admin seals for good only what korbat then reports (runbook, Task 12(a)). Every staff account is mailed at once after a decision that lifts a seal or answers a suspect item without one, and the home screen lists them for 30 days, as for reveals (7.5 step 4, 7.12). 3c keeps what it sealed: the release, the quarantine and the replay after a restore are 3d's, and the record of the report to the authority is 3e's (sections 16, 17). Flagged (owner): a report of child sexual abuse material hides nothing by itself. From the report until a staff member seals the images while deciding they stay public: for as long as it takes one of one or two people to read a mail, hours at night, a day or more when both are away. The urgent mail, repeated about every 20 minutes, is the only alert until 3e's phone alert, and lane 0's target is 24 hours. After a seal, Cloudflare's edge and browsers may still serve a copy for up to five minutes, until 3d's purge. Until the seal only the rule that staff never open a reported post in the app keeps them from seeing it, and after it the app still shows what the case does not reach. The person who seals cannot look: here "a person decides" means that a person may refuse, in a flood or on a plain repeat, and acts under a name, a key, a record and the caps. What shrinks the window without a program deciding on a report is 3e's phone alert and 3d's hash match, both before sub-project 4. Flagged (owner): the seal on an account case covers every image on the account's posts, because nobody can tell which ones the report means. So one anonymous report that names an account, and that nobody can check, can hide every image the account ever posted from everyone, its author included; the report form asks for the post or the image instead where the sender can give it (section 9). An admin who can tell from the report's words which post is meant opens a finding on that post instead and answers the account's item without a seal. The account's notifier then reads its answer with `ACCOUNT_SUSPECT_SCOPE`, and the statement of the finding's seal names a moderator's finding as its source. Flagged for counsel: whether Art. 17(3)(b) needs that statement to say that it followed a notice. Flagged (owner): a seal covers the images that exist when it is taken. Until 3e's suspension an account whose images are sealed can post new ones; while its item is unanswered they are frozen and the urgent mail starts again, after a recorded choice not to seal as after a seal (7.12), and after the answer they need a report of their own. Flagged (owner): a report of this kind about a post that quotes another opens a case on the quoted post too, in the urgent lane, so that author's images are frozen, and may be sealed, on a report that named someone else's post; the notifier may have meant the quoting post's own images. An added item does not count against a notice's ten targets, so a notice of ten quoting posts opens twenty urgent cases. Flagged (owner): after an admin's answer without a seal, moderators may open the images (as round 2 decision 21 allowed for a closed case), and every staff account is mailed. That answer is, in effect, the decision that staff may now look: the first person who opens an image afterwards is the first to learn what it is. Flagged (owner): 3c's software has no function to release, hand over or erase sealed images; it keeps them, and the operator does either by hand on an authority's written instruction (runbook). korbat does not meet people the operator does not know before 3e and 3d have shipped (row Splitting 3c). A restore before then loses every freeze and seal recorded after the backup: sealed images are back as public as they were at the backup until a staff key is enrolled again and an admin seals again, and the retention switch is what keeps the job from deleting them meanwhile (README, Restoring). Flagged (owner): a false report can get every image of an account sealed until the authority answers, which no law obliges it to do. An admin's seal for good is taken without anyone having seen the images; korbat then reports the author to the police with what it holds about the account, the author's appeal waits without a time limit, and 3c has no release. The admin's other answer, without a seal, is as unseen, and after it staff may open the images. Flagged (owner): a report of this kind, a false one included, takes the images of every post its case reaches out of staff's sight on every case until an admin answers. Reports on other grounds about those images, corrections and the author's appeals are meanwhile decided on text alone or wait; an admin's answer also answers the earlier reports of its case unseen (their senders are told so and may ask again); and whoever sends a new report after each answer, the author of the post included, keeps it so, at five notices an hour from one network. What breaks the loop is a person: staff open the images after an answer without a seal, and the next answer carries the measure for what they saw (6.1, the freeze; 7.5 step 1; runbook, Task 12(a)). Flagged (owner): the statement of a seal tells its author of the report at once, and Art. 17 gives korbat no right to delay it; an author who then deletes the account takes with it the address korbat could have given the police. So whoever has the server runs `staff.js account-facts` as soon as the urgent mail arrives and keeps its output off the server until the admin's answer (runbook); the seal never waits for it. Whether an account under a seal can be deleted before the report is made is 3e's to decide (section 16). Flagged (owner): a report of this kind about an account reaches neither the posts it reposted nor the posts its posts quote, though its profile shows their images; and one about a post reaches neither the post it replies to nor its replies, though the post's page in the app shows them with their images. So staff never open a post or an account in the app while a suspect item about it is unanswered or its images are sealed (6.1, the freeze), and an admin who can tell from the report's words that such a post is meant opens a finding on it. Flagged: after an answer without a seal the case page shows, for a post, the day staff last opened one of its images in the console (7.3, `imagesOpenedOn`); nothing on the server but a note records a look at an account's images in the app, and a note goes with its case's evidence (6.4; runbook). Flagged: a delete that fired before a suspect item was committed, on a row the intake could not lock, wins: the report then came too late for that image, as it would have a moment later. Flagged: a stream of suspect items into one case can keep an admin from answering it; that fails closed, and an admin can still lift a wrong seal taken while deciding by a correction (6.2, `decisions_record` step 6). Flagged: a suspect item that was answered by a measure on text without a seal cannot be re-examined, and when the item korbat added for a quoted post is closed to re-examination, a re-examination of the quoting post's item freezes that post alone; the notifier's way to bring the quoted post's images before an admin again is a new notice (7.2). Flagged for counsel: whether leaving reported images public until a staff member acts is expeditious under DSA Art. 6(1)(b) for a provider that does not open the material; whether a statement of reasons that says the images were reported as child sexual abuse material and that korbat's staff do not open such images meets Art. 17(3)(b) and (d) or (e); that no provision lets korbat delay that statement, though it tells a suspect of the report before the police act (`research-3/practice.md` 2.3); whether Art. 18(1)'s "promptly" runs from the first seal, taken while deciding, or from the admin's answer; whether a notice that nobody at korbat examined is information giving rise to a suspicion under Art. 18(1), and whether giving the author's data to the police on it is covered by GDPR Art. 6(1)(c); and whether a decision on a notice taken without examining the content, either way, is diligent and objective under Art. 16(6). Flagged for counsel: whether sealing every image of an account on a notice that names the account is proportionate (Art. 14(4)), and whether a seal taken while deciding that an admin does not answer for days is. Flagged for counsel: the statement to the author of a quoted post says that the decision followed a notice and that no automated means were used; the notice named the quoting post, and korbat's software added the item (Art. 17(3)(b) and (c)). Flagged for counsel: `intake_items.suspect`, `decisions.suspect`, `decisions.unseen`, `seal_posts` and the counts of earlier answers are records of a suspected offence about a person, and decisions are kept for good as rows (GDPR Art. 10). Flagged for counsel: what the home Member State's law adds: a duty to report, a rule against telling the suspect, and what "without right" (Directive 2011/93/EU Art. 5(1), recital 17) gives a host that keeps the files for the police. The owner may still choose otherwise, each at its cost. (a) Hiding the images from the report on: it closes the window, and costs an automated decision and statement on one anonymous report nobody checked, against main design lines 172, 180 and 184, with GDPR Art. 22 to answer, and it makes the anonymous path a switch by which anyone hides any account's images until an admin is back, again after every answer without a seal; 3d's hash match seals automatically in any case. (b) r7's form, in which the author keeps seeing the images until an admin seals for good: it keeps main design line 190 whole, and has korbat serve a file reported as child sexual abuse material to its uploader for as long as the admin is away. (c) A timed seal: when nobody has sealed, or recorded a choice not to, within a published number of hours, the database seals while deciding by an automated decision whose statement says so; it bends "a moderator decides" only where no moderator came, and its abuse is (a)'s, delayed. (d) A guard for staff in the app: a session whose role is not `user` gets a placeholder for every image in every view, so that a staff member signed in as staff cannot be shown a reported image there; it costs a rule in the view builder and does nothing for staff who browse signed out. | Viewing it behind a step-up (possession, and the harm to the one or two people who would see it daily, T41); sealing automatically on report volume (main design line 180 forbids it); suspicion computed from which cases ever held which items, and a seal that moved the body out, flagged every snapshot of the post, held images with seal holds and copied the body back on reversal (each new path to the same material needed a rule of its own: r2-19, r2-21, r2-22, r3-27, r3-28, r4-2; class C3); r7's model, in which suspicion was computed from open cases through three joins while holds were rows written at intake for the images that existed then, so that the two sets differed on account targets, quoting posts, deletions, the purge, retention and the redaction, and the seal existed only on a post case (r5-1 to r5-4, r6-1 to r6-8; `korbat-scratch/research-3c/csam-r8.md`); a mark written to each reached image at intake (a second copy of the truth, written to thousands of rows by an anonymous request and unwound row by row); the quoted post as a branch of the predicate (a seal on the quoting post's case would restrict another author's post under a decision about someone else); refusing account targets on this path (an anonymous notifier does not come back, and Art. 16(6) has every notice processed); a seal that hid text (row What the author sees of removed content). |
| Reveals | A reporter's handle or a signed-out notifier's given name is revealed to a moderator only through the step-up action `identity_revealed`, with a reason category and a saved reason text, written to the record and the access log and listed on every staff member's home screen for 30 days (C20); one person may reveal at most 10 sources a UTC day, and every reveal is mailed at once to every staff account, the person's own included, since a reveal cannot be undone and a stolen key acts at the speed of key prompts (T50, C22; 7.3). A notifier's address is never revealed. Opening a case, viewing a snapshot and unblurring an image need no key: they are logged, and a moderator who has opened 400 different cases in a UTC day is refused others and every staff account is mailed (C22; 7.3). Flagged: the default numbers (400 case opens, 10 reveals). | A step-up for every case opened (fatigue, T61, with no protection the access log does not give); revealing addresses for "verification". |
| Appeals and re-examination | Appeals have one shape (P5): the author's appeal of a final decision with outcome `restricted` while any of its measures is in force, one per decision, within six calendar months of being told with a month-end clamp in UTC (Ozone's rule, practice-3c 10.9), its statement optional and free of form (recital 58). A review answers that one appeal (`kept`, `partly_reversed`, `reversed`), never restricts and never adds a measure; the database refuses a review by anyone who decided in the case, opened a finding in it, or is a party (C30); with one moderator, appeals wait, and the author's screen says so and why (T60, fail closed; 6.3, 7.7). Interim decisions are not appealable: their measures end with the case's final decision, which can be appealed if it restricts, and anyone eligible may lift one early, except a seal (row Recusal). Notifiers and signed-in reporters get **re-examination**: within six months of the release of an item a moderator answered `no_violation` or `not_evident`, they may ask once, with an optional statement, and the request becomes a new item of the same intake on the same target, decided and told like any item (7.2); an item korbat added for a quoted post is re-examined like any other, and an answer given while nobody could open the post's images (`UNSEEN_NO_ACTION`, section 10) does not spend the one request: it can be asked again, also when it answered a re-examination. Art. 20 does not bind korbat (Art. 19(1); `law-3c.md` final table: NOT BINDING, ADOPT); recital 58's aim, a free, electronic, human re-look at "no action" within six months, is kept, and Art. 16(5)'s redress information names re-examination, the courts and the Digital Services Coordinator. Flagged (owner): the promise to notifiers changes form, and the terms and the status page say so. Flagged (owner): the database does not refuse the original decider on a re-examination, because a final decision answers a contiguous range of items, so such a refusal would block every later report on the target while korbat has one moderator (Art. 16(6)); the console marks the item and the README asks another staff member to take it; a guaranteed different decider needs a design of its own. Flagged: korbat cannot keep the "different human" promise with one moderator; the appeal waits without a time limit. Flagged: with one admin and one moderator, every R2 or R5 case the moderator withheld or sealed while deciding, or opened as a finding, and the admin then decided, leaves the author's appeal without a reviewer until a third staff member joins, who must be an admin for a decision that holds `seal_media` or cites R2 (`review_needs_admin`, 6.3), and the terms say so. Flagged: the author cannot contest an interim withhold or a seal taken while deciding inside korbat, and with one admin away an R2 or R5 one can last days, so a case holding an interim measure for more than 24 hours sends the queue-age mail whatever its lane (7.12). Flagged: recital 58's "not adequate" is met for "no action" only. Flagged: notifiers get the item's outcome, not the reviewer's reason (`law-3c.md` 5.1 had adopted it from Art. 20(5), which does not bind korbat). | One appeal per case (`appeals_case_idx`, `safety.ts:67`: a merged case with many notifiers could then hear one); a cooling-off re-read by the same moderator (T60); approve and reject as independent updates (practice-3c 11.3); r4's appeals by three appellant kinds with one result, restricting reviews and superseded interim appeals (each combination needed its own rule, words and reviewer exclusion, and notifiers' appeals used up the different reviewer the author needed: r1-30, r1-35, r2-31, r4-10 to r4-13; class C6). |
| Limits on staff | Per-staff decision caps counted by the database under an advisory lock, 60 an hour and 300 a day by default, raised only by `staff.js limits` (the single-row table `moderation_limits`, which also holds the daily case-open, findings (50) and reveal limits), and a mail to every staff account when one person confirms 20 decisions within 15 minutes (C24; 6.2 step 7, 7.5). No batch action across cases in 3c: one decision answers every open item of its case (up to the newest suspect item where the case holds one unanswered, 7.5 step 1), which covers a brigade on one target; closing one reporter's items across many cases waits for 3d's brigading signals. Flagged: the caps' numbers, the findings and reveal limits; the absence of a cross-case batch. | A "confirm for 15 minutes" window (3a rejected it: a hidden page could act several times); batches over several authors (T50: one prompt, many effects). |
| Retiring the old tables | Migration 0014 drops `cases`, `reports`, `appeals`, `strikes`, `transparency_log` (empty, never used, `repo-3c.md` 1.7), their enums and `profiles.reputation`. 3d defines its transparency table from 3c's decisions; 3e defines strikes as ledger measures. Flagged: the main design's `transparency_log` (section 2) is then 3d's to recreate in the Transparency Database's shape. | Keeping them beside the new tables (two models, allow-all policies and default UPDATE and DELETE grants that the threat model asks to revoke, U1). |
| Alerts before 3e | Until 3e's phone channel, staff are alerted by mail to every staff account, with counts and times only and no link (7.12): `moderation_urgent` when a case enters lane 0, a re-examination included, repeated while a lane-0 item waits unanswered with no interim decision, or, on a post not visible or an account case, no note, since it arrived; for a suspect item whose case reaches an image that is still served, repeated until the images are sealed, a staff member records that they do not seal, or an admin answers, and again once an image is posted afterwards that the case reaches (7.12); and `moderation_waiting` when a case passes its lane's response time (24 hours in lanes 0 and 1, seven days in lane 2) or holds an interim measure for more than 24 hours (C23). The note silences the repeat on a post that is not visible, and on an account case, because an interim measure there changes nothing (on an account, nothing when the content lies in a handle) and mails its author at once, telling them a report arrived (T47; P2 security condition (c)). Flagged: a mail is not a pager; the README says so. Flagged: the three response times. | No alert at all (T41, C23); Web Push from the console (measured blocked under the console's policy, G8 5.1); a flood alert on signed-out intake (G9 3.5): Turnstile and the per-network limits bound it, a lane-0 flood already alerts, and the waiting mail and the home screen show the rest. |
| The terms and the rulebook | 3c writes the moderation parts of the draft terms and the rulebook (section 9), without a new `TERMS_VERSION` (`draft-2026-09` stays, as 1b, 1c and 2c did): the grounds, the procedure, the tools and human review (DSA Art. 14(1)), notices, re-examination, appeals and their rules of procedure, evidence kept after deletion and why, the terrorist-content policy TCO Art. 7(1) requires now, and the rewritten sentence "removed only when it breaks a published rule" (`apps/app/src/lib/terms.ts:18`) and the rulebook's "complete list of reasons" (`packages/rules/src/index.ts:2-4`), which 3c's code makes false. R1, R2, R4, R5 and R6 change as section 9 gives them, each with its reason. The terms' lists of measures, outcomes, limits and periods are built from the same constants as the mechanism and pinned by tests (P9). No new rule for terrorist content: it is illegal content handled through notices (and, with 3e, orders), with Art. 1(3)'s exception for journalism, research and polemic stated. 3e adds strikes, suspensions and orders. Sub-project 4 has the whole text reviewed and publishes the first non-draft version with the re-acceptance flow; nobody signs up publicly under the draft (G7 7.2; row Splitting 3c). | A new terms version now (the app, the API setting and the tests change together for a text that is still a draft); a rule R7 for terrorist content (the definition is the Regulation's, and a rule would invite rule-based removals of polemic). |
| One statement per rule | Each rule is stated once, where the code implements it from: database rules in section 6, each with its reason and, in full, its lock modes and order, the statement boundaries after a lock, its SQLSTATEs, forced columns, grants and lab references; flows in section 7 (P9). Section 2 keeps decisions, alternatives and flags and points to them. The composer's refusal words (section 8), the outcome and redress phrases (section 10), the terms' lists (section 9) and the README runbook (Task 12) are each checked against one table (the contracts' constants, `messages.ts`, `@korbat/rules`), with a test per table (section 12). Every legal anchor and every counsel and owner flag sits next to the rule it qualifies, and section 11 keeps the full list of what stays impossible even for an admin, with its mechanism and its test. Counsel still reviews the derived terms in sub-project 4. | Restating each rule in sections 2, 6, 7, 8, 9, 10 and the runbook (in r4 the texts lagged the mechanism, a class of its own in every round, C12, and each round grew the design by about 50 KB). |

## 3. Research (facts the design depends on)

- **The code after 3a** (`repo-3c.md`, checked against the files cited here). The five trust-and-safety tables are unchanged since 0000 and unused; `reports.reporter_id` and `rule_id` are NOT NULL (`packages/db/src/schema/safety.ts:36,39`); `reports_reporter_target_idx` is unique for ever (`:48`), so a reporter could never report a target again after its case closed (G3 L14); `cases_target_idx` is neither unique nor partial (`:29`); every one of them is allow-all for `korbat_api` with the default UPDATE and DELETE grants (`:29,50,67,79,95`; `0001_grants.sql:16`). `audit_log` has exactly one index, the unique `chain_seq` one, pinned by `packages/db/test/schema.test.ts:488`. `appendAuditRow` (`apps/api/src/staff/audit.ts:43-60`) is the staff module's writer for public tables, unused by 3a; the late-confirmation guard (`boundLockWaits`, `appendAuditBefore`, `LateConfirmation`) is private to the auth repository (`apps/api/src/auth/repo.ts:1487-1538`) and runs on the auth pool. `requireStaffStepUp` treats the whole body but `stepUp` as the action's params, validated by the action's strict schema (`apps/api/src/auth/staff/step-up.ts:158-178`), and `audit-verify` fails for good any row of a step-up action without a valid assertion, whatever its actor (`apps/api/src/cli/staff.ts:332-334`), and any row stamped at or after its challenge's expiry (`:373`). `STAFF_STEP_UP_ACTIONS` holds one action and the options request is a union listed by hand (`packages/contracts/src/staff.ts:154-157,173`); a step-up target id must be a lower-case uuid (`:147`), and a schema with a transform (`MediaId`, `packages/contracts/src/media.ts:20`) cannot be a params member. The only writers of `posts.state` set `deleted_by_author` (`apps/api/src/social/repo.ts:484,1093`); the author may delete a post in any other state (`:480-497`); the purge blanks every post and deletes every image row (`:1079-1121`; `apps/api/src/account/flows/deletion.ts:21-26`). The API updates only `body`, `state`, `deleted_at` and the four counters of `posts` (`repo.ts:469-562,1084-1102,1169-1172`). `lockLive` takes `FOR KEY SHARE` (`repo.ts:1160-1163`). `postAccess` (`apps/api/src/social/visibility.ts:18-23`) shows a post in any state but `visible` and `deleted_by_author` to its author, so a new state would fail open there; `visibleTo` (`repo.ts:318-321`) and `serve` (`apps/api/src/media/flows/serve.ts:31-35`) name only `withheld` and `removed` and fail closed; the export prints every post's body whatever its state (`repo.ts:976-990`, `apps/api/src/account/flows/export.ts:166-170`), and `readyImages` returns every ready image of the owner (`apps/api/src/media/repo.ts:131-138`). Images follow their post: visible ones are public for five minutes, withheld and removed ones go to their author only (`serve.ts:27-48`); views and the export carry every ready image (`media/repo.ts:114-138`). The console's policy allows images from its own origin only and its site proxies only `/api/staff` (`apps/admin/site-headers.mjs:9-10`; `infra/caddy/Caddyfile:82-99`); its source scans allow `href={` only in `src/nav.tsx` and no `src={` at all (`apps/admin/test/source.test.ts:65,73`); `ConsolePath` is pinned to three paths (`apps/admin/src/router.ts:7`). The staff route table pins the route list and its one path parameter, and refuses answer members named like `email`, `address`, `userId` or `accountId` (`apps/api/test/staff/route-table.test.ts:183,186-204,282`). Only the auth module holds addresses, the mailer and Turnstile (`apps/api/src/auth/index.ts:138-140,184`); its notices are best effort (`apps/api/src/auth/flows/notify.ts:4-11`); templates are a closed union of 25 (`apps/api/src/mail/mailer.ts:1-27`). `@korbat/rules` is a dependency of the API only (`apps/api/package.json:21`), and no source file imports it today. Profiles hold no image: `avatar_path` is only ever nulled (`repo.ts:1118`). drizzle-orm 0.45.2 runs every pending migration in one transaction (`node_modules/drizzle-orm/pg-core/dialect.js:60-71`): a value added with `ALTER TYPE ... ADD VALUE` to an enum created by an earlier, committed migration cannot be used before that transaction commits, while on a fresh install the type is created in the same transaction and the value is usable, so a lab run only on a fresh database would hide an upgrade failure.
- **One open case per target** (G3, PostgreSQL 18.6). A check-then-insert without a partial unique index made two open cases; with it the second insert waited and was refused (L6). `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (target_type, target_id) WHERE closed_at IS NULL DO NOTHING` followed by an unlocked read let an R5 report attach to a case a moderator had just closed (L7); a migrator-owned `BEFORE INSERT` trigger on items that updates the open case and raises `serialization_failure` when it is closed made every such insert retry into a new case (L8, L10: 10,128 items, 34 retries, 0 failures). Decisions that answer through the sequence the moderator saw and refuse only when another decision landed: 18,900 items, 2,729 decisions, 0 violations of seven invariants (L11). Two notices naming the same two posts in opposite orders deadlocked; sorted by target they merged (L12). Without UPDATE on `cases`, `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` is refused for `korbat_api` even with no conflict; `DO NOTHING` works (L15).
- **The confirmed transaction on the API pool** (G2). A migrator-owned definer function in `public` reads `auth.users` and `auth.staff_keys` for `korbat_api` with 0001's grants unchanged, and the owner bypasses their RLS (3.1). `FOR SHARE` on the account's row, then on the key's row in a second statement, serialises a decision with a revocation or a take-down in either order; `FOR KEY SHARE` let a decision commit after its key was revoked; a single statement locking only the account let a revoked key through; key-first deadlocked with a revocation (3.2, S1 to S10). `korbat_api` may `set_config('lock_timeout', ..., true)`, which bounds every wait inside the definer guard and the chain's trigger (3.3). 3a's clock check works on the API role, and `RETURNING created_at` gives the exact stamp `audit-verify` compares (3.4).
- **Triggers, roles and statements** (G4, and the labs of review rounds 1 to 4, PostgreSQL 18.6). Deferred constraint triggers run at COMMIT after the audit insert while the head is held, add only ACCESS SHARE locks, and a caller can only make them earlier (`SET CONSTRAINTS ... IMMEDIATE`), never skip them (G4 5.3, 5.4); one can require that the transaction's last audit row names the event (5.5). `korbat_api` cannot disable a trigger, set `session_replication_role`, or lock a table it holds only INSERT and SELECT on (T15). A plain (not SECURITY DEFINER) trigger sees the role that ran the statement as `current_user`; `korbat_api`'s `SET ROLE`, `set_config('role', ...)` and `SET SESSION AUTHORIZATION` to the migrator are refused; and a plain trigger fired by a statement inside a migrator-owned SECURITY DEFINER function sees the migrator (labs of rounds 3 and 4). A logical restore into a fresh cluster restarts the transaction id counter while restored rows keep their stamps (measured: counter 773 after restoring stamps of 3761 and 3762), so a stored `xid8` is compared together with the transaction's start time (G4 6). An AFTER INSERT trigger on `decisions` sees none of its decision's actions, which are inserted after it (lab, round 4). Under READ COMMITTED each statement of a VOLATILE PL/pgSQL function takes a fresh snapshot, so a read in a statement of its own after a lock sees every row committed while the lock waited, while one statement whose subquery read a table before its row lock waited keeps the old snapshot (labs of rounds 2 to 4: `media_holds_apply`, `erase_case_evidence`, the restore of r4). Drizzle 0.45.2 nests transactions as savepoints that postgres 3.4.9 never releases (`node_modules/postgres/src/index.js:258,269,275-278`), so a row's `xmin` differs from `pg_current_xact_id()` after any nested block (G4 5.1).
- **Lock order** (G5, 60-second contention runs). A profile `FOR UPDATE` deadlocks with today's `deletePost` (post first, then the author's `post_count`) 32 to 38 times a minute; taking the author's row first removed every deletion cycle (V1fix, 4.2). Post-then-profile deadlocked 55 to 87 times a minute with likes and replies (V2, O2). Today's follow and unfollow already deadlock with each other with no moderation at all (B0). A transaction already holding a profile row upgrades without queueing behind a waiter (4.1). Column-level UPDATE grants keep `korbat_api` off a column while Drizzle's updates keep working (they name only the columns they set); inserts name every column, so INSERT stays table-level and a `BEFORE INSERT` trigger forces the guarded columns (P1 to P13). A definer bridge reading `auth.users` `FOR SHARE` decides "is this account staff" consistently against a concurrent promotion; `FOR KEY SHARE` does not (section 6).
- **Holds** (G6, lab E1 to E6). Every image file is deleted by a request file the API writes, which the worker obeys within about a second and cannot check (`apps/api/src/media/files.ts:180-183`; `apps/worker/src/loop.ts:68-83`); the reconcile pass sweeps any stored image older than an hour without a row (`apps/api/src/media/sweeper.ts:65-89`). So a hold must keep the `post_media` row: a column on the row was the only race-free form (a concurrent delete waits for the hold, re-reads and skips the row); a `BEFORE DELETE` trigger that returns NULL for a held row protects every present and future delete path, and must raise on a cascade (depth above 1), where returning NULL silently breaks foreign keys (E5d, E5b).
