A hold that comes after the author's deletion finds nothing, so holds belong in the intake transaction (E3b). A hold insert must lock the image rows first, in id order (lab, section 5, `MediaForCases`).
- **The freeze's keep trigger** (lab `korbat-scratch/research-3c/lab-gate-k`, PostgreSQL 18.6, four runs on 2026-10-03 under READ COMMITTED unless said; the machine was under load, so the times are upper bounds). A `BEFORE DELETE` row trigger on `post_media` that reads the freeze in a statement of its own sees a suspect item that committed while the delete waited for the intake's row locks: the ordered delete (`delete ... where id in (select id ... order by id for update)`) and a plain `delete ... where post_id = $1` both waited for the intake's `FOR KEY SHARE`, then deleted nothing and returned no id, for a post target and for an account target, whether the trigger read with `SELECT ... INTO` or with a plain assignment (T1 to T8, C1, C2). Without the intake's row locks the delete did not wait and won (T5), and an account item that committed with no lock while a purge of 20,000 rows ran kept only the 15,820 rows the purge reached after the commit (run 3, E); the intake's lock statement cost 77 ms and 287 kB of WAL at 5,000 rows and 102 ms and 1.1 MB at 20,000 (H). The same predicate as a filter of the deleting statement deleted the rows, since it runs on the statement's snapshot (T9; G6 E4 again). A predicate that found the post's author through the `posts` row did not refuse a cascade from a deleted post, because for the trigger's statement that row was already gone (T11); a core that reads no `posts` row, called with the image row's own `post_id` and `owner_id`, raised SQLSTATE 23001 at depth 2 for a cascade from a deleted post and from a deleted profile, under each kind of freeze (A1 to A6). An update guard refused moving an attached image, detaching it and attaching an upload to another account's post, and let an owner's own attach pass, so the owner of an attached row is its post's author (B1 to B4). Under REPEATABLE READ and SERIALIZABLE the trigger's read saw the transaction's old snapshot and the rows went; raising unless `transaction_isolation` is `read committed` kept them (run 4, I). As a SECURITY DEFINER function the trigger read the truth for a deleting role with no right on `cases` or `intake_items` (J). As three index-driven arms the predicate cost 6 buffer hits for a post nobody reported among 100,000 closed and 2,000 open cases (F). The purge of one owner took 0.5 to 0.7 s at 1,000 image rows, 2.3 to 3.6 s at 5,000 and 7 to 16 s at 20,000, kept or deleted alike (T13, D); an account's seal over image rows only took 0.3 s at 1,000, 1.5 s at 5,000 and 5.9 s at 20,000 (G).
- **Free text and digests** (G1). jsonb refuses the NUL character (U+0000) in a string and lone surrogates (L1 to L3); SQL, Node and WebCrypto compute the same salted digest (L9, N5); a CHECK can hold a stored text to its digest, and erasure then nulls text and salt (L10). Params of ids, enums, integers and hex digests survive the jsonb round trip with the same action hash (N3, N4).
- **Intake rules** (G9, lab T1 to T16). The CHECK constraints of section 6 accept every legitimate intake and refuse a CSAM notice carrying an account or a contact, an illegal-ground notice without explanation or bona fide statement, an old Transparency Database category and `OTHER_VIOLATION_TC` on an illegal ground; `korbat_api` cannot read a notifier contact and `korbat_auth` cannot read a notice (T13). Ordered by lane, the queue is served by one partial index without a sort (T15).
- **The law** (`law-3c.md`, re-read from the Publications Office on 2026-10-02). Art. 16 binds every host: a mechanism for anyone, electronic, "at least as easy" as the rules report (recital 50), facilitating the four elements of 16(2) with name and email asked but not required and not collected for child sexual abuse (EDPB Guidelines 3/2025 paras 29 to 31); a receipt and a decision "without undue delay" to whoever left contact details, the decision with redress and any automated means (16(4) to (6)); every notice processed (16(6)). Art. 17 binds: a statement for every restriction, "at the latest" when imposed, with the six points of 17(3). Art. 20 does not bind a micro platform (Art. 19(1)); korbat adopts its standards for the author's appeal and keeps for notifiers recital 58's human re-look at "no action" through re-examination (`law-3c.md` 5.1). Art. 23(2), suspending an abusive notifier's notices, does not bind and collides with 16(6). Art. 14(1) binds: the terms describe every moderation tool and step and the appeal's rules of procedure; Art. 14(4) asks for diligent, objective and proportionate enforcement. TCO Art. 7(1) binds now: a terrorist-content policy in the terms; the Regulation applies to a host insofar as it disseminates information to the public (Art. 1(2)), and its removal orders run within one hour (Art. 3). The Transparency Database's schema (updated 1 July 2025) is the shape to store, with 16 categories (G9 2.5). GDPR: a reported person's access reaches the source as a category, a reporter's identity is withheld after a case-by-case balance (`law-3c.md` 9.6); a copy must not adversely affect others (Art. 15(4)), which supports making an R4 address illegible and never handing back images reported as child sexual abuse material (G6 8).
- **Practice** (`practice-3c.md`). No surveyed tool merges reports into one row; the case is the unit of decision and the report the unit of intake and feedback (key finding 1). Ozone's appeal window is six calendar months with a month-end clamp and one function decides both what the screen offers and what the API accepts (`O:inbox/appeal.ts:74-84,283-285`). Mastodon previews exactly what the author will receive before the moderator confirms, but renders old decisions with the current rule text (3.2). No tool's code checks that an appeal's reviewer differs from the decider (5.2). Evidence decays before appeals are heard where it is not kept (Hachyderm, 6.1).
- **Corrections between the research files.** C8's lock order "case, post, profile" is replaced by G5's profile-first order (measured). C9's guard keyed on `case_events`, which r4 kept in G4's "xact" form keyed on `decision_actions`, is replaced by the ledger and its derivation (P1): the record is still the decisions and their actions, bound to the chain by the audit link. C13's "name and email optional for CSAM" becomes "not collected" (EDPB para 29), and `law-3c.md` 1.2's "does not attach a signed-in reporter's account" now rests on Coimisiun na Mean's investigations, not para 29 (G9 2.1). C14's question whether decisions may be withheld from unconfirmed addresses is answered no (G9 3.6). C23's "accounts under 48 hours last" applies only under the emergency switch (G9 3.8). C32 and C34 (orders from the console or the command line) are 3e's, with the orders path. C26's "once per post, only from a reversal" is replaced by G10's resume rule, in 3e. T52's unsalted snapshot hash is salted (G1 5.5).

## 4. Scope

In (3c): migrations 0014 to 0018 (the moderation tables, their grants, triggers and functions, the ledger and its derivation, the auth-side notifier and staff-link tables, the columns on `posts` and `post_media`, the table `seal_posts`, the retired tables dropped); the contracts (`packages/contracts/src/moderation.ts`, `canonical.ts`, three step-up actions in `staff.ts`); the rulebook's versions and statement rendering (`packages/rules`); a new API module, `apps/api/src/moderation/`, with its app routes, its console operations and two jobs; the auth module's `AuthForModeration` (Turnstile, notifier contacts, mails to accounts, notifiers and staff), the social module's `SocialForModeration` and readers that fail closed on every state they do not know, the media module's `MediaForCases` and the honouring of the freeze, of sealed images and of the disposal predicate by every image reader and deleter; the staff module's case routes; `staff.js link`, `unlink`, `limits`, `text-verify`, `account-facts` and `access-request`; the console's queue, case, decision, appeal and reveal screens; the app's report form, notice status page with re-examination, case screens, chips and report controls; nine mail templates; the export's `moderation` member; the draft terms' moderation sections and the rulebook's texts; configuration (`NOTIFIER_ENC_KEY`, `NOTIFIER_ENC_KEYS_PREVIOUS`, `MODERATION_DISPATCH_HOUR`, `MODERATION_DEFER_UNPROVEN`, `MODERATION_RETENTION`, each passed to the api service by `infra/compose.yml`); the README's moderation section and runbook.

Out: everything of 3e (section 16, and section 2's first row), among it strikes, handle retirement and every part of the authorities' orders path, the record of a report to the police and the hand-over of sealed images on an authority's request. 3d's: the public transparency and rules pages, the transparency table, R1 scoring, R2 hash matching with the automatic seal, the quarantine of sealed files, the release of sealed images with its replay after a restore, the rule for backups that hold released material and the period for which sealed images are kept (section 17), upload hashing, the Cloudflare cache purge (until then, an image removed or sealed in 3c stays at Cloudflare's edge for up to five minutes), brigading signals, publishing the audit head, the count of average monthly active recipients in the Union that DSA Art. 24(3) obliges korbat to give the Digital Services Coordinator and the Commission on request (Art. 3(p) counts signed-out readers; measured without personal data), and submitting statements to the Transparency Database if korbat ever opts in. Sub-project 4's: the reviewed terms and privacy policy with counsel, the governing law, the first non-draft terms version and its re-acceptance flow, per-IP engagement limits. Also out: per-country withholding, attachments, machine translation of notices (moderators use a local tool, runbook), an "obvious case" recusal exception, claims on cases, a cross-case batch, staff notes shown to users, author notification of no-action decisions (except a decision that ends or lifts a measure taken while deciding, section 2), appeals by notifiers and reporters (they have re-examination).

## 5. Architecture

A new module, **moderation** (`apps/api/src/moderation/`, role `korbat_api`, the API pool only), owns every moderation table but `media_holds` (the media module's) and the auth-side tables (the auth module's). Its index is the only file other modules import; the architecture test's `MODULES` gains it, with the rules the social, media and staff modules already follow: it imports no auth-schema table or enum from `@korbat/db` and never names `post_media` (`apps/api/test/architecture.test.ts:28,115-125`). Its files: `context.ts` (deps, `ModerationError` and its messages), `links.ts` (korbat link parsing), `intake.ts` (reports, notices, findings, re-examinations, snapshots, holds and the liveness rule of 7.1 step 3), `cases.ts` (queue, case reads, reporter summaries), `texts.ts` (the saved texts), `overlap.ts` (the overlap check, a pure function), `decide.ts` (the confirmed decision transaction), `appeals.ts` (the author's appeals and the reviews), `reveal.ts`, `author.ts` (the author's reads: the cases list, the decision view, `authorFacts`, the export's `decisions` member), with their SQL in `author-repo.ts`, the only queries the author's surfaces are built from; `reports.ts` (the reporter's list and the export's `reports` member), `statement.ts` (the data a statement and its mail are rendered from), `dispatch.ts` and `retention.ts` (the two jobs), `export.ts`, `routes.ts` (the app's routes) and `repo.ts` (the rest of its SQL, raw like the social module's reads, Drizzle builders for inserts). `createModeration` returns `routes`, `forStaff` (`ModerationForStaff`, the console's operations), `forSocial` (`ModerationForSocial`, the facts a post view needs for its author's chip), `forAccount` (`ModerationForAccount`, the export's member and the purge's forgetting), `startJobs(lock)` and `settle()` (7.1 step 5).

It reaches the other modules only through interfaces they export, as the account module does (1b design, section 5):

- **`AuthForModeration`** (`apps/api/src/auth/index.ts`, beside `AuthForAccount` and `AuthForStaff`): `verifyHuman(token)` (Turnstile, `deps.turnstile`); `storeNotifierContact(contactId, { name?, email? })` (normalise, encrypt under `NOTIFIER_ENC_KEY`, keyed hash of the address and, when an address is given, a fresh mail token stored encrypted beside it; disposable domains accepted; every member that reads a contact decrypts with `NOTIFIER_ENC_KEY` and the keys of `NOTIFIER_ENC_KEYS_PREVIOUS` and writes nothing back); `mailNotifier(contactId, message)`, which sends a signed-out notifier's receipt, applies suppression, confirmation and the 24-hour cap, and returns `sent`, `folded`, `suppressed`, `no_address`, `forgotten` (no contact row has the id: it was never stored, 7.1 step 5, or the janitor deleted it after its `forget_after`) or `refused`; and `mailAccount(userId, message)`, which applies neither confirmation nor the cap (an account's address was confirmed by its sign-in code), applies a suppression of the account's address (its `emailHash`) only to the report mails `notice_received` and `notice_update`, never to `moderation_decision`, and returns `sent`, `suppressed`, `no_address` (a purged account) or `refused`; its `notice_update` carries `signedIn` true and no receipts or pages. Both answer `refused` when the relay refused the recipient for good (`MailRefused`, below) and throw `MailUnavailable` for any other failure, so the caller records `failed` or retries (they never use `notifyAccount`'s best effort). `mailNotifierUpdates(parts)`, each part `{ contactId, ref, lines, receipt? }`, `lines` a `NoticeUpdateLine[]` (the mailer bullet below), `receipt` `{ ref, time, items }` (`ref` is the contact's notice's reference; `receipt` is a folded receipt's `{ ref, time, items }`): groups the parts by their contacts' address, which only the auth module can see, and sends one `notice_update` per address with `signedIn` false, every part's lines, every carried receipt with its page link, and the page link of each other notice it has a line for (`pages`, 7.15). It applies suppression but neither confirmation nor the cap. It answers per contact `sent`, `suppressed`, `no_address`, `forgotten`, `refused` or `unavailable` (the relay could not take that address's mail; nothing is thrown, since other addresses' mails went). It reads each contact in a try of its own: a contact it cannot read (a ciphertext no configured key opens, `apps/api/src/auth/crypto.ts:64`, or any other error for that contact) is logged with the contact's id, never an address, and answered `unavailable`, so its lines stay `pending` until 7.8 step 6 gives them up while every other address's update is still sent. `mailStaff(message)` (every live staff account's address, the account a mail names included, best effort: it never throws for a failed send and answers the number of addresses the relay accepted, 0 when it took none or there is no live staff account, for the six staff templates `moderation_urgent`, `moderation_waiting`, `moderation_burst`, `moderation_reads`, `moderation_reveal` and `moderation_csam_lifted`; it applies no throttle, since the moderation module keeps every throttle as a key in the shared limit store, 7.12); `notifierFacts(contactIds)` (`{ found, named, contactGiven, confirmed, suppressed }` each; for an id with no row, never stored or forgotten, `found` is false and the other four are false); `notifierName(contactId)` (the given name or null, for a confirmed reveal only); `notifierMailTokenMatches(contactId, token)` (decrypts the mail token and compares it in constant time; false when there is none); `confirmNotifier(contactId, token)` and `stopNotifier(contactId, token)`, which act only when the token is the contact's mail token and answer false otherwise (confirm records the address as confirmed for 30 days and removes any suppression of it; stop suppresses it); `accountReportMailStopped(userId)` (whether the account's address, by its `emailHash`, is suppressed) and `resumeAccountReportMail(userId)` (deletes that suppression and answers whether there was one), for the account's own Settings: the session proves the address, which the account's sign-in code confirmed; `forgetNotifierAfter(contactIds, at)` (sets `forget_after` of each); `findNotifierContacts(email)` (command line only: the contacts whose keyed hash matches an address, with their given names, for an access request from a signed-out notifier, G9 3.7); `overdueContacts()` (the number of `auth.notifier_contacts` rows whose `forget_after` is more than a day past plus the number of `auth.notifier_confirmations` rows whose `expires_at` is more than a day past, for the overview's `erasureOverdue`, 7.3; a count only). A `message` is a typed union over the user-facing templates, so no caller passes free text. The auth module builds every page link in its mails to a contact itself, as `{APP_ORIGIN}/notice/<ref>#t=<mail token>`. The moderation module never sees a mail token, and no mail ever carries the notice's own token.
- **The mailer.** `smtpMailer` (`apps/api/src/mail/smtp.ts:30-34`) throws `MailRefused`, a new subclass of `MailUnavailable` in `apps/api/src/mail/mailer.ts`, so every existing caller (`apps/api/src/auth/flows/email.ts:37` among them) behaves as today. It throws it when nodemailer's error has `code` `EENVELOPE`, `command` `RCPT TO`, a `responseCode` from 500 to 599, and a `response` (the relay's reply, `smtp-connection/index.js` `_formatError`) that begins with an RFC 3463 enhanced status saying that the recipient's address itself is bad, matched by `/^5[0-9][0-9][ -]5[.]1[.](1|2|3|6|10)(?![0-9])/`: 5.1.1 (no such mailbox), 5.1.2 (no such destination system), 5.1.3 (a malformed address), 5.1.6 (the mailbox has moved) or 5.1.10 (the domain accepts no mail, RFC 7505). No retry changes such an address, and RFC 5321 section 4.2.1 says a client should not repeat a request after a 5yz reply. Every other failure stays a plain `MailUnavailable`: a connection or TLS error, `EAUTH` (a 535 included), a refused `MAIL FROM`, a refused `DATA` or message, any 4yz reply, and every other 5yz reply to `RCPT TO`, with or without an enhanced status. Postfix checks its client, HELO and sender restrictions at `RCPT TO` by default (`smtpd_delay_reject = yes`), so a relay that refuses korbat itself (relaying denied, `554 5.7.1`, missing credentials, a sender it refuses, 5.1.7, 5.1.8, 5.7.x, or a quota) answers 5yz there for every recipient; such mails wait and are sent once `API_SMTP_URL` or the relay is fixed, or are given up after five days (7.8 step 6). `smtpTransportOptions` (`apps/api/src/mail/smtp.ts:12-21`) sets explicit time limits: `connectionTimeout: 10_000`, `greetingTimeout: 10_000`, `socketTimeout: 30_000` and `dnsTimeout: 10_000` (nodemailer 10.0.10's defaults are 2 minutes, 30 seconds, 10 minutes and 30 seconds, `smtp-connection/index.js:11-14`). A relay that does not answer, or answers and then stalls, then fails a send within about 50 seconds, as a plain `MailUnavailable`, and every existing caller gains the same bound (a sign-in code answers `mail_unavailable` within a minute). A message the relay took but acknowledged after 30 seconds is retried and may arrive twice; that is accepted. `MailMessage` (`apps/api/src/mail/mailer.ts:29-33`) becomes a union discriminated by `template`: either `{ to: string; template: "notice_update"; data: NoticeUpdateData }` or `{ to: string; template: Exclude<TemplateName, "notice_update">; data: Record<string, string> }`. The other templates keep flat strings, so the 25 existing templates, `need`, their callers and their tests are unchanged. `renderTemplate(template, data)` keeps its signature for the flat templates, and `renderNoticeUpdate(data)` renders the one structured template. `smtpMailer` and `MemoryMailer` (`smtp.ts:29`, `memory.ts:12`) call whichever one the message's `template` selects, and `MemoryMailer.lastCodeFor` reads `data.code` only from a flat message. `NoticeUpdateData` (in `mailer.ts`) is `{ signedIn: boolean; lines: NoticeUpdateLine[]; receipts: { ref: string; time: string; items: string; pageUrl: string }[]; pages: { ref: string; pageUrl: string }[]; termsUrl?: string }`. `NoticeUpdateLine` is `{ ref: string; item: number; link: string; outcome: string; reexamination: boolean; changed: boolean; reexaminable: boolean; unseen: boolean; automatic: boolean }`. Its fields: `link` is the item's address as 7.2 builds it (an account's from the handle that was named); `outcome` is the line's text from section 10's phrases, with `ALREADY_NOT_SHOWN`, `ACCOUNT_ITEM_SCOPE` or `ACCOUNT_SUSPECT_SCOPE`, and `QUOTE_ITEM_SCOPE` added where 7.8 step 4 says; `reexamination` marks the answer to a re-examination; `changed` marks the new outcome of an item that a review or a correction changed; `reexaminable` marks a moderator's `no_violation` or `not_evident` on an item that is not re-examined and is either no re-examination or one that reads `UNSEEN_NO_ACTION` (7.2 leaves that one open); `unseen` marks a line that reads `UNSEEN_NO_ACTION` (the item's `unseen` of 7.2 with a no-action outcome), re-examined again or not, so that the template never prints "final within korbat" for it (7.8 step 4); `automatic` marks a link that named nothing (a case-less `not_available`). The moderation module sets each line's fields. The auth module adds `signedIn`, the receipts' and pages' links and `termsUrl`. The template chooses every sentence of 7.15 from these fields alone.
- **`SocialForModeration`**, exported by `apps/api/src/social/index.ts` as `socialForModeration(apiDb)`, a factory over the social repository like `mediaForAccount(deps)` (`apps/api/src/media/index.ts:46-55`): `lockAccounts(tx, ids, mode)` (one statement, sorted by id, `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` or `FOR KEY SHARE`, refusing a missing or deleted account where the caller asks); `capturePost(tx, postId)` (the post `FOR SHARE` with its author's handle, body, state, times, reply and quote ids, or null; when the post's `erased_at` is set it answers the body as `''`, so no capture copies erased text, 6.1 `evidence_snapshots`); `captureProfile(tx, accountId)`; `findAccountByHandle(handle, withDeleted)` (with `withDeleted` true, for a suspect intake, it also answers a profile whose `deleted_at` is set and, for a handle that no profile holds now, the profile that `handle_history` records for it, 7.1 step 3; with `withDeleted` false an old handle names nothing, as 1b decided); `postAuthor(postId)`; `postRef(postId)` (the post's author, state and `quote_of_id`, or null when no row has the id; no lock; the liveness rule and the added item of 7.1 step 3 read it). A factory, not a member of `createSocial`'s result, because the social module takes `ModerationForSocial` for its views and the moderation module is built between the two.
- **`MediaForCases`**, exported by `apps/api/src/media/index.ts` (`mediaForCases(deps)`):
  - `hold(tx, pairs)`, each pair `{ caseId, postId }` (lock and hold) or `{ ownerId }` (lock only; for a suspect item whose target is an account, 7.1 step 4). It first locks, in one statement and one global id order, every image row the pairs name: the rows of each post and, for an owner pair, every row of that owner that is on a post (`select m.id, ... from post_media m join posts p on p.id = m.post_id where m.post_id = any($1) or m.owner_id = any($2) order by m.id for key share of m`: the posts are read, never locked, here). `post_media_guard` (6.1) makes the owner of an attached row its post's author, so an owner's rows on posts are exactly the images of that account's posts. Then, for the post pairs only, it inserts the case's hold, in `media_id` order, on each row that statement returned that has no unreleased hold from that case and whose own and whose post's `erased_at` are null (so no report prolongs the retention of an image that an R4 redaction erased); a row that `dropDisposable` deletes meanwhile is skipped rather than failing the intake on `media_holds`' foreign key. An owner pair gets no hold: the images of a frozen post are kept by `post_media_keep` whether a hold stands or not (6.1, the freeze), and the lock is what makes a concurrent delete wait and then read the committed item. The lock writes its mark to each row it takes, though no column changes (section 3). It answers, per post, every image that statement returned for a post pair, with its size and alt text, the alt text null for an image whose own or whose post's `erased_at` is set, so no snapshot holds an erased image's alt text. An empty answer means the images are gone (G6 E3b). Lab, PostgreSQL 18.6 (review round 6, and section 3's lab of the keep trigger): the owner form's plan is LockRows over a Sort on `m.id`; against the purge's ordered delete, an intake that locked first made the purge wait and delete only the owner's upload on no post, and a purge that locked first made the intake wait and skip the deleted rows without an error.
  - `staffImage(mediaId, size)`: the checked open of 7.3, Images in the console (gate V; 6.1, the freeze).
  - `reach(target)`: for a post case's post or an account case's account, `{ posts, images, sealed, served, newestPostOn }`: the number of posts with image rows that a case about the target reaches (6.1, the freeze: the post itself, or every post whose `author_id` is the account, whatever its state), the number of their image rows, how many of those have the state `sealed`, how many are served (not sealed, their post not `deleted_by_author`, neither their own nor their post's `erased_at` set), and the creation day of the newest such post, or null. Beside them, for the urgent repeat alone, it gives `newestServedAt`, the creation time of the newest post with a served image, or null (7.12 compares it with a `seal_declined` text's time); the case answer does not carry it. One statement that locks nothing; it answers counts, a day and that time, never an id, an alt text or a file. Its callers are the case answer (7.3) and the dispatch job's urgent repeat (7.12).
  - `dropDisposable(tx)`: deletes, in the caller's transaction, at most `DISPOSAL_BATCH` disposable `post_media` rows (the predicate of 7.9, which leaves out every retained row; `post_media_keep` is what decides (6.1, the freeze)), the lowest ids first, selecting them `order by id limit DISPOSAL_BATCH for update` inside its delete (`delete from post_media where id in (select ... order by id limit $1 for update)`), so it and `hold` never lock one post's images in opposite orders, and answers their ids. `DISPOSAL_BATCH` is `RECONCILE_MAX_ORPHANS / 2`, 25 (`apps/api/src/media/sweeper.ts:24`). So if a crash loses one batch's deletion request, those files, together with other deletions' requests lost in the same crash, stay within what the reconcile pass deletes without `MEDIA_RECONCILE=force` (`sweeper.ts:76-83`). Lab, PostgreSQL 18.6: the plain insert failed with SQLSTATE 23503 when such a delete committed while it waited; the locking form skipped the deleted rows; a hold that locked first made the delete skip both rows; opposite lock orders deadlocked, and the ordered forms did not. Lab, PostgreSQL 18.6 (review round 5): the bounded form waited for a row that a concurrent transaction was making retained, skipped it on the re-check, and still answered a full batch of the next ids.
  - `overdueDeletions()`: the number of rows 7.9's disposal predicate names now (all of them, not one batch) none of whose holds ended, and none of whose disposal marks (`erased_at`, the post's `deleted_at` or `erased_at`) was set, in the last day, for the overview's `erasureOverdue` (7.3). A row whose freeze ended in the last day may be counted until the job's next round takes it.
  - `requestDeletion(ids)`: asks the worker to delete those files, called after the caller's commit.
  - `createMedia`'s result gains `forCases`.
- **`imageModerationStates(apiDb, afterId, limit)`**, a second export of `apps/api/src/media/index.ts` beside `mediaForCases`. It is built on the media repository over the pool it is given, with no files and no configuration. It answers up to `limit` rows of `post_media` that have a `post_id` and an `id` greater than `afterId` (from the start when `afterId` is null), in `id` order, each as `{ id, postId, moderationState, erasedAt }`. Its only caller is `staff.js text-verify` (7.13), which reads every image 1000 rows at a time. Neither the command line nor the moderation module may name `post_media` (`apps/api/test/architecture.test.ts:115-117`), and the command line builds no `MediaDeps`, since `staff.js` opens only database URLs (`apps/api/src/cli/staff.ts:20-24`).
- **`ModerationForSocial`** (`authorFacts(viewer, postIds)`): for each of the viewer's own posts whose `moderation_level` is `withheld` or `removed`, the decision for the chip: the decision of the strongest post measure in force (a `remove` before a `withhold`, the newest first, read from `measures_in_force`, 6.1), with its id, its `rule_id`, `legal` (its ground is `illegal`) and `interim` (its kind is `interim`); or, when the level is `removed` only because `posts.erased_at` is set and no post measure is in force, the decision whose erasure it was (the newest decision with `redacted_at` set that removed the post, 6.4). Only an interim decision or a final decision with outcome `restricted` holds a post measure, so the chip's decision always carries a ground (and a rule when the ground is `incompatible`), and its author was told of it. One query per page, only for posts the viewer wrote, as `listedInExplore` is asked (`apps/api/src/social/views.ts:40-41`).

The **staff module** mounts the console's case routes (`apps/api/src/staff/cases.ts`, a route group the existing `staffRoutes` mounts) and calls `ModerationForStaff`; `createStaff({ apiDb, auth, moderation })` gains the third member. Step-up stays the auth module's (`requireStaffStepUp`), and the three new step-up actions are entries of `STAFF_STEP_UP_ACTIONS`. `ModerationError` joins `app.ts`'s `onError` (`apps/api/src/app.ts:78-96`). The moderation module's app routes mount at `/api/notices` and `/api/moderation`, after the global rate limit and session resolution (`app.ts:65-66`), so a signed-out notice is a request without a session that `resolveSession` already lets through with an Origin check (`apps/api/src/auth/middleware.ts:20-24`). The moderation routes and both jobs run whether or not the console is on. With `CONSOLE_ORIGIN` unset (`apps/api/src/app.ts:73-74`), reports and notices are still taken, kept and given receipts, as Art. 16(1) and (6) require, and staff are still alerted, but nothing can decide them until the console is on.

Wiring in `apps/api/src/index.ts`: auth, media as today; then `createModeration({ apiDb, auth: auth.forModeration, social: socialForModeration(apiDb), media: media.forCases, requireSession: auth.requireSession, rateLimitStore, config, logger })` (`requireSession` is the auth module's middleware, passed in the options as every module with signed-in routes receives it, `apps/api/src/index.ts:45,60,74`; it is not a member of `AuthForModeration`); then `createSocial({ ..., moderation: moderation.forSocial })`; `createAccount({ ..., moderation: moderation.forAccount })`; `createStaff({ apiDb, auth: auth.forStaff, moderation: moderation.forStaff })`; `moderation.startJobs(jobLock)` beside the other jobs (it always starts the dispatch job, and starts the retention job only while `config.MODERATION_RETENTION` is `on`; while it is `off` it logs the warning `moderation-retention: off (MODERATION_RETENTION)` at every start); and `createApp({ ..., moderation })`.

The **database does the moderating** (section 2, row No moderation state without a decision; 6.2): the module inserts rows, migrator-owned triggers check them, a migrator-owned function derives every restriction from them, and deferred checks refuse at commit what cannot stand. The API decides which rows to write; the database decides whether they can stand and what they mean.

The **freeze** of the path for suspected child sexual abuse material (6.1) is enforced at four gates, each the only door of its kind. Gate K, in the database: the trigger `post_media_keep` keeps every image row of a frozen post against every delete, and `post_media_guard` keeps an image on its post (6.1). Gate E, in the database: `erase_snapshots`, the one function that erases a snapshot's content, keeps the snapshots of a frozen post (6.4). Gate V, in the media module: `MediaForCases.staffImage`, the one function that opens an image file for staff, refuses an image of a frozen post (7.3). Gate S, in the media module: the readers that give an image to anyone else (`fileAccess` behind `serve`, `forPosts`, `readyImages`) read the state that only `moderation_apply` writes and give a sealed image to nobody, and `find` behind `status` answers for a kept image of a deleted post, or an erased one, as for a row that does not exist (7.10).

The **console** gains a queue, a case page, a decision composer with a preview, an appeals list and a review composer (section 8), under 3a's policy unchanged: images come from `/api/staff/cases/:case/media/...` on the console's own origin, through the staff gate and `requireStaff`. The **app** gains the report form, the notice status page and the case screens (section 9).

## 6. Data model (migrations 0014 to 0018)

Five migrations, each run by drizzle's migrator in one transaction with the others pending (section 3): two for Task 1, then one per task that needs one. Task 1's two are both generated from the schema. drizzle-kit 0.31.10 asks "created or renamed?" whenever one diff both deletes and creates tables, enums or a table's columns (the new `cases` and `appeals` reuse the old tables' names), and its prompt refuses to run without a terminal ("Interactive prompts require a TTY terminal"), so the drops and the creates go in separate diffs, as 2b's 0005 and 0006 did (2b design, section 16): 0014 is generated with the old tables, their enums and `profiles.reputation` removed from the schema and nothing added, so its diff only deletes and it is not edited; 0015 is generated once the new schema is in place, so its diff only creates and adds, and it is completed by hand in the same file, as 0012 was. 0016 to 0018 are hand-written (`pnpm --filter @korbat/db generate:custom --name=...`) and change no table, so the generate gate prints "No schema changes" after each task. 0015 adds no value to an existing enum: every enum it names is created in it with every value, so its view and its SQL-language functions (6.1, the freeze) may name those values on a database already at 0013 as on a fresh one. The labs and the database tests (section 12) migrate both a fresh database and one already at 0013, committed (`scripts/test-db.mjs --fresh` and `--upgrade-from 13`, Task 1). Every function below is owned by `korbat_migrate`, has its `search_path` pinned (`public, pg_temp` or `auth, pg_temp`), names every object schema-qualified, and has EXECUTE revoked from PUBLIC; trigger functions fire for every role all the same, and the few that a role calls directly are granted to that role alone (the pattern of `0012_aberrant_wendell_vaughn.sql:154-294`). Times written by the database are `now()` (the transaction's start) unless said; every "forced" column is set by a `BEFORE INSERT` trigger whatever the insert supplied, so `korbat_api` can neither choose nor forge it (the pattern 3a's chain trigger set, and G5 P13 measured for a column Drizzle names on insert). Errors follow 3a's convention: the code as the message under SQLSTATE P0001, read by the module's raised-message helper, unless a SQLSTATE is named.

### 6.1 Migrations 0014 and 0015: the tables (Task 1)

0014 drops, with their indexes and policies, `transparency_log`, `strikes`, `appeals`, `reports` and `cases` (all empty; nothing reads them; drizzle-kit writes each table's `DROP POLICY` statements and then `DROP TABLE ... CASCADE`, so the order it chooses does not matter), then the column `profiles.reputation` and the enums `report_status`, `case_action`, `appeal_status` and `target_type`. Everything else in this section is 0015's.

New enums: `moderation_target` (`post`, `account`), `intake_kind` (`user`, `finding`), `intake_channel` (`signed_in`, `signed_out`, `staff`), `delivery_state` (`pending`, `sent`, `folded`, `suppressed`, `no_address`, `none`, `failed`), `item_outcome` (`restricted_rule`, `restricted_legal`, `no_violation`, `not_evident`, `not_available`, `partly_reversed`, `reversed`), `decision_kind` (`interim`, `final`, `review`, `correction`), `decision_outcome` (`restricted`, `no_violation`, `not_evident`, `kept`, `partly_reversed`, `reversed`), `decision_ground` (`incompatible`, `illegal`), `decision_source` (`notice`, `report`, `own_initiative`), `action_kind` (the measures `withhold`, `remove`, `seal_media`, `withhold_media`, `remove_media`, `reset_display_name`, `reset_bio`, and the reversals `restore`, `unseal_media`, `restore_media`, `restore_display_name`, `restore_bio`), `appeal_result` (`kept`, `partly_reversed`, `reversed`), `moderation_level` (`visible`, `withheld`, `removed`), `media_moderation_state` (`visible`, `withheld`, `removed`, `sealed`; only an image is ever `sealed`), `staff_text_kind` (`facts`, `reason`, `legal_reference`, `review_reason`, `reveal_reason`, `note`, `seal_declined`), `access_kind` (`case_opened`, `image_viewed`, `identity_revealed`). `failed` is final: korbat stopped trying, because the relay answered that the recipient's address does not exist (section 5, `MailRefused`) or the mail was still unsent five days after it was due (7.8 step 6). 3e adds values with `ALTER TYPE ... ADD VALUE` (among them `decision_source` `order` and its account measures), which PostgreSQL allows in a transaction when the value is not used there (1b design, section 3). Transparency Database categories and keywords are text under CHECK lists generated from one contracts constant, never enums, because the Commission renames and removes values and an enum cannot drop one (G9 2.5). A CHECK cannot drop a value that a row holds either: `ADD CONSTRAINT` checks every existing row, and a CHECK added `NOT VALID` is checked again on every later UPDATE of an old row, whatever column it sets (lab, PostgreSQL 18.6: once a list without a value was added `NOT VALID`, updating `author_mail` of a decision holding that value was refused). Since `intakes` and `decisions` are kept and updated, a later migration only adds values to these lists: a value the Commission removes stays in its constant and in the CHECKs, and only the lists offered for new input (`TDB_FORM_CATEGORIES` and the console's category choice) drop it.

The Drizzle schema of the moderation tables is a new file, `packages/db/src/schema/moderation.ts`; `safety.ts` keeps `audit_log` and `audit_head`; the auth tables join `auth.ts`. `media_holds` is defined in `moderation.ts` beside `cases`, which it references (it stays the media module's table in the API). `content.ts` never imports `moderation.ts`, so the schema files import in one direction (`moderation.ts` imports `content.ts` for `postState` and `postMedia`), and `index.ts` gains `export * from "./moderation"` after `./content`. A new custom column type `xid8` joins `helpers.ts` beside `bytea` (`packages/db/src/schema/helpers.ts:15-25`). Every public table below has RLS on and one policy, `<table>_api_all`, FOR ALL to `korbat_api` using and checking `true` (`fullAccess`), except `moderation_limits` (one SELECT policy); the grants do the restricting, and every append-only table has the default UPDATE and DELETE of `0001_grants.sql:16` revoked, as `0003` did for `auth_events`, and holds no TRUNCATE (3c's tables are created with the default privileges of 0001, which grant SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE and DELETE only; the grants test pins it, 6.5) and never MAINTAIN (G4 T15: with only INSERT and SELECT, `korbat_api` cannot lock the table).

- **`cases`**: `id uuid` primary key default `gen_random_uuid()`; `target_type moderation_target not null`; `target_id uuid not null` (no foreign key: it names two tables, and posts and profiles are never deleted in production, G3 4.1); `opened_at timestamptz not null` (forced `now()`); `lane smallint not null default 2` check 0 to 2; `intake_seq integer not null default 0`; `answered_seq integer not null default 0`; `state_version integer not null default 0`; `oldest_open_at timestamptz` (the oldest unanswered item); `last_decided_at timestamptz`; `closed_at timestamptz`. Checks `cases_seq_order` (`answered_seq <= intake_seq`) and `cases_closed_answered` (`closed_at is null or answered_seq = intake_seq`). Unique index `cases_one_open_per_target (target_type, target_id) where closed_at is null`; index `cases_queue_idx (lane, oldest_open_at) where closed_at is null` (G9 T15); index `cases_target_idx (target_type, target_id, opened_at)`. Grants: `korbat_api` SELECT and INSERT only, so the derived columns move only through the triggers of 6.2 (G3 L15). Trigger `cases_insert` (BEFORE INSERT) forces every column but `id`, `target_type` and `target_id` to its initial value.
- **`intakes`**: `id uuid` primary key; `kind intake_kind not null`; `ref text not null unique` check `ref ~ '^N-[A-Z2-7]{10}$'` (the public reference printed on screens and mails, made by the API); `lookup_hash text unique` (the sha256 hex of the notice's token, check `intakes_lookup`); `channel intake_channel not null`; `reporter_id uuid references profiles(id) on delete set null`; `finding_by uuid references profiles(id) on delete restrict`; `contact_id uuid` (the auth-side contact's id, no foreign key, as `profiles.id` has none to `auth.users`, `identity.ts:8`); `rule_id text` check in R1 to R6; `alleges_illegal boolean not null default false`; `csam boolean not null default false`; `category text not null` (check in the 16 values); `category_keyword text` (check in `KEYWORD_CHILD_SEXUAL_ABUSE_MATERIAL`, `KEYWORD_CHILD_SEXUAL_ABUSE_MATERIAL_DEEPFAKE`, `KEYWORD_GROOMING_SEXUAL_ENTICEMENT_MINORS`; null for the CSAM path's `abuse` choice, for which the Transparency Database has no keyword); `cited_law text` check at most 300 characters; `explanation text` check at most 2000; `bona_fide boolean not null default false`; `automated_processing boolean not null default false` (false in 3c; disclosed in the decision when true, Art. 16(6)); `received_at timestamptz not null` (forced); `receipt delivery_state not null default 'none'` and `receipt_sent_at timestamptz`; `erased_at timestamptz`. Checks (G9 4, measured): `intakes_has_ground` (`rule_id is not null or alleges_illegal or csam`); `intakes_illegal_elements` (`kind <> 'user' or not alleges_illegal or csam or (bona_fide and (explanation is not null or erased_at is not null))`); `intakes_finding_legal` (`kind <> 'finding' or not alleges_illegal`: a moderator's own finding never alleges illegality in 3c); `intakes_illegal_not_tc` (`not alleges_illegal or category <> 'STATEMENT_CATEGORY_OTHER_VIOLATION_TC'`); `intakes_csam` (`not csam or (category = 'STATEMENT_CATEGORY_PROTECTION_OF_MINORS' and reporter_id is null and contact_id is null)`); `intakes_contact_signed_out` (`contact_id is null or channel = 'signed_out'`); `intakes_reporter_signed_in` (`reporter_id is null or channel = 'signed_in'`); `intakes_finding` (`(kind = 'finding') = (finding_by is not null) and (kind = 'finding') = (channel = 'staff')`); `intakes_lookup` (`(lookup_hash is not null) = (channel = 'signed_out' or csam)`); `intakes_erased` (`erased_at is null or (explanation is null and cited_law is null)`); `intakes_r2_finding_only` (`rule_id is distinct from 'R2' or kind = 'finding'`). A finding holds no explanation, cited law or good-faith statement: what a moderator writes is a staff text (7.4). Indexes `intakes_reporter_idx (reporter_id, received_at)`, `intakes_finding_idx (finding_by, received_at)` (the findings limit and the home screen's count) and `intakes_unerased_idx (received_at) where erased_at is null` (the retention job's candidates, 7.9). Grants: SELECT, INSERT, and UPDATE of `receipt` and `receipt_sent_at` only; erasure and forgetting a reporter go through the functions of 6.4. Trigger `intakes_insert` (plain) forces `received_at`, `erased_at` null, `receipt_sent_at` null, and `receipt`: `pending` when a contact or (a signed-in reporter and `alleges_illegal`) can be mailed, else `none`.
- **`intake_items`**: `id uuid` primary key; `intake_id uuid not null references intakes(id) on delete restrict`; `position smallint not null` check 1 to 20 (1 to 10 the targets the sender named; 11 to 20 an item korbat added for a quoted post, which has no column of its own: its origin is the item of the same intake at its position minus 10, `intake_items_quote` below); `target_type moderation_target not null`; `target_id uuid not null` (for an item without a case: the uuid the link named when it names a post, and a fresh random uuid that names nothing when it names an account, 7.1 step 4; a re-examination's is copied from its item, below); `media_id uuid` (the image the reporter pointed at, a hint); `named_handle text` (for an account target, the handle as the notice or finding named it, in lower case; null for a post target, forced; the check `intake_items_named_handle` holds both halves; an account item's link is built from it and never from the handle the profile holds now, 7.2); `case_id uuid references cases(id) on delete restrict`; `seq integer` (forced); `lane smallint not null` check 0 to 2 (from the contracts' `laneFor`, section 10); `reporter_account_id uuid` and `alleges_illegal boolean not null default false` (both forced, copied from the intake: both for the index below, the second also for the decision triggers); `suspect boolean not null default false` (forced: its intake is on the CSAM path or cites R2; the freeze, below; the default is there so that no insert must name a forced column); `created_at timestamptz not null` (forced); `outcome item_outcome` (what the answering decision found, written once by it, 6.2, or `not_available` at insert for an item without a case); `answered_by uuid references decisions(id)`; `outcome_sent_at timestamptz` (the release, written only by `release_outcomes`); `shown_outcome item_outcome` (what the notifier or reporter is shown, written only by `release_outcomes`, 6.2); `revision_pending_at timestamptz` (set when a review or correction reverses a measure of the answering decision, cleared by the release); `revised_sent_at timestamptz`; `outcome_mail delivery_state not null default 'none'` (forced); `reexamines_item_id uuid references intake_items(id)` (set on a re-examination: the item it re-examines, 7.2); `statement text` (a re-examination's statement, at most 2000 characters) with `statement_salt text` and `statement_digest text` (made by the server with the contracts' `textDigest`); `erased_at timestamptz`. Checks: `intake_items_case` (`(case_id is null) = (seq is null) and (case_id is not null or outcome = 'not_available')`: an item whose link names nothing live has no case and is answered at once, "not available", by automated means, G3 5.3); `intake_items_shown` (`shown_outcome is null or outcome_sent_at is not null`); `intake_items_named_handle` (`(target_type = 'account') = (named_handle is not null)`: an account item always carries the handle it was named by, a post item never); and `intake_items_statement`, which allows a statement only on a re-examination and covers three states, no statement (all three null), a statement held to its digest, and erased (statement and salt null, digest kept, `erased_at` set): `(statement_digest is null or reexamines_item_id is not null) and (statement is null) = (statement_salt is null) and (statement is null or (erased_at is null and statement_salt ~ '^[0-9a-f]{64}$' and statement_digest = encode(sha256(convert_to('korbat.text.v1', 'UTF8') || decode('0a', 'hex') || decode(statement_salt, 'hex') || convert_to(statement, 'UTF8')), 'hex'))) and (statement is not null or statement_digest is null or erased_at is not null) and (statement_digest is null or statement_digest ~ '^[0-9a-f]{64}$')`. Unique `intake_items_target_once (intake_id, target_type, target_id) where reexamines_item_id is null` (a notice names a target once; the route de-duplicates first, G3 L12); unique `intake_items_reexamines_once (reexamines_item_id) where reexamines_item_id is not null` (an item is re-examined once); unique `(case_id, seq)`; unique `intake_items_reporter_once (case_id, reporter_account_id, alleges_illegal) where reexamines_item_id is null` (one item per signed-in reporter, case and ground: a rule report and a later notice by the same person that the item is illegal both stand; signed-out notifiers, whose column is null, are never merged, Art. 16(6)); index `(answered_by)`; index `intake_items_suspect_open_idx (case_id) where suspect and answered_by is null` (the freeze's probe); index `intake_items_revision_idx (revision_pending_at) where revision_pending_at is not null`. Grants: SELECT, INSERT, and UPDATE of `outcome_mail` only, so the API can move neither a release time nor what a notifier is shown. Trigger `intake_items_attach` (BEFORE INSERT, SECURITY DEFINER, G3 L8 extended), in this order:
  1. It forces `created_at`, `answered_by`, `outcome_sent_at`, `shown_outcome`, `revision_pending_at`, `revised_sent_at` and `erased_at` null, copies `reporter_account_id` and `alleges_illegal` from the intake, forces `suspect` to the intake's `csam or coalesce(rule_id = 'R2', false)` (a notice on the law alone has no rule, and `suspect` is then false, never null), forces `named_handle` null when `target_type` is `post`, and forces `outcome_mail`: `pending` when the intake has a `contact_id`, or a `reporter_id` and `alleges_illegal`, else `none`.
  2. For a re-examination (`reexamines_item_id` set), it first locks the intake `FOR KEY SHARE`, so it waits for `erase_intake` (6.4), which locks it `FOR UPDATE`, and is not attached to an erased intake. Then, in a statement of its own, it raises `reexamination_not_open` unless the intake's `erased_at` is null and the re-examined item belongs to the same intake, is not itself a re-examination (except one about a post that is not suspect and whose answering decision's `unseen` is true: an answer given while nobody could open the images does not spend the re-examination, section 10 `UNSEEN_NO_ACTION`), was answered by a decision (`answered_by` set) with outcome `no_violation` or `not_evident`, has `outcome_sent_at` set, and `clock_timestamp() < public.appeal_deadline(outcome_sent_at)`, read after the lock (never `now()`, which is the transaction's start). It then copies the re-examined item's `target_type`, `target_id`, `named_handle`, `position` and `lane` (the original's lane, P5). The unique index refuses a second re-examination of one item (`reexamination_exists`, mapped from the unique violation).
  3. With no case, it refuses anything but outcome `not_available`. With a case, it forces `outcome` null and runs `update public.cases set intake_seq = intake_seq + 1, lane = case when answered_seq = intake_seq then NEW.lane else least(lane, NEW.lane) end, oldest_open_at = coalesce(oldest_open_at, now()) where id = NEW.case_id and closed_at is null and target_type = NEW.target_type and target_id = NEW.target_id returning intake_seq` into `NEW.seq`, raising `case_closed` with SQLSTATE `40001` (serialization_failure) when no row matched, so the intake's transaction is retried into a new case.
  Constraint trigger **`intake_items_quote`** (AFTER INSERT ON `intake_items`, `DEFERRABLE INITIALLY DEFERRED`, SECURITY DEFINER; created after `intake_items_attach`) holds the rule that a suspect item about a post that quotes another is a report about both posts (7.1 step 3), so that no later intake path can forget the quoted post. At commit it raises `quote_item_missing` (SQLSTATE P0001; a database-only code like `moderation_column`) unless: (a) for an inserted suspect item whose target is a post, at a position up to 10, that is no re-examination and whose post's `quote_of_id` is set, its intake holds an item on that post that is no re-examination; (b) for an inserted item at a position above 10 that is no re-examination, its intake holds a suspect post item at its position minus 10, no re-examination, whose post's `quote_of_id` is the item's own target; (c) for an inserted re-examination of a suspect post item whose intake holds an added item on the post it quotes, that added item is not open to re-examination at commit: it is unanswered, or its outcome is neither `no_violation` nor `not_evident`, or its `outcome_sent_at` is null, or `clock_timestamp() >= public.appeal_deadline(outcome_sent_at)`, or a re-examination of it exists, this transaction's included. An item without a case (a link that names no row) passes. It reads the post by its key and the intake's items through `intake_items_target_once`. No retry repairs a missing item, so the code is not among the retried SQLSTATEs of 7.1 step 4: on the notice and finding routes it is a defect and answers 500.
- **`decisions`**: `id uuid` primary key (the API may supply it); `case_id uuid not null references cases(id) on delete restrict`; `kind decision_kind not null`; `reviews_decision_id uuid references decisions(id)` (reviews and corrections); `appeal_id uuid references appeals(id)` (a review's one appeal, forced, 6.3); `seen_state_version integer not null`; `through_seq integer not null default 0`; `answers_from_seq integer` (forced); `outcome decision_outcome not null`; `ground decision_ground`; `rule_id text` (check R1 to R6); `rules_version smallint not null`; `redress_version smallint not null`; `category text not null` (the 16 values); `category_keyword text` (null, or a value of the contracts' `DECISION_KEYWORDS`, in 3c only `KEYWORD_TERRORIST_CONTENT`, the Transparency Database's `category_specification` value "Terrorist content", read 2026-10-02); `source decision_source not null` (forced); `automated_detection boolean not null default false` and `automated_decision text not null default 'not_automated'` (check in `fully`, `partially`, `not_automated`; forced to false and `not_automated` in 3c); `territorial_scope text not null default 'worldwide'` (check `= 'worldwide'` in 3c); `snapshot_id uuid not null references evidence_snapshots(id) on delete restrict`; `snapshot_digest text not null`; `facts_digest`, `reason_digest`, `legal_reference_digest text` (each a lower-case hex sha256 or null); `decided_by uuid not null references profiles(id) on delete restrict` (the staff account); `decided_at timestamptz not null` (forced); `xact xid8 not null` (forced `pg_current_xact_id()`); `puid text not null unique` (forced: 32 random hex characters from `gen_random_uuid()`, the Transparency Database's platform id, never the internal id); `informed_at timestamptz not null` (forced equal to `decided_at`: the author's case screen shows the statement at commit); `suspect boolean not null` (forced, 6.2 step 6: the decision is taken on a case that holds, or answers, a suspect item); `unseen boolean not null default false` (forced, 6.2 step 6: true for a `final` decision on a post case taken while the post was frozen, so that nobody could open its images; `UNSEEN_NO_ACTION`, section 10); `needs_admin boolean not null` (forced, 6.2 step 6, and set by `decision_actions_check` for a decision that holds `unseal_media`); `ended_interim boolean not null` (forced, 6.2 step 10, and set by `decision_actions_check` for a reversal of an interim measure and by `decisions_apply` when its answer ends a seal taken while deciding that its author was told of, 6.2); `author_mail delivery_state not null default 'none'` (forced, 6.2 step 10) and `author_mail_at timestamptz`; `redacted_at timestamptz` (the R4 redaction, 6.4). Checks: `decisions_review_target` (`(kind in ('review','correction')) = (reviews_decision_id is not null)` and `(kind = 'review') = (appeal_id is not null)`); `decisions_interim` (`kind <> 'interim' or through_seq = 0`); `decisions_ground_rule` (`ground is distinct from 'incompatible' or rule_id is not null`); `decisions_ground_law` (`ground is distinct from 'illegal' or legal_reference_digest is not null`); `decisions_restricted` (`outcome <> 'restricted' or (ground is not null and facts_digest is not null and reason_digest is not null)`); `decisions_digests` (each digest null or 64 lower-case hex); `decisions_keyword` (`category_keyword is null or ground = 'illegal'`); `decisions_law_no_rule` (`ground is distinct from 'illegal' or rule_id is null`: a decision on the law cites no korbat rule); `decisions_reversal_plain` (`kind not in ('review', 'correction') or (ground is null and rule_id is null and category_keyword is null and facts_digest is null and legal_reference_digest is null)`: a review or correction states no ground of its own, and renders its reviewed decision's). Indexes `(case_id, decided_at)`, `(decided_by, decided_at)` (the caps), `(reviews_decision_id)`; unique `decisions_appeal_once (appeal_id) where appeal_id is not null`. Grants: SELECT, INSERT, and UPDATE of `author_mail` and `author_mail_at` only.
- **`decision_actions`**, the ledger: `id uuid` primary key; `decision_id uuid not null references decisions(id) on delete restrict`; `kind action_kind not null`; `media_id uuid` (image measures and their reversals); `subject_account_id uuid not null` (forced: the post's author or the account); `post_id uuid` (forced for post and image measures and their reversals; null, like `media_id`, for `seal_media` and `unseal_media`, whose posts `seal_posts` lists); `reverses_action_id uuid references decision_actions(id)` (forced for reversals: the one measure it lifts, 6.2); `no_effect text` (forced for measures: null when the measure changed what someone sees when it was imposed, `already` when another measure already kept the target from being shown or the field already held its placeholder, `deleted` when its author or the purge had deleted the target; check in those two; null for reversals); `created_at timestamptz not null` (forced). Unique `decision_actions_once (decision_id, kind, media_id) nulls not distinct`; unique `decision_actions_reversed_once (reverses_action_id) where reverses_action_id is not null` (a measure is lifted at most once); indexes `(post_id, kind)` and `(subject_account_id, kind)` (the in-force reads). Grants: SELECT, INSERT.
- **`seal_posts`**, part of the ledger: `action_id uuid not null references decision_actions(id) on delete restrict`; `post_id uuid not null` (no foreign key, as `cases.target_id` has none); `images smallint not null` check `images > 0`; primary key `(action_id, post_id)`; index `seal_posts_post_idx (post_id)` (the freeze's probe). One row for each post whose images a `seal_media` measure seals, with the number of image rows the post had when the measure was recorded. It is written only by `decision_actions_apply` (6.2), from the image rows it locked, so no caller chooses a seal's list, and it is append-only like `decision_actions`. Grants: `korbat_api` SELECT only. `moderation_apply`, the freeze's predicate, the statement, the case answer and `staff.js text-verify` read it.
- **`appeals`**: `id uuid` primary key; `decision_id uuid not null references decisions(id) on delete restrict`; `appellant_account_id uuid not null` (the decision's subject, checked by `appeals_check`, 6.3); `statement text` (at most 2000 characters); `statement_salt text` (64 lower-case hex) and `statement_digest text` (made by the server with the contracts' `textDigest`); `filed_at timestamptz not null` (forced); `answered_by uuid references decisions(id)`; `result appeal_result`; `answered_at timestamptz`; `erased_at timestamptz`. Check `appeals_statement`: `intake_items_statement`'s expression without its first clause (no statement; a statement held to its digest; erased, digest kept). Unique `appeals_once (decision_id)` (G3 L13: of two simultaneous appeals exactly one committed). Indexes `(decision_id) where answered_by is null` (the pending appeals) and `appeals_statement_idx (decision_id) where statement is not null` (the statements `erase_case_evidence` has still to erase: the retention job's candidates, 7.9). Grants: SELECT, INSERT.
- **`staff_texts`**: `id uuid` primary key; `case_id uuid not null references cases(id) on delete restrict`; `author_id uuid not null references profiles(id) on delete restrict`; `kind staff_text_kind not null`; `text text` (at most 5000 UTF-16 units, the Transparency Database's `decision_facts` bound, G1 5.2); `salt text` (64 lower-case hex); `digest text not null unique`; `created_at timestamptz not null` (forced); `decision_id uuid references decisions(id)` (forced null; set by the decision trigger when a confirmed decision names the digest); `confirmed_at timestamptz` (forced null; set to `now()` by `decisions_apply` together with `decision_id`, and by `append_confirmed_audit` for a reveal's reason; a text is confirmed when `confirmed_at` is set); `erased_at timestamptz`. Check `staff_texts_digest` (G1 L10): `(text is null) = (salt is null) and (text is null) = (erased_at is not null) and (text is null or digest = encode(sha256(convert_to('korbat.text.v1', 'UTF8') || decode('0a', 'hex') || decode(salt, 'hex') || convert_to(text, 'UTF8')), 'hex'))`. Index `staff_texts_open_idx (case_id, author_id) where confirmed_at is null and erased_at is null` (a moderator's drafts, 7.4, and the retention job's candidates, 7.9: a note and a `seal_declined` text are never confirmed, so an erased one must leave the index). Grants: SELECT, INSERT.
- **`evidence_snapshots`**: `id uuid` primary key; `case_id uuid not null references cases(id) on delete restrict`; `target_type moderation_target not null`; `target_id uuid not null`; `taken_at timestamptz not null` (forced to `date_trunc('milliseconds', now())`: the transaction's start to the millisecond, as JavaScript reads times and as the audit chain stores them, `0012_aberrant_wendell_vaughn.sql:118,165`); `author_handle text not null` (the post's author or the account, at capture); `post_state post_state`; `post_created_at timestamptz`; `reply_to_id uuid`; `quote_of_id uuid`; `body text`; `media jsonb` (an array of `{ id, alt, width, height }`, in position order); `display_name text`; `bio text`; `handle text`; `salt text`; `digest text not null`; `erased_at timestamptz`. A capture of a post whose `erased_at` is set holds an empty body and null alt texts, and a capture of any other post a null alt text for an image erased on its own (`capturePost` and `MediaForCases.hold`, section 5), so no capture copies erased content. Whether a snapshot's content may be shown is one predicate, `public.snapshot_withheld` (6.2), not a flag on the row. Check `evidence_erased` (`erased_at is null or (body is null and media is null and display_name is null and bio is null and salt is null)`). Indexes `(case_id, taken_at)`, `evidence_snapshots_target_idx (target_type, target_id)` (every snapshot of a post in any case: the redaction and gate E) and `evidence_snapshots_unerased_idx (case_id) where erased_at is null` (the retention job's candidates). Grants: SELECT, INSERT. `erased_at` is written only by `erase_snapshots` (6.4). The digest is computed by the API with the contracts' `snapshotDigest` (section 10) and checked by the console; SQL never recomputes it (the canonical text is sorted JSON, which jsonb's print is not, G1 L6). The API reads `select date_trunc('milliseconds', now())` in the snapshot's own transaction before it computes the digest; `now()` is fixed for the transaction, so this is the value the trigger forces. The API uses it as `takenAt` and compares it with the insert's `RETURNING taken_at`, rolling the transaction back when they differ.
- **`case_access_log`**: `id bigint` generated always as identity primary key; `staff_id uuid not null references profiles(id) on delete restrict`; `case_id uuid not null references cases(id) on delete restrict`; `kind access_kind not null`; `media_id uuid`; `intake_item_id uuid`; `text_id uuid` (a reveal's reason); `at timestamptz not null` (forced). Indexes `(staff_id, at)`, `(case_id, at)`, and `case_access_log_reveals_idx (at) where kind = 'identity_revealed'` (the home screen's list). Grants: SELECT, INSERT.
- **`moderation_limits`**: `id smallint` primary key check `id = 1`; `decisions_per_hour integer not null`; `decisions_per_day integer not null`; `case_opens_per_day integer not null`; `findings_per_day integer not null`; `reveals_per_day integer not null`; `updated_at timestamptz not null`. One row `(1, 60, 300, 400, 50, 10, now())` inserted by the migration. Grants: `korbat_api` SELECT only; one policy `moderation_limits_api_select`. The decision trigger falls back to 60 and 300, the case read to 400, the findings route to 50 and the reveal route to 10 when the row is missing, so the integration setup's truncate needs no new row.
- **`media_holds`** (the media module's; defined in `moderation.ts`): `id bigint` identity primary key; `media_id uuid not null references post_media(id) on delete cascade` (the history goes with a row the disposal deletes); `case_id uuid not null references cases(id) on delete restrict`; `held_at timestamptz not null` (forced); `until timestamptz` (null: until released); `released_at timestamptz` (forced null on insert). Every hold is a case's evidence hold; the images of a frozen post are kept without one (the freeze, below). Trigger `media_holds_insert` (plain) forces `held_at` and `released_at`. Indexes `(media_id) where released_at is null` and `(case_id) where released_at is null`. Grants: SELECT, INSERT; release goes through `public.release_case_holds` (6.4). Trigger `media_holds_apply` (AFTER INSERT OR UPDATE, SECURITY DEFINER) runs three statements: (1) it locks the image's `post_media` row (`PERFORM 1 FROM public.post_media WHERE id = NEW.media_id FOR NO KEY UPDATE`); (2) in a statement of its own, it reads the latest `coalesce(until, 'infinity')` of the image's unreleased holds, or null; under READ COMMITTED this statement takes its snapshot after the lock was granted, so it sees every hold committed meanwhile; (3) it writes that value to `post_media.hold_until`. One UPDATE whose subquery reads `media_holds` is not enough: a release that waits for a new hold's row lock re-checks only the `post_media` row and keeps its subquery's old snapshot (lab, PostgreSQL 18.6: case c1's hold was released while case c2's new hold was uncommitted, which left `hold_until` NULL with c2's hold unreleased; the lock-first form left `infinity`). `MediaForCases.hold` inserts its rows in `media_id` order, and `release_case_holds` releases one row at a time in `media_id` order, so two transactions lock one post's images in the same order.
- **`post_media`** gains `moderation_state media_moderation_state not null default 'visible'` (written only by `moderation_apply`), `hold_until timestamptz` (written only by `media_holds_apply`) and `erased_at timestamptz` (an R4 `remove_media` made final, written only by `redact_r4_body`). `korbat_api` loses table-level UPDATE and gets UPDATE of every other column back (`status`, `error`, `post_id`, `position`, `width`, `height`, `blurhash`, `alt_text`, `nsfw_score`; the 0012 pattern, G5 P1 to P6); trigger `post_media_insert` forces the three to their defaults (G5 P13). A row is **retained** while `hold_until > now()` or its post is frozen. The triggers that enforce it, `post_media_keep` (BEFORE DELETE) and `post_media_guard` (BEFORE UPDATE), are defined with the freeze, below (gate K). There is no retention by state and no release mark in 3c.
- **`posts`** gains `moderation_level moderation_level not null default 'visible'` and `erased_at timestamptz` (the R4 redaction erased its content, 6.4; one-way), both written only by migrator-owned functions; `posts.state` mirrors the level unless the author deleted the post, and `post_state` gains no value. `korbat_api` loses table-level UPDATE on `posts` and gets UPDATE of `body`, `state`, `deleted_at`, `like_count`, `repost_count`, `reply_count` and `quote_count` back, the only columns the API writes (section 3). `korbat_api` also loses DELETE on `posts` and on `profiles` (the freeze, below). Migration 0016 (Task 2, 6.2), not 0015, creates the function `public.posts_guard()` (plain, not SECURITY DEFINER), behind the triggers `posts_guard_insert` BEFORE INSERT ON `posts` FOR EACH ROW and `posts_guard_update` BEFORE UPDATE OF `state`, `moderation_level`, `erased_at` ON `posts` FOR EACH ROW `WHEN (OLD.state IS DISTINCT FROM NEW.state OR OLD.moderation_level IS DISTINCT FROM NEW.moderation_level OR OLD.erased_at IS DISTINCT FROM NEW.erased_at)`, so the counter updates of likes, reposts, replies and quotes never fire it. For every role, the migrator included, an update that moves `state` from `deleted_by_author` to anything else is refused: the author's deletion is terminal. Unless `current_user` is `korbat_migrate`, an insert must have `state` and `moderation_level` `visible` and `erased_at` null, and an update may change `state` only to `deleted_by_author` and may not change `moderation_level` or `erased_at`. A refusal raises `moderation_column` with SQLSTATE `42501`, the pattern r4 measured for a plain trigger's `current_user` check (section 3): `korbat_api` can neither become the migrator nor disable the trigger, and inside the migrator-owned definer functions `current_user` is the migrator. It is not a `ModerationErrorCode`: no API path writes these columns. Which definer functions write them is pinned by the function test (6.5): `moderation_apply` and `redact_r4_body`, no other. The guard comes with 0016 because the 18 direct `posts.state` writes of the integration tests, made as `korbat_api`, move to `decideForTest` only in Task 2 (section 12). With the guard in 0015 they would fail Task 1's gate with SQLSTATE 42501 (lab, PostgreSQL 18.6). The column grants above stay in 0015; they leave `state` writable by `korbat_api`.
- **`handle_history`** is unchanged in 3c (3e's handle retirement adds its column), so the 1b schema test's exact column list (`schema.test.ts:136`) stays.
- **`auth.notifier_contacts`** (each `_enc` column AES-256-GCM through the existing `encrypt` helper, `apps/api/src/auth/crypto.ts:52`, under `NOTIFIER_ENC_KEY`; a contact is never re-encrypted, since `korbat_auth` may update only its `forget_after`): `id uuid` primary key (the moderation module chose it); `name_enc text`; `email_enc text`; `email_hash text` (`emailHash`, `apps/api/src/auth/identifiers.ts:16-18`); `mail_token_enc text` (the contact's mail token: 32 random bytes, base64url, made by `storeNotifierContact` when an address is given and encrypted under `NOTIFIER_ENC_KEY` like the name and the address; null without an address); `created_at timestamptz not null default now()`; `forget_after timestamptz not null` (13 months after creation; the daily dispatch moves it to six months after each day on which the notice is still owed something, 7.8 step 3(b), and a re-examination moves it to six months after its filing, 7.2). `korbat_auth`: SELECT, INSERT, DELETE, UPDATE of `forget_after` only.
- **`auth.notifier_confirmations`**: `email_hash text` primary key; `confirmed_at`, `expires_at timestamptz not null` (30 days). `korbat_auth`: every default privilege.
- **`auth.notifier_suppressions`**: `email_hash text` primary key; `created_at timestamptz not null default now()`. `korbat_auth`: SELECT, INSERT, DELETE.
- **`auth.staff_personal_accounts`**: `staff_user_id uuid not null references auth.users(id) on delete restrict`; `personal_user_id uuid not null references auth.users(id) on delete restrict`; `linked_at timestamptz not null default now()`; primary key `(staff_user_id, personal_user_id)`; check that the two differ. `korbat_auth` holds nothing on it (REVOKE ALL); its one policy, `staff_personal_accounts_auth_none`, FOR SELECT to `korbat_auth` using `false`, satisfies the schema test's rule that every table has one. Only `staff.js link` and `unlink` write it, as the migrator, and only the definer functions of 6.2 and `auth.staff_link_counts` (6.4) read it.
- Each auth table has RLS on and one FOR ALL policy for `korbat_auth` (3a section 15, "One policy per staff table") except the last.

**The freeze.** The path for suspected child sexual abuse material is stated once: here, for the words, the predicate and the gate that 0015 creates, and in 6.2 for the seal. Every other section points to these two places (P9). Its words:

- A **suspect item** is an intake item whose intake is on the CSAM path (`intakes.csam`) or is a moderator's finding citing R2; `intake_items.suspect` stores it. It is **unanswered** while its `answered_by` is null and it has a case, and only an admin's final decision answers it (6.2 step 6).
- **Reach**: a post case reaches its post; an account case reaches every post whose `author_id` is the account, whatever the post's state, deleted and purged ones included. Nothing else is reached: not a post that a reached post quotes (it gets an item of its own, 7.1 step 3), not a post the account reposted. An image is reached through its post; an upload on no post is never reached.
- A post is **frozen** while an open case about it, or about its author's account, holds an unanswered suspect item, or a seal in force lists it (`seal_posts`). An image is frozen while its post is. Nothing is written to a post or an image when a freeze begins or ends: it is computed when read.
- **Sealed** is a state of an image (`post_media.moderation_state`), never of a post or of text. A sealed image is served, listed with content and exported to nobody, its author included (6.2).
- An image is **served** while korbat's server would give it to some reader: it is not sealed, its post is not `deleted_by_author`, and neither its own nor its post's `erased_at` is set.
- To **open** is to load an image. Text (a body, an alt text, a display name, a bio, a report's words) is not opened material: staff read it on every path. Nobody at korbat opens an image of a frozen post.

The freeze is three prohibitions and the seal a fourth, each enforced at one gate that every path of its kind must pass, so that no path has a rule of its own and a later one cannot forget it: no image row of a frozen post is deleted, and no image leaves its post (gate K, below); no snapshot of a frozen post is erased (gate E: `erase_snapshots`, 6.4); no staff member is served an image of a frozen post (gate V: `MediaForCases.staffImage`, 7.3); a sealed image reaches nobody (gate S: the image readers, 7.10). While a post is frozen its images are judged in no procedure. An item on another ground, a correction and the review of an appeal that turn on an image are decided on text alone or wait until an admin has answered the suspect items. A final decision on a post case taken meanwhile is stored as `unseen` (6.2 step 6), so that a report it answers without action says so and can be asked again (section 10, `UNSEEN_NO_ACTION`). Staff never open a post or an account in the app while a suspect item about it is unanswered or its images are sealed: no gate holds the app, and its pages show more than a case reaches (section 2, the path's row). After an admin's answer without a seal they may, and must the same day: a post's images in the console, on its case; an account's in the app, which is then the only place they are shown, since an account case shows no image (7.3).

- **`public.measures_in_force`**, a view with `security_invoker = true`: every `decision_actions` row of a measure kind, with the action's `id` under that name, its `kind`, `post_id`, `media_id` and `subject_account_id`, and its decision's id, kind and case, for which no row names it in `reverses_action_id` and, when its decision's kind is `interim`: for a `seal_media`, whose case holds an unanswered suspect item whose `created_at` is not after the seal decision's `decided_at`; for any other kind, whose case's `closed_at` is null. It is the one definition of "in force", read by `moderation_apply`, the freeze's predicate, the triggers of 6.2, `appeals_check`, the due rules of 6.4, `item_current_outcome` and the author's chip. It names no value of `post_state`, and every enum value it names is created by this migration. A seal taken while deciding is a measure of the suspicion, not of the case: it ends by itself when an admin answers the suspect items, also while an item of another kind keeps the case open; and since a final decision that answers a suspect item answers every unanswered suspect item of its case (6.2 step 6), it never ends while one is unanswered. The comparison of the two times, each the start of its transaction, has one purpose: a suspect item that arrives after a seal ended never brings that seal back (no report hides anything by itself); the post is frozen again through the new item, and a person seals again. An item that committed while the seal's decision waited for its first locks may fall on either side; either way it freezes, and 7.12's alert uses the same comparison.
- **`public.frozen_for(post uuid, author uuid) returns boolean`** is the one definition of the freeze. It reads no `posts` row, because inside a cascade from a deleted post that row is already gone for the trigger's statement (section 3). Each of its three arms is driven by an index (`cases_one_open_per_target`, then `intake_items_suspect_open_idx` only when a case was found; `seal_posts_post_idx`):

```sql
create function public.frozen_for(post uuid, author uuid) returns boolean
  language sql stable set search_path = public, pg_temp as $$
  select exists (
      select 1 from public.cases c
      join public.intake_items i on i.case_id = c.id and i.suspect and i.answered_by is null
      where c.closed_at is null and c.target_type = 'post' and c.target_id = frozen_for.post)
    or exists (
      select 1 from public.cases c
      join public.intake_items i on i.case_id = c.id and i.suspect and i.answered_by is null
      where c.closed_at is null and c.target_type = 'account' and c.target_id = frozen_for.author)
    or exists (
      select 1 from public.seal_posts s
      join public.measures_in_force m on m.id = s.action_id
      where s.post_id = frozen_for.post)
$$;
```

- **`public.post_frozen(post uuid) returns boolean`** is that definition for the callers that have a post and not its author: `select public.frozen_for(post_frozen.post, (select p.author_id from public.posts p where p.id = post_frozen.post))`. Both functions are plain, `language sql`, STABLE, with `search_path = public, pg_temp`, and have EXECUTE granted to `korbat_api` only. Who names them is pinned (6.5): in the database `frozen_for` is named only by `post_frozen` and `post_media_keep`, and `post_frozen` only by `decisions_record` (6.2 step 6, for `decisions.unseen`) and by `erase_snapshots` and `redact_r4_body` (6.4); in the API `post_frozen` is named only in `apps/api/src/media/repo.ts` and `apps/api/src/moderation/repo.ts`, and `frozen_for` nowhere. A post nobody reported costs three index probes (section 3). The predicate is read at a delete of an attached image row, at an erasure, at a staff image request, at a case read, at a final decision on a post case and when the retention job chooses candidates; never by `serve` or a timeline.
- **Gate K: `post_media_keep`**, BEFORE DELETE ON `post_media` FOR EACH ROW.
