Its function is PL/pgSQL, volatile and SECURITY DEFINER, so the read below depends on no grant or row policy of the deleting role (section 3). It tests the row's own columns first, so the sweeper's deletes of uploads on no post never read the predicate: a row whose `post_id` is null is retained only while `hold_until > now()`. For a row whose `post_id` is set it raises `read_committed_only` (SQLSTATE `25000`; a database-only code) unless `current_setting('transaction_isolation')` is `read committed`, since under any other level its read would see the transaction's old snapshot (section 3); then the row is retained when `hold_until > now()`; only otherwise it reads `select public.frozen_for(OLD.post_id, OLD.owner_id) into retained`, a statement of its own. For a retained row it answers NULL on a direct delete (the row is skipped and `RETURNING` omits it, so no deletion is requested for its files) and raises SQLSTATE `23001` (restrict_violation) when `pg_trigger_depth() > 1`, a cascade (G6 E5, E5b). The row is locked before a `BEFORE DELETE` row trigger fires, and a statement inside the trigger function takes a fresh snapshot under READ COMMITTED, so a delete that waited for an intake's `FOR KEY SHARE` reads the suspect item that intake committed, for the ordered delete and for a plain `delete ... where post_id = $1` alike (measured, section 3). A filter inside the deleting statement would not, so no deleter relies on a filter of its own: `detachAll` and `purgeOwner` take their rows in id order with no filter, and the filter in `dropDisposable`'s candidates only keeps kept rows out of a batch (7.9). A TRUNCATE fires no row trigger; `korbat_api` holds none on `post_media`, `posts` or `profiles`.
- **`post_media_guard`**, BEFORE UPDATE OF `post_id`, `owner_id`, `alt_text`, `position`, `width`, `height`, `blurhash` ON `post_media` FOR EACH ROW; its function is plain, like `posts_guard`. It raises `moderation_column` (SQLSTATE `42501`) for every role, the migrator included, when `OLD.post_id` is set and `NEW.post_id` differs (an image never leaves its post, so nothing detaches it into the sweeper's reach, G6 M4), when `OLD.post_id` is null and `NEW.post_id` names a post whose `author_id` is not `NEW.owner_id`, and when `owner_id` changes; and, unless `current_user` is `korbat_migrate`, when `OLD.post_id` is set and `alt_text`, `position`, `width`, `height` or `blurhash` changes, since on an account case the alt texts on the kept rows are the only copy of them. `attach` sets `post_id`, `position` and `alt_text` in one update of a row whose `post_id` is null, for the post's author (`apps/api/src/media/repo.ts:87-96`), and nothing else in the code updates these columns of an attached row; the definer functions run as the migrator, so an erasure may still blank what it erases. So the owner of an attached row is its post's author by construction, which is what lets `post_media_keep` pass `OLD.owner_id` as the author.
- `korbat_api` holds no DELETE on `posts` or on `profiles` (revoked in this migration; no code deletes a row of either, and `scripts/clean-dev-fixtures.mjs` runs as the superuser), so "posts and profiles are never deleted in production" is a grant, and a cascade is only ever the migrator's or the superuser's.
- Files follow rows. Every deletion request the API writes names an id that a delete of `post_media` returned, an upload that failed before it could be put on a post, or a file that has no row (the reconcile pass, which sees every row); so a kept row keeps its files, but for a request written by a purge whose commit then fails (7.10).
- Flagged (section 2, row Lock order): the trigger reads the predicate once for each row, so the purge of an account with several thousand image rows takes seconds (section 3).

The hand-written part of 0015, in this order:

1. The public tables' grants, each taking back what 0001's default privileges gave `korbat_api` (`0001_grants.sql:16`: SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE and DELETE; they give no TRUNCATE, which each revoke below names all the same, as 0001 and 0003 do, and whose absence the grants test pins, 6.5):
   - `REVOKE UPDATE, DELETE, TRUNCATE ... FROM korbat_api` on `cases`, `intakes`, `intake_items`, `decisions`, `decision_actions`, `appeals`, `staff_texts`, `evidence_snapshots`, `case_access_log` and `media_holds`; `REVOKE INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE, TRUNCATE ON seal_posts FROM korbat_api`;
   - `REVOKE INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE, TRUNCATE ON moderation_limits FROM korbat_api`;
   - `GRANT UPDATE (receipt, receipt_sent_at) ON intakes`, `GRANT UPDATE (outcome_mail) ON intake_items` and `GRANT UPDATE (author_mail, author_mail_at) ON decisions`, each `TO korbat_api`;
   - `REVOKE UPDATE ON post_media FROM korbat_api` and `GRANT UPDATE (status, error, post_id, position, width, height, blurhash, alt_text, nsfw_score) ON post_media TO korbat_api`;
   - `REVOKE UPDATE ON posts FROM korbat_api` and `GRANT UPDATE (body, state, deleted_at, like_count, repost_count, reply_count, quote_count) ON posts TO korbat_api`; `REVOKE DELETE ON posts, profiles FROM korbat_api`.
2. The forced-field triggers (`cases_insert`, `intakes_insert`, `staff_texts_insert`, `evidence_snapshots_insert`, `case_access_log_insert`, `post_media_insert`, `media_holds_insert`), each plain: they write only the row being inserted.
3. **`public.appeal_deadline(informed timestamptz) returns timestamptz`**, IMMUTABLE: `((informed at time zone 'UTC') + interval '6 months') at time zone 'UTC'`. PostgreSQL clamps month arithmetic to the month's last day (31 August plus six months is 28 or 29 February), and the UTC zone keeps the result independent of the session's (G8 8). It bounds the author's appeal and the re-examination window alike. The contracts' `appealDeadline` computes the same in UTC; a test holds them equal over every month end. EXECUTE is revoked from PUBLIC and granted to `korbat_api`. Then, in this order, each trigger function with its trigger: `intake_items_attach`; `intake_items_quote`; the view `measures_in_force`; `frozen_for`; `post_frozen`; `post_media_keep`; `post_media_guard`; `media_holds_apply`. PostgreSQL checks the body of a SQL-language function when it is created, so the view comes before the two functions and they before the trigger that calls them. `GRANT SELECT ON public.measures_in_force TO korbat_api` follows the view.
4. The auth tables' grants: `REVOKE UPDATE ON auth.notifier_contacts FROM korbat_auth` and `GRANT UPDATE (forget_after) ON auth.notifier_contacts TO korbat_auth`; `REVOKE UPDATE ON auth.notifier_suppressions FROM korbat_auth`; `REVOKE ALL ON auth.staff_personal_accounts FROM korbat_auth`.
5. The `moderation_limits` row.

### 6.2 Migration 0016: decisions and the ledger (Task 2)

**The model** (P1): measures and reversals are ledger rows (6.1 `decision_actions`); which are in force is `measures_in_force` (6.1); every post's level and image's state is derived from them by `moderation_apply`, from the ledger with `seal_posts`, case closures, the answers to suspect items and the one-way erasure only, so it is a pure function of rows the chain covers that an auditor can recompute, whatever the order in which cases, measures and reversals arrive.

**The seal** (6.1 defines the freeze it rests on). `seal_media` is the one measure that hides images reported as child sexual abuse material, and it is a person's act: a report hides nothing by itself. It seals every image the case reaches when it is recorded, for everyone, their author included; the posts it covers are listed in `seal_posts` by the database (`decision_actions_apply`, below), so no caller chooses them, and an image posted later is not covered until a person seals again. Anyone eligible takes it while deciding, on a post case or an account case that holds an unanswered suspect item; only an admin takes it for good, since only an admin answers a suspect item (`decisions_record` step 6); and only an admin lifts it, with `unseal_media`, in a correction or a review of the decision that holds it (`decision_actions_check` steps 3, 6 and 9). A seal for good is lifted only on the answer of the authority the images were reported to, in a correction or in the review of the author's appeal. Taken while deciding it ends by itself with the admin's answer to the case's suspect items (6.1 `measures_in_force`), and `decisions_apply` is the one place that decides that it has ended; before that answer an admin lifts it, by a correction, only when it was taken in error (the wrong case, or a report that the runbook's standard gives no reason to believe; runbook, Task 12(a)). Every lifting is mailed to every staff account (`moderation_csam_lifted`, kind `unsealed`; 7.5 step 4). It never changes a post's level and never hides text: a post's text is restricted by the ordinary measures, by anyone who may take them. Nothing in 3c releases, hands over or erases a sealed image (section 17).

- **`public.account_is_staff(account uuid) returns boolean`**, SECURITY DEFINER, `search_path = auth, pg_temp`: `select u.role <> 'user' from auth.users u where u.id = account for share`, false when there is no row (G5 6: `FOR SHARE` makes it consistent against a concurrent promotion; `FOR KEY SHARE` let a suspended moderator through). Called only by the triggers below; no grant.
- **`public.staff_party_conflict(staff uuid, parties uuid[], target_post uuid) returns boolean`**, SECURITY DEFINER, `search_path = public, pg_temp` (it reads `auth.staff_personal_accounts` by its qualified name). It answers true when `staff` is one of `parties`, or a personal account linked to `staff` is one of them, or such an account follows, blocks or mutes a party (`follows.follower_id`, `blocks.blocker_id`, `mutes.muter_id`) or replied to a post of a party in the last 90 days (`posts.reply_to_id`, `created_at`), or `target_post` (the case's post; null for an account case) replies to or quotes a post written by such an account (`reply_to_id`, `quote_of_id`). Only what the linked account did, and the post the case judges, count: a party's own follow, block, mute or reply towards a linked account never does, since anyone can make one without the staff member doing anything, and one click would otherwise keep a reported threat public (step 5 counts the reporters of an interim decision) or put an account beyond every staff member whose posting account it follows (WP:INVOLVED, practice-3c 2.7; T48's control names the linked accounts' relations). Called only by the triggers below and by `staff_case_conflict`; no grant.
- **`public.staff_case_conflict(staff uuid, case_id uuid, everyone boolean) returns boolean`**, SECURITY DEFINER, `search_path = public, pg_temp`, EXECUTE to `korbat_api` only. False when the case does not exist; otherwise `staff_party_conflict(staff, parties, target_post)`, `target_post` being the case's `target_id` for a post case and null for an account case, where `parties` is the case's subject account (a post case's `posts.author_id`, an account case's `target_id`) and, when `everyone` is true, also every non-null `reporter_account_id` of the case's items. It takes a case, not a list of accounts, so no route passes account ids; it does not keep the link from an API under an attacker's control, which can name any account in a case or an item it inserts (section 11). The case routes and the reveal call it with `everyone` false; the appeals list's mark calls it with `everyone` true, a superset of a review's parties, so the console never offers a review that the trigger refuses.
- **`public.measures_in_force`, `public.frozen_for` and `public.post_frozen`** are created in 0015 and defined in 6.1 (the freeze). The triggers below read the view; one reads `post_frozen`, `decisions_record` in its step 6, to force `decisions.unseen`; a decision's own suspicion is the stored `decisions.suspect`.
- **`public.snapshot_withheld(snapshot uuid) returns boolean`**, plain, STABLE, EXECUTE to `korbat_api`: true when the snapshot is erased, or when it holds content (a non-empty body, or any image with an alt text) and its target post's `erased_at` is set. It is the one predicate by which a snapshot's content is withheld wherever it would be shown: the case answer, the author's decision view, `access-request` and the export (7.3, 7.6, 7.11, 7.13). A snapshot taken after such an erasure holds no content (6.1), so it is shown as it is and the console can digest it (T52).
- **`public.moderation_apply(posts uuid[], levels boolean)`**, SECURITY DEFINER, `search_path = public, pg_temp`, PL/pgSQL, EXECUTE to nobody (its owner calls it from the triggers below and from `redact_r4_body`, 6.4). It is the only writer of `posts.moderation_level`, of `posts.state` other than the author's deletion, and of `post_media.moderation_state`. With `levels` true it derives the posts' levels and their images' states; with `levels` false only the images' states, under the image rows' locks alone, which is the form a seal uses: a seal changes no post level, `attach` puts images on a post only in the transaction that creates it, and `post_id` cannot change (6.1 `post_media_guard`), so the image rows are where the derivation of image states serialises. In this order:
  1. When `levels` is true: `PERFORM 1 FROM public.posts WHERE id = ANY(posts) ORDER BY id FOR UPDATE` (nothing for an id with no row).
  2. When `levels` is true, in a statement of its own, so it sees every ledger row and case closure committed while the lock waited (section 3), each post's level: `removed` when a `remove` of the post is in force or the post's `erased_at` is set; else `withheld` when a `withhold` is in force; else `visible`.
  3. When `levels` is true: `UPDATE public.posts SET moderation_level = level` where it differs, then `UPDATE public.posts SET state = level::text::public.post_state` where `state <> 'deleted_by_author'` and it differs: the author's deletion stays terminal, and moderation never moves `state` to or from `deleted_by_author`.
  4. `PERFORM 1 FROM public.post_media WHERE post_id = ANY(posts) ORDER BY id FOR NO KEY UPDATE`.
  5. In a statement of its own, for each of those rows, `moderation_state` = `sealed` when a `seal_media` in force lists the row's post in `seal_posts`; else `removed` when a `remove_media` of the image is in force or its `erased_at` is set; else `withheld` when a `withhold_media` of it is in force; else `visible`; written only where it differs.
  It is called with `levels` true and one post for every post or image measure or reversal (`decision_actions_apply`), when a final decision closes a post case (`decisions_apply`, so its interim measures end), and after the R4 erasure (6.4); and with `levels` false, over a seal's listed posts, for `seal_media` and `unseal_media` (`decision_actions_apply`) and when a decision's answer ends a seal taken while deciding (`decisions_apply`). Under READ COMMITTED each statement after a lock takes a fresh snapshot, so the result is the maximum over every committed measure whatever the order of arrival; decisions about one subject already serialise at its profile lock, and the redaction and the intake take the case or the post before the images (section 2, Lock order; measured in Task 2's lab, section 12).
- **`public.posts_guard()`** with its triggers `posts_guard_insert` and `posts_guard_update`, exactly as 6.1's `posts` bullet defines them.
- **`public.decisions_record()`**, BEFORE INSERT ON `decisions`, SECURITY DEFINER. Forces `decided_at`, `xact`, `puid`, `informed_at`, `answers_from_seq`, `source`, `automated_detection`, `automated_decision`, `territorial_scope`, `suspect`, `unseen`, `needs_admin`, `ended_interim`, `author_mail` and `author_mail_at` (null). Then, in this order:
  1. Locks the case `FOR UPDATE` and raises `case_changed` when its `state_version` is not `seen_state_version`.
  2. The kind and its outcomes: an `interim` decision `restricted`, on an open case; a `final` one `restricted`, `no_violation` or `not_evident`, on an open case, with `answered_seq < through_seq <= intake_seq` (else `case_changed`), setting `answers_from_seq = answered_seq + 1`; a `correction` `partly_reversed` or `reversed`, naming in `reviews_decision_id` an `interim` or `final` decision of this case with outcome `restricted` and carrying that decision's `category` and `rules_version` (its `redress_version` is its own) and a `reason_digest`; a `review` is refused until 0017 replaces this function. Any other combination is `invalid_decision`.
  3. The snapshot: it belongs to this case (`snapshot_mismatch` otherwise), is not erased (`snapshot_erased`), and its digest equals `snapshot_digest` (`snapshot_mismatch`).
  4. The texts: each non-null digest names an unconfirmed, unerased `staff_texts` row of this case, written by `decided_by`, of the matching kind (`facts_digest` a `facts` text, `reason_digest` a `reason` text, or a `review_reason` text for a review or a correction, `legal_reference_digest` a `legal_reference` text); otherwise `text_not_found`.
  5. The parties: the case's subject account (a post's author, or the account) and the `reporter_account_id` of every item the decision considers (for `final`, seq in `answers_from_seq` to `through_seq`; for `interim`, every unanswered item; for a correction, the items its corrected decision answered, or for an interim one every unanswered item); `staff_party_conflict(decided_by, parties, the case's post or null)` raises `staff_recused`.
  6. The role and the suspicion. `decided_by`'s `auth.users` row is read `FOR SHARE` (the row and mode `append_confirmed_audit` takes again at the end, G5 6), so a demotion either waits for the decision's commit or is read by it; then `account_is_staff(subject)` takes the subject's row `FOR SHARE`, so the lock order of section 2 holds whichever trigger later locks the post. `suspect` is forced, read now, before `decisions_apply` answers any item: for an `interim` decision, true when the case holds an unanswered suspect item (6.1); for a `final` one, true when an item it answers is suspect; a `correction` or a `review` copies its reviewed decision's. `unseen` is forced in the same step, before `decisions_apply` answers anything: true for a `final` decision on a post case when `public.post_frozen(post)` is true at that moment (through a suspect item of its own case, through its author's account's case, or through a seal in force), read in a statement of its own after step 1's lock; false for every other decision. It is the stored fact that the decision was taken while nobody could open the post's images: `UNSEEN_NO_ACTION`, `NoticeStatus.unseen`, the mail's line and the re-examination exception follow from it alone, never from `suspect` (6.1 `intake_items_attach`; 7.2; section 10). A `final` decision whose `suspect` is true must answer every unanswered suspect item of its case: when one has a `seq` above `through_seq`, read after step 1's lock, it raises `case_changed`, so a suspect item that arrived while the admin read the case is never left behind, and a seal taken while deciding never ends while one is unanswered. A stream of suspect items into one case can keep an admin from answering it; that fails closed, and an admin can still lift a wrong seal taken while deciding by a correction. The step asks nothing about an item above `through_seq` that is not suspect: such an item stays open (7.5 step 1). A decision whose `rule_id` is R2 needs `suspect` true (`invalid_decision`: an R2 finding is opened first, which freezes). `needs_admin` is forced true for: a `final` decision whose `suspect` is true, that considers an item whose intake cites R5, or whose own `rule_id` is R2 or R5, whatever its outcome; a `correction` whose corrected decision's `needs_admin` is true; and (0017) a `review` for which `review_needs_admin(reviews_decision_id)` is true. An `interim` decision is forced false, so whoever is on duty can seal reported images or withhold a reported threat at once. When `needs_admin` is true and the locked row's role is not `admin`, it raises `admin_required`. The one measure that needs an admin whatever the decision's kind, `unseal_media`, is checked by `decision_actions_check`, which sets `needs_admin` too.
  7. The caps: first `pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtextextended('korbat.decision_cap:' || NEW.decided_by::text, 0))`, a one-bigint key, a key space the social module's two-key pair locks (`apps/api/src/social/repo.ts:1133`) never share, held until commit, so one person's decisions are counted one at a time; then more decisions by `decided_by` in the last hour or the last day than `moderation_limits` allows raises `decision_cap`.
  8. The ground: `ground = 'illegal'` and `outcome = 'not_evident'` each need a considered item with `alleges_illegal` (`invalid_decision`).
  9. `source`: `notice` when a considered item of a `user` intake alleges illegality; else `report` when a considered item came from a `user` intake; else `own_initiative`. A correction or a review copies its reviewed decision's.
  10. Telling. `ended_interim` is forced true for a `final` decision that answers through the case's last item (`through_seq = intake_seq`, so it closes the case) while an interim measure of the case, other than a `seal_media`, that its author was told of (its decision's `author_mail` is not `none`) is in force, read from `measures_in_force` in a statement of its own after step 1's lock; `decision_actions_check` also sets it for a decision holding a reversal of an interim measure, and `decisions_apply` for the end of a seal taken while deciding, by the rule of its own bullet (below). `author_mail` is forced `pending` for: an `interim` decision, unless the case's target was deleted by its author or the purge (the post `deleted_by_author`, or the profile's `deleted_at` set, read after the subject's profile lock of 7.5 step 2, which deletion and the purge take first, so it cannot change before the commit), since such a measure can never take effect; a `final` decision with outcome `restricted`; a `correction` of a decision its author was told of; (0017) a `review`, whatever its outcome, since it answers the author's appeal; and any decision whose `ended_interim` is true (P1 law condition 4: every end or lift of a measure in force is told); else `none`.
- **`public.decisions_apply()`**, AFTER INSERT ON `decisions`, SECURITY DEFINER. For a `final` decision it sets `answered_by` and `outcome` on the items it answers (`restricted` with ground `illegal` gives `restricted_legal`, with `incompatible` `restricted_rule`; `not_evident` gives `not_evident` on an item alleging illegality and `no_violation` on any other; `no_violation` gives `no_violation`). For every kind: `state_version + 1`, `last_decided_at`; for `final`, `answered_seq = through_seq`, then `lane` and `oldest_open_at` recomputed from the items still unanswered (2 and null when none) and `closed_at = now()` when none is left, and then, for a post case it closed, `moderation_apply(array[post], true)`, so the case's interim measures end (it takes the post and its images after the subject's `auth.users` row, step 6). One place decides that a seal taken while deciding has ended: for a `final` decision this trigger reads the case's interim `seal_media` in force from `measures_in_force` before it answers the items and again after. For each that is no longer in force it calls `moderation_apply(<that seal's listed posts>, false)`; when the author was told of any of them, it sets the decision's `ended_interim` true and its `author_mail` `pending` where it is `none`, an update of its own row as `decision_actions_check` makes. A final decision that then holds a `seal_media` of its own lists and seals again in its action's trigger, so each image row is written twice inside the transaction and never stops being sealed outside it. It confirms the decision's texts: `update public.staff_texts set decision_id = NEW.id, confirmed_at = now() where digest in (the decision's non-null digests) and confirmed_at is null and erased_at is null`, raising `text_not_found` unless it confirmed one text per non-null digest the decision names (measured: without that check, a sweep that deleted a text after step 4 read it left the decision naming nothing). This trigger fires before any of the decision's actions exists (section 3); the actions' own triggers do the rest.
- **`public.decision_actions_check()`**, BEFORE INSERT ON `decision_actions`, SECURITY DEFINER. In this order:
  1. It refuses (`invalid_decision`) an action whose decision was not inserted by this transaction (unless `decisions.xact = pg_current_xact_id()` and `decisions.decided_at = now()`, the pair that a logical restore cannot reproduce, section 3), so a measure or a reversal is added only with its own confirmed decision.
  2. It clears and then forces `subject_account_id`, `post_id`, `reverses_action_id`, `no_effect` and `created_at`; for a `seal_media` or an `unseal_media` it also forces `media_id` to null (6.1 `decision_actions`).
  3. Fit (`invalid_decision` otherwise): post and image kinds only on a post case, field kinds only on an account case; an `interim` decision holds `withhold`, `withhold_media`, `reset_display_name`, `reset_bio`, and `restore` or `restore_media` of the case's interim `withhold` or `withhold_media`; a `final` one holds `remove`, `remove_media`, `reset_display_name`, `reset_bio`, and the same reversals; `seal_media` is held by an `interim` or a `final` decision, on a post case or an account case; `unseal_media` only by a `review` or a `correction`; an interim field reset is lifted early only by a correction of its own decision, and otherwise ends with its case; a `review` or a `correction` reversals only, of the reviewed decision's measures; a decision holds at most one post measure (`withhold` or `remove`; a `seal_media` is none and may stand beside one); an image measure's or reversal's `media_id` is an image of the post.
  4. On a post case it locks the post `FOR UPDATE` (the lock `moderation_apply` takes after the insert when it writes the post's level, taken earlier in the same order; for a `seal_media` or an `unseal_media`, `moderation_apply` takes image rows only and this is the only lock on the case's own post) and reads what follows in statements of their own.
  5. An interim `withhold` or `withhold_media` is refused (`invalid_decision`) when an interim measure of the same kind and `media_id` is already in force in the case; the case is held `FOR UPDATE` since `decisions_record` step 1, so two decisions of one case cannot both pass. An interim `reset_display_name` or `reset_bio` is not refused for that reason: its author may set the field again at any time (section 2, row Case targets and measures), so whoever is on duty can reset it again while the case waits, for example for an admin's final decision on an R5 item. Each such reset is a measure of its own decision, lifted only by a correction of that decision (step 3), so step 6 never chooses among several.
  6. A reversal names one measure, deterministically: the measure of the matching kind (`restore` a post measure, `unseal_media` the `seal_media`, `restore_media` the image measure of its `media_id`, `restore_display_name` a `reset_display_name`, `restore_bio` a `reset_bio`) held by the reviewed decision, for a review or a correction, or, for an interim or final decision (a `restore` or `restore_media` only, step 3), the case's interim measure of that kind and `media_id` in force. A decision holds at most one post measure and one measure per kind and image (`decision_actions_once`), and a case at most one interim withhold of the post and of each image in force (step 5), so there is never a choice among several, and no search for "the newest" (P1). The measure must be in force (`invalid_decision` otherwise); `reverses_action_id` is set to it, and `decision_actions_reversed_once` refuses a second lift.
  7. The seal. A `seal_media` needs the decision's forced `suspect` true and its rule R2 or its category `STATEMENT_CATEGORY_PROTECTION_OF_MINORS` (`invalid_decision`). It is not refused because a seal of the case is already in force: each seal is a measure of its own decision with its own list (P2), which on an account case is how images posted since are covered. No other measure is special to a decision whose `suspect` is true: a moderator decides the text of a frozen post as of any post, and its images are not there to be judged.
  8. `account_is_staff(subject)` is called for every measure (the row is already held `FOR SHARE` since `decisions_record` step 6, so the lock order holds), and a measure of a decision whose kind is not `interim` on a staff account is refused (`staff_target`): staff are disciplined by demotion, while an interim measure applies to them as to anyone.
  9. Admin measures: an `unseal_media` sets the decision's `needs_admin` true (an UPDATE of its own transaction's decision row, as its owner) and needs the role `admin` of the deciding account's row already held `FOR SHARE` (`admin_required`). So a moderator's correction that lifts a seal is refused, also for a seal that moderator took while deciding, and whatever later asks (a correction of this decision, the console) reads the stored `needs_admin`.
  10. `no_effect`, for a measure, read after the post's lock or the subject's profile lock: `deleted` when the post is `deleted_by_author` or the profile's `deleted_at` is set; `already` when the post's level already is at least the measure's (`withhold`: not `visible`; `remove`: `removed`), when an image measure's post is not `visible` or its image's state already is at least the measure's (in the order `visible`, `withheld`, `removed`, `sealed`), or when a reset's field already holds its placeholder (the display name equal to the handle, the bio `''`); else null. For a `seal_media` this step forces null, and `decision_actions_apply` sets it once it has made the seal's list (below). The statement, the notifiers' line and the console read it (P2: a measure that changes nothing now says so).
  11. A reversal of an interim measure its author was told of (its decision's `author_mail` is not `none`) sets the decision's `ended_interim` true and its `author_mail` `pending` where it is `none`, so every end or lift of a measure the author knows of is told, and none they were never told of.
- **`public.decision_actions_apply()`**, AFTER INSERT ON `decision_actions`, SECURITY DEFINER:
  - every post or image measure or reversal: `moderation_apply(array[post_id], true)` (a `seal_media` and an `unseal_media` have no `post_id` and have their own bullets, below);
  - `seal_media`, in this order: (1) one statement locks the image rows the case reaches, in id order, `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`: on a post case `where post_id = <the case's post>`, on an account case `where owner_id = <the account> and post_id is not null`, the rows `MediaForCases.hold` locks for an owner (section 5); no post is locked for it (section 2, Lock order); (2) it writes one `seal_posts` row for each distinct `post_id` among the rows that statement returned, with their number, and raises `invalid_decision` when there is none (the case reaches no image, so there is nothing to seal); (3) it forces the action's `no_effect`, by an UPDATE of its own row as the owner: `deleted` when the case's target is deleted (the post `deleted_by_author`, or the profile's `deleted_at` set); else `already` when seals in force of at least its own standing already list every post it lists (for a seal taken while deciding, any seal in force; for a seal for good, seals for good only, so that the seal for good that follows its own case's seal taken while deciding is not told as changing nothing); else null; (4) `moderation_apply(<the listed posts>, false)`. The list is fixed at that moment: an image posted later is not sealed by it. Measured (section 3): an account's seal takes 1.5 s at 5,000 image rows and 5.9 s at 20,000;
  - `unseal_media`: `moderation_apply(<the lifted seal's listed posts>, false)`;
  - `reset_display_name` and `reset_bio` whose `no_effect` is null: refuse with `profile_changed` unless the live field equals the decision's snapshot (compare-and-set, T51(d), G3 7.1), then set the display name to the profile's handle as it then stands, or the bio to `''`; with `no_effect` set they write nothing, so nothing is ever written into a row the purge anonymised;
  - `restore_display_name` and `restore_bio`: nothing; the old value stays readable on the screen of the decision that reset it, whose snapshot holds it by the reset's compare-and-set, while that snapshot is kept, unless an R4 redaction erased it (6.4, 7.6; P1 law condition 6);
  - a reversal in a `review` or a `correction` of a `final` decision: `revision_pending_at = now()` on the items that decision answered, so the daily release shows their new outcome (6.2 `release_outcomes`, 7.8).
- **`public.decisions_check()`**, SECURITY DEFINER, behind the constraint trigger `decisions_check` AFTER INSERT ON `decisions` `DEFERRABLE INITIALLY DEFERRED`. At commit it requires:
  - (a) the audit link and its content (G4 5.5): the head row `a` (`public.audit_head h join public.audit_log a on a.chain_seq = h.seq where h.id = 1`) must have `a.metadata ->> 'decisionId' = NEW.id::text`, `a.step_up is not null` and `a.actor_id = NEW.decided_by`; for a decision of kind `review`, `a.action = 'appeals_reviewed'`, `a.target_type = 'decision'` and `a.target_id = NEW.reviews_decision_id::text`, for every other kind `a.action = 'case_decided'`, `a.target_type = 'case'` and `a.target_id = NEW.case_id::text`; `(a.metadata -> 'params') - 'actions'` must equal (jsonb `=`) the object built from the row under section 10's member names: for `case_decided` `jsonb_build_object('kind', kind, 'stateVersion', seen_state_version, 'throughSeq', through_seq, 'reviewsDecisionId', reviews_decision_id, 'snapshotId', snapshot_id, 'snapshotDigest', snapshot_digest, 'outcome', outcome, 'ground', ground, 'ruleId', rule_id, 'rulesVersion', rules_version, 'redressVersion', redress_version, 'category', category, 'categoryKeyword', category_keyword, 'factsDigest', facts_digest, 'reasonDigest', reason_digest, 'legalReferenceDigest', legal_reference_digest)`, for `appeals_reviewed` `jsonb_build_object('stateVersion', seen_state_version, 'appealId', appeal_id, 'snapshotId', snapshot_id, 'snapshotDigest', snapshot_digest, 'outcome', outcome, 'rulesVersion', rules_version, 'redressVersion', redress_version, 'category', category, 'reasonDigest', reason_digest)` (the members `AppealsReviewedParams` carries, section 10; a review's other statement columns are null by `decisions_reversal_plain`); and the decision's `decision_actions`, aggregated as `jsonb_build_object('kind', kind, 'mediaId', media_id)`, must equal `metadata.params.actions` as a set (each array contains the other, with the operands in parentheses, and both have the same length; `decision_actions_once` and the params' refinement exclude repeats). Each refusal raises `invalid_decision` with a detail naming the member. Measured on PostgreSQL 18.6: a decision naming another case than its audit row, another decider, another rule or one action more than the params was refused at COMMIT; the matching decision committed with its actions inserted in another order than the params list them.
  - (b) consistency of outcome and actions: `restricted` holds at least one measure; `no_violation` and `not_evident` hold no measure (they may hold `restore` or `restore_media` of the case's interim withholds, `decision_actions_check` step 3); `kept` holds no action; `reversed` holds at least one reversal and leaves no measure of the reviewed decision in force; `partly_reversed` holds at least one reversal and leaves at least one in force. Each refusal raises `invalid_decision` with a detail naming the rule. No rule here reasons about other cases' measures or about states: the level follows from the ledger. 3a's rule that an audit row is always the last statement of its transaction becomes "followed only by deferred checks that read": they take only ACCESS SHARE locks, which `korbat_api` cannot block (G4 5.3).
- **`public.append_confirmed_audit(actor_id uuid, key_id uuid, action text, target_type text, target_id text, metadata jsonb, staff_session uuid, action_hash text, step_up jsonb, request_id text, expires_ms bigint) returns bigint`**, SECURITY DEFINER, `search_path = public, pg_temp`, EXECUTE to `korbat_api` only (G2 4.2): refuses an action outside `case_decided`, `appeals_reviewed` and `identity_revealed`, and any null key, session, hash, step-up or expiry; `PERFORM 1 FROM auth.users u WHERE u.id = actor_id AND u.deleted_at IS NULL AND u.role <> 'user' FOR SHARE` (else raise `staff_confirmation_ended`); then, a second statement, `PERFORM 1 FROM auth.staff_keys k WHERE k.id = key_id AND k.user_id = actor_id AND k.revoked_at IS NULL FOR SHARE` (else `staff_confirmation_ended`); for `identity_revealed`, it then confirms the reason: `UPDATE public.staff_texts SET confirmed_at = now()` on the one text that is of kind `reveal_reason`, written by `actor_id`, belongs to the case of the intake item `target_id` names, has the digest `metadata->'params'->>'reasonDigest'`, and has `confirmed_at` and `erased_at` null, raising `text_not_found` when no row changed; inserts the row into `public.audit_log` returning `chain_seq` and `created_at`; raises `staff_confirmation_late` when `floor(extract(epoch from created_at) * 1000) >= expires_ms`; returns the place.
- **`public.item_current_outcome(item uuid, at timestamptz) returns item_outcome`**, plain, STABLE, EXECUTE to nobody (`release_outcomes` calls it as its owner): for an item whose `outcome` is `restricted_rule` or `restricted_legal`, `reversed` when none of its answering decision's measures is in force counting only reversals created before `at`, `partly_reversed` when some but not all are, else the written outcome; for any other item, its written outcome. An item keeps what its answering decision wrote; what notifiers are shown is this reading, made at the daily release (P1).
- **`public.release_outcomes(cutoff timestamptz) returns integer`**, SECURITY DEFINER, EXECUTE to `korbat_api` only: the daily release of 7.8 step 3(a), the only writer of `outcome_sent_at`, `shown_outcome`, `revised_sent_at` and `revision_pending_at`'s clearing, so the API supplies neither a release time nor an outcome (a compromised API cannot tell notifiers a false outcome). It raises unless `cutoff <= now()`. Then, locking the items `FOR UPDATE` in id order, each write in a statement of its own after the lock: (1) every item whose `outcome` is set, whose `outcome_sent_at` is null and which was answered before the cut-off (its answering decision's `decided_at`, or for an item answered at intake its `created_at`) gets `outcome_sent_at = now()` and `shown_outcome = item_current_outcome(item, cutoff)`; (2) every released item whose `revision_pending_at` is set gets, when `item_current_outcome(item, cutoff)` differs from its `shown_outcome`, that outcome as `shown_outcome`, `revised_sent_at = now()` and `outcome_mail = 'pending'` where it is not `none` (only when the shown outcome actually changes); (3) in both, `revision_pending_at` is cleared when it is before the cut-off and kept otherwise, so a reversal made after the cut-off is released at the next one (T47). It answers how many items it changed.
- **`public.case_history`**, a view with `security_invoker = true`, the case page's history in one read (practice-3c 10.2's event log, without a table of its own): one row per item attached (`seq`, `created_at`, the intake's kind and ground, and whether it is a re-examination), snapshot taken, decision (its kind, outcome, `decided_by`), appeal filed or answered, note (a `note` text's id), a recorded choice not to seal (a `seal_declined` text's id), reveal (`case_access_log` rows of kind `identity_revealed`), evidence erased and R4 redaction, ordered by time. Notes and erasures carry ids, never text.

### 6.3 Migration 0017: appeals (Task 8)

- **`public.appeals_check()`**, BEFORE INSERT ON `appeals`, SECURITY DEFINER. Forces `filed_at`, `answered_by`, `result`, `answered_at` and `erased_at` (null). Before any check it locks the appealed decision's case row `FOR KEY SHARE`, which waits for a transaction holding the case `FOR UPDATE` (every decision, `decisions_record` step 1; `erase_case_evidence` and `redact_r4_body`, 6.4) and does not wait for an intake's update of an open case. Its checks are then statements of their own, which under READ COMMITTED see a decision that committed while it waited, and the deadline is compared with `clock_timestamp()` read after the lock, never `now()`: an appeal whose transaction began before its deadline but whose lock waited for an erasure or a redaction that found the decision final is refused rather than left pending on erased evidence (lab, PostgreSQL 18.6: after the wait `now()` still gave the transaction's start). It refuses with `appeal_not_open` unless the decision is `final` with outcome `restricted`, `appellant_account_id` is the decision's subject account, `clock_timestamp() < appeal_deadline(informed_at)`, and at least one of the decision's measures is in force (`measures_in_force`), so a decision a correction has reversed in full leaves its author nothing to appeal, and an appeal filed while such a correction commits waits for it and is refused. The unique index refuses a second appeal (`appeal_exists`).
- **`public.review_needs_admin(decision uuid) returns boolean`**, SECURITY DEFINER, STABLE, `search_path = public, pg_temp`, EXECUTE to `korbat_api` only. It only reads, and it reads two stored facts: true when the decision cites R2 or holds a `seal_media`; false otherwise, including for a decision that does not exist. It never reads the freeze, so its answer does not change while an appeal waits. A review of a restriction under R5 therefore needs no admin (round 1 decision 11): the stored `needs_admin` of the reviewed decision is not inherited by reviews (it is by corrections, 6.2 step 6). `decisions_record` forces a review's `needs_admin` from it, and `appeal_reviewer_state`, the appeals list's mark, `AppealsPage`, `CaseAppeal` and the author's `DecisionView` read it (7.7). It can be true only for a decision whose author was told that it cites R2 or sealed their images, so the author's surfaces show it as it is.
- **`public.appeal_reviewer_state(appeal uuid) returns text`**, SECURITY DEFINER, `search_path = public, pg_temp`, EXECUTE to `korbat_api` only. It only reads, and answers a word, never which accounts: null for an appeal that does not exist or is answered; otherwise `available` when at least one live staff account (`auth.users` with `role <> 'user'` and `deleted_at` null, read without a lock) decided no decision of the case, opened no finding in it (the rule of `reviewer_conflict`) and has `staff_case_conflict(that account, the case, true)` false; else `none_single_staff` when korbat has at most one live staff account; else `none_eligible`. When `review_needs_admin` of the appeal's decision is true, only accounts with `role = 'admin'` count, in each of the three answers.
- **`public.decisions_record()`** replaced (CREATE OR REPLACE keeps owner and grants; stated again all the same). Step 2 gains `review`: `reviews_decision_id` names a `final` decision of this case with outcome `restricted`; `appeal_id` is forced to that decision's one pending appeal, read after step 1's lock (`case_changed` when there is none); the outcome is `kept`, `partly_reversed` or `reversed`; the review carries the reviewed decision's `category` and `rules_version` and its own `redress_version` (`invalid_decision` otherwise). Step 4 requires `reason_digest` to name a `review_reason` text. Step 5's parties are the subject and the reporters of the items the reviewed decision answered. A new check refuses (`reviewer_conflict`) a review whose `decided_by` decided any earlier decision of the case or is the `finding_by` of an intake with an item in the case (C30; practice-3c 10.20: no review by a moderator who reported in the case). Step 6 forces `needs_admin` from `review_needs_admin(reviews_decision_id)`. Step 10 makes `author_mail` `pending` for every review, `kept` included.
- **`public.decisions_apply()`** replaced: a review sets `answered_by`, `result` (its outcome) and `answered_at = now()` on its appeal. A correction with outcome `reversed` answers the corrected decision's pending appeal: `answered_by` the correction, `result = 'reversed'`, `answered_at = now()`. A correction with outcome `partly_reversed` answers none: the appeal waits for a review, whose reversals reach only what is still in force, and whose `kept` means that what remains stands.

### 6.4 Migration 0018: retention (Task 9)

Each kind of data has its own due rule, read only from the anchors of the data it erases (P8), its own erase function and its own term in `erasureOverdue` (7.3). The R4 redaction is a one-way erasure keyed on measures in force. No due rule knows the freeze: a frozen post's snapshots and images are kept by gates E and K, which every erase function and every delete meets (6.1, the freeze).

- **`public.decision_final_at(decision_id uuid) returns timestamptz`**, plain, STABLE, `search_path = public, pg_temp`, EXECUTE to `korbat_api`: for a `final` decision with outcome `restricted`, null while its author's appeal is pending, its `answered_at` once answered, and `appeal_deadline(informed_at)` when none was filed; for every other decision, its `decided_at` (an interim decision, a no-action decision, a review and a correction cannot be appealed).
- **`public.case_evidence_due_at(case_id uuid) returns timestamptz`**, plain, STABLE, EXECUTE to `korbat_api`: null (not due) while the case is open, and while a `final` decision of the case with outcome `restricted` has a measure in force and its `decision_final_at` is null or in the future; otherwise the latest of the case's `closed_at`, those decisions' `decision_final_at`, 24 hours after the case's newest unerased snapshot (`taken_at`) and its newest unconfirmed, unerased text (`created_at`), and 30 days after each field reset of the case stopped being in force (its reversal's `created_at`, or `closed_at` for an interim reset that ended with the case), so a person whose reset stops before the case's copies are erased can read the old value on the screen of the decision that reset it for at least 30 days (P1 law condition 6, P8 condition 5). It reads nothing about items, mails, releases, notifiers or the freeze. A snapshot of a frozen post does not go with its case's evidence: gate E keeps it (6.1, the freeze). So a fresh snapshot or a draft text that a moderator takes on a case already due, for a correction or a review, waits a day before `erase_case_evidence` may erase it, and `sweep_staff_texts` removes an unused draft after 24 hours anyway.
- **`public.intake_due_at(intake_id uuid) returns timestamptz`**, plain, STABLE, EXECUTE to `korbat_api`: null while any item of the intake is unanswered (a re-examination included), has `revision_pending_at` set, has an outcome and no `outcome_sent_at`, has `outcome_mail` `pending`, or is open to re-examination (outcome `no_violation` or `not_evident` from a decision, `outcome_sent_at` set, `appeal_deadline(outcome_sent_at)` in the future, not re-examined and not itself a re-examination, but for the one that 6.1's attach trigger excepts: an item about a post that is not suspect and that a decision whose `unseen` is true answered), and while the answering decision of any of its items has outcome `restricted` and a `decision_final_at` that is null or in the future, so a reversal on the author's appeal still reaches a signed-in reporter by mail and in Your reports (P8 conditions); otherwise 30 days after the latest `outcome_sent_at` and `revised_sent_at` of its items. A correction made after an intake was erased still records `no_address` for a signed-in reporter, the residual round 4 accepted.
- **`public.erase_case_evidence(case_id uuid) returns integer`**, SECURITY DEFINER, EXECUTE to `korbat_api`: first locks the case `FOR UPDATE`, then, in a statement of its own, raises `not_due` unless `case_evidence_due_at(case_id) <= now()` (a null due time is not due). The lock waits for a decision on the case, an appeal being filed (`appeals_check`'s `FOR KEY SHARE`), and the insert of a snapshot or a text (their foreign keys' `FOR KEY SHARE`), and the due time read after it sees their commits (without it, a decision that read its snapshot unerased committed naming a snapshot the erasure had just erased; lab, PostgreSQL 18.6). It then erases three things: the case's unerased snapshots, through `erase_snapshots` (below), which keeps those of a frozen post; the case's notes and unconfirmed texts (`seal_declined` texts among them; a confirmed `review_reason` text is its review's reason, kept as a statement text); and the statements of the appeals of the case's decisions (`statement` and `statement_salt` null, `erased_at = now()`, digest kept). It then releases the case's holds (`release_case_holds(case_id, null)`), which frees no image of a frozen post (gate K), and answers how many rows it changed. It touches no intake. A case holds a row this function would change exactly when it is a candidate case of 7.9; a case whose only unerased rows are snapshots of a frozen post holds none.
- **`public.erase_snapshots(snapshots uuid[]) returns integer`** (gate E; 6.1, the freeze), SECURITY DEFINER, `search_path = public, pg_temp`, EXECUTE to nobody: `erase_case_evidence` and `redact_r4_body` call it as its owner, and it is the only writer of `evidence_snapshots.erased_at` (pinned, 6.5). It raises `read_committed_only` (6.1) unless the transaction's isolation is READ COMMITTED. In one statement it erases the content of each named unerased snapshot (`erased_at = now()`, the content columns null) except a snapshot whose `target_type` is `post` and for whose `target_id` `public.post_frozen` is true, and answers how many it erased. So no path erases a snapshot of a frozen post, in whatever case the snapshot is, and no due rule needs a term for it. It judges the freeze on its statement's snapshot: a suspect item that commits during that statement is not seen, and the outcome equals "erased first, reported second".
- **`public.erase_intake(intake_id uuid) returns integer`**, SECURITY DEFINER, EXECUTE to `korbat_api`: first locks the intake `FOR UPDATE` (which a re-examination's `FOR KEY SHARE` waits for, 6.1), then, in a statement of its own, raises `not_due` unless `intake_due_at(intake_id) <= now()`. It nulls `explanation` and `cited_law` and sets `erased_at` (its `bona_fide` stays), sets `reporter_id` and its items' `reporter_account_id` to null, so who reported whom goes with the intake record, and erases its re-examinations' statements (statement and salt null, `erased_at = now()`, digest kept). It answers how many rows it changed.
- **`public.erase_statement_texts(decision_id uuid) returns integer`**, the same pattern: it nulls the texts that the decision's `facts_digest`, `reason_digest` and `legal_reference_digest` name, a review's or a correction's `review_reason` text among them (digests kept), when the decision's subject account has `auth.users.deleted_at` set at least 12 months before `now()`, read inside the function; otherwise it raises `not_due`. The retention job finds candidates by `profiles.deleted_at`, which `korbat_api` can write; the function decides by the auth row, which it cannot write, and whose mark `korbat_auth` can set only to `now()` and never clear (`users_deleted_mark`, below). The take-down sets the auth row's mark before the purge sets the profile's, so the function never refuses a decision the job names in good faith.
- **`public.redact_r4_body(decision_id uuid) returns boolean`**, SECURITY DEFINER, EXECUTE to `korbat_api`.
  - It raises `invalid_decision` unless the decision is `final`, has `rule_id = 'R4'`, and holds a `remove`, `remove_media`, `reset_bio` or `reset_display_name`.
  - It locks, in this order: for an account case, the profile's row `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`; then the decision's case `FOR UPDATE`, so it waits for a decision on the case or an appeal being filed and sees their commits; then, for a post case, the post's row `FOR UPDATE`, and, in a statement of its own, every image row of the post `ORDER BY id FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, before it writes anything. This is the order of `moderation_apply` and of an intake's locks. Writing the erased image's `erased_at` first deadlocked with an intake locking the same images (lab, PostgreSQL 18.6).
  - Then, in statements of their own, it raises `read_committed_only` (6.1) unless the transaction's isolation is READ COMMITTED, raises `not_due` unless `decision_final_at(decision_id) <= now()`, and changes nothing and answers false unless the decision's `redacted_at` is null and one of its R4 measures is in force, whatever the post's lifecycle (the author's deletion and the purge leave korbat's copies, which the redaction must still erase; P3). For a post case it also changes nothing and answers false while `post_frozen(post)` is true, read in a statement of its own after the locks: the post's body, its images and its snapshots in every case then wait for the end of the freeze. After an admin's answer without a seal the redaction erases them as below; after a seal for good it goes on waiting, since sealed images and the snapshots of their post are kept (6.6). The retention job tries again at its next round (7.9).
  - It sets `posts.body = ''` and `posts.erased_at = now()` when its `remove` is in force (a deleted post's body is already blank, and its state never changes), and the image's `post_media.erased_at = now()` when its `remove_media` is in force. `posts.erased_at` is set only for a post-level removal, never for an image or a field (P1 security condition (f)).
  - It erases, through `erase_snapshots`, every unerased snapshot whose target is the decision's target (the post, or the account), in this case and in any other, so no copy of the R4 content outlives the restriction's finality or, for a frozen post, the end of the freeze (G6 row 8).
  - It sets the decision's `redacted_at = now()` only when no unerased snapshot of that target is left in any case. When gate E kept one (a suspect item about the author's account committed between the check and the erasure: the intake locks image rows `FOR KEY SHARE`, which does not wait for this function's locks), the decision stays a candidate, and the round after the freeze ends finishes it. It releases the case's holds on the images it erased: `release_case_holds(case_id, media)`, where `media` is every image of the post for its `remove` and that image for its `remove_media`. For a post case it then calls `moderation_apply(array[post], true)`. The case's other holds (another image of the post) stay its evidence until `erase_case_evidence`, so an unanswered item of a case still open keeps its images through the author's deletion (section 14, criterion 4). The post stays removed for good (`erased_at`) and the image is disposable once nothing retains it (7.9); a later reversal lifts the measure in the ledger and changes nothing visible.
- **`public.sweep_staff_texts(older_than timestamptz) returns integer`**: raises unless `older_than <= now() - interval '24 hours'`; deletes the texts of every kind but `note` and `seal_declined` whose `confirmed_at` is null and whose `created_at` is before `older_than`; never a confirmed text, a note or a `seal_declined` text.
- **`public.delete_access_log(older_than timestamptz) returns integer`**: raises unless `older_than` is at least 12 months ago. It first erases the `reveal_reason` texts that the `identity_revealed` rows older than `older_than` name and that are not erased yet (text and salt null, `erased_at = now()`, digest kept), then deletes the access rows older than `older_than`.
- **`public.forget_reporter(account uuid) returns integer`**: for an account whose `auth.users.deleted_at` is set, read inside the function (the take-down sets it before any purge step, so the purge's call between `purgeProfile` and `finishPurge`, 7.10, finds it; `purged_at` is not set yet then and is not required), sets `intakes.reporter_id` and `intake_items.reporter_account_id` of the account to null; for any other account it changes nothing. Its intakes go on being processed (Art. 16(6); `law-3c.md` 9.7).
- **`auth.users_deleted_mark()`**, a plain trigger function (`search_path = auth, pg_temp`, EXECUTE revoked from PUBLIC) behind the trigger `users_deleted_mark` BEFORE UPDATE OF `deleted_at` ON `auth.users` FOR EACH ROW. When `current_user` is `korbat_auth`, a `deleted_at` that is set cannot be cleared or changed (SQLSTATE 23514), and one set from null becomes `now()` whatever value was supplied. The API holds `korbat_auth` too (section 11); without the trigger, code running as the API could back-date a live account's mark, call `erase_statement_texts` or `forget_reporter`, and clear the mark (`0013_forget_revoked_staff_keys.sql:2`; `0001_grants.sql:8,14`). No code clears the mark (`apps/api/src/auth/repo.ts:445,459` set it only where it is null), and `now()` replaces the take-down's own time with its transaction's start. The migrator, and tests that move the clock as it, are not bound.
- **`public.release_case_holds(case_id uuid, media uuid[])`**: sets `released_at` on the case's unreleased holds, one row at a time in `media_id` order: every such hold when `media` is null, and otherwise only those on the images `media` names. `erase_case_evidence` calls it with null, and `redact_r4_body` with the images it erased. EXECUTE to nobody.
- **`auth.staff_link_counts() returns table (staff_user_id uuid, links integer)`**, SECURITY DEFINER, `search_path = auth, pg_temp`, EXECUTE revoked from PUBLIC and granted to `korbat_auth` alone: one row per staff account with at least one link and its count, never which accounts. `staff.js keys` calls it on the auth role.
- Every function of this section but `auth.staff_link_counts`, `auth.users_deleted_mark`, `decision_final_at`, `case_evidence_due_at` and `intake_due_at` is SECURITY DEFINER with `search_path = public, pg_temp`; all but `release_case_holds`, `erase_snapshots`, `auth.staff_link_counts` and `auth.users_deleted_mark` have EXECUTE granted to `korbat_api` alone, which the retention job and the account purge call.

### 6.5 What the schema, grants and function tests pin

`packages/db/test/schema.test.ts`: "there are 28 tables in total" (`:49-51`) becomes 39 (28, minus the five dropped, plus `cases`, `intakes`, `intake_items`, `decisions`, `decision_actions`, `seal_posts`, `appeals`, `staff_texts`, `evidence_snapshots`, `case_access_log`, `moderation_limits`, `media_holds`, and the four auth tables). The `transparency_log` and `appeals_case_idx` tests (`:52-55,60-63`) are replaced by tests pinning: the partial unique indexes `cases_one_open_per_target`, `intake_items_target_once`, `intake_items_reexamines_once`, `intake_items_reporter_once` (its three columns and its predicate), `decisions_appeal_once` and `decision_actions_reversed_once`, and the unique `appeals_once`; every check of `intakes` and `decisions` by name, and `intake_items_named_handle`; `decisions`' and `decision_actions`' forced columns and the `xid8` type of `decisions.xact`; `posts`' two new columns, `post_media`'s three, `intake_items.suspect`, `intake_items.named_handle`, `decisions.suspect`, `decisions.unseen`, `seal_posts`' columns and key, and that `post_state` still has its four values; each new auth table's schema, RLS and single policy; and that no moderation table has a column named like `email` or `address`. The 1b test's exact column list of `handle_history` (`:136`), `audit_log`'s single index (`:488`) and the auth event type's tail (`:363-374`) do not change. A database test (Task 1, as `korbat_api`) pins the grants: UPDATE, DELETE and TRUNCATE refused on `cases`, `decision_actions`, `appeals`, `staff_texts`, `evidence_snapshots`, `case_access_log` and `media_holds`; on `intakes`, `intake_items` and `decisions` only their granted columns updatable and no DELETE or TRUNCATE; `moderation_limits` SELECT only; `seal_posts` SELECT only; no DELETE on `posts` or `profiles` and no TRUNCATE on `posts`, `profiles` or `post_media`; the column grants of `posts` and `post_media` (no UPDATE of `moderation_level`, `erased_at`, `moderation_state` or `hold_until`); no access to `auth`; and `has_schema_privilege('korbat_api', 'auth', 'usage')` false, which no test pins today (G2 4.4).

A function test, an integration test as `korbat_api` in the manner of `apps/api/test/integration/staff-schema.test.ts:383-403`, reads the definition (`pg_get_functiondef`) of every function owned by `korbat_migrate`, and the definitions of the views, triggers, constraints, index predicates and policies, and pins who writes and who names what; its expected lists grow with the migrations. After 0015 (Task 1): `frozen_for` is named only by `post_frozen` and by the function of `post_media_keep`; `post_frozen` is named by nothing else; no function deletes from `post_media`. After 0016 (Task 2): only `moderation_apply` writes `posts.moderation_level`, `posts.state` and `post_media.moderation_state`, and only `decision_actions_apply` writes `seal_posts` (P1 security condition (c)); `post_frozen` is named by `decisions_record`, which reads it to force `decisions.unseen`, and by nothing else. After 0018 (Task 9): only `redact_r4_body` writes `posts.erased_at` and `post_media.erased_at`; `post_frozen` is named also by `erase_snapshots` and `redact_r4_body`, and by nothing else; only `erase_snapshots` writes `evidence_snapshots.erased_at`. The source pins that go with them are in the API's architecture test (section 12): `post_frozen` named only in `apps/api/src/media/repo.ts` and `apps/api/src/moderation/repo.ts`, and `frozen_for` nowhere in the API; a delete of `post_media` only in `media/repo.ts`; and the names `requestDeletion` and `openImage` in a pinned list of file and count.

### 6.6 Retention

What 3c keeps, and for how long (G6 section 9 adopted, by consumer, P8; the clocks are those of 6.4). Every period is a design choice published in the terms, built from the contracts' constants (section 10), not a legal figure, unless an anchor says otherwise. G6's row 14 (strike history anonymised after expiry) is 3e's, with strikes.

| Record | Kept | Then | Anchor |
|---|---|---|---|
| Body and images of a withheld post | while the interim measure is in force (until the case's final decision) | per the rows below | main design line 190 |
| Body and images of a post removed under R1, R2, R3, R5, R6 or the law (sealed images follow the row for frozen posts) | while the author keeps the post and the account | blanked and deleted by the author's or the account's deletion | main design line 191; GDPR Art. 15(3). Counsel: R3 or R5 bodies naming a third person |
| Content restricted under R4: a removed post's body and images (also when its author or the account's deletion deleted the post, before or after the removal), a removed image, a reset bio or display name | until the restriction is final (6.4 `decision_final_at`) and, for a post, while it is frozen (6.1) | by `redact_r4_body`: the body blanked and the post removed for good (`erased_at`), the image marked erased, the case's hold on it released, and the image deleted once nothing retains it, every snapshot of the post (or, for a field, of the account) erased; chip and reason kept | GDPR 5(1)(e), 17(1)(a); EDPB 01/2022 paras 172-173. Counsel: longer for claims? |
| The images of a frozen post, kept on their rows whether a hold stands or not, and every snapshot of it in any case (6.1, the freeze) | while a report or finding of child sexual abuse material about the post or about its author's account is unanswered and, once the images are sealed, for as long as the seal stands | after an answer without a seal, or once a seal is lifted, each follows its own row of this table; sealed images, and the snapshots of their post, are released only by sub-project 3d (section 17), and until then the operator acts on them only by hand, on an authority's written instruction (README) | Directive 2011/93/EU Art. 5. Counsel: the period and its basis, for sealed images and for what a freeze keeps on an unverified report (section 2) |
| Case evidence: snapshots (but those of a frozen post, the row above), notes, unconfirmed texts, the author's appeal statements, the case's holds (but those on images an R4 redaction erased, which `redact_r4_body` releases) | until the case is closed and every restriction of the case is final for its author (appeal answered, or six months passed), and 24 hours after the case's newest snapshot or draft text; 30 days after a field reset stopped being in force, when it stopped before they were erased | erased; ids, handles, digests, enums and times kept (the snapshot's `author_handle` and `handle`, which are among the handles `handle_history` keeps for good with their profile, `apps/api/src/social/repo.ts:1051,1114`) | GDPR 17(3)(e); `law-3c.md` 9.5 |
| The intake record: explanation, cited law, who sent it (`intakes.reporter_id`, `intake_items.reporter_account_id`), re-examination statements | until every item is answered, released and mailed, every changed outcome is released, every no-action item's re-examination window has closed (six months after release) or its re-examination is answered, and every restriction it led to is final for its author; then 30 days, while the reporter's list shows them; or until the reporter's account is deleted (who sent it) | erased (`erase_intake`); a reporter summary counts only the rows still linked | GDPR 5(1)(c), (e); `law-3c.md` 9.5; G6 row 13 |
| Images held by a case | as the case's evidence; the images of a frozen post as the row for frozen posts says, held or not | hold released; rows of deleted or erased images are then deleted once their post is not frozen (7.9) | G6 7.3 |
| Statements (facts, reason or a review's reason, legal reference) | while the account exists, and 12 months after its deletion | erased; the decision's enums and dates kept | GDPR 5(1)(e), 17(3)(e). Counsel: the period after deletion |
| Unconfirmed staff texts, but notes and `seal_declined` texts, which are never confirmed and go with their case's evidence (the row Case evidence) | 24 hours | deleted | G1 |
| Access log (case opened, image viewed, identity revealed) | 12 months | deleted | accountability (T55). Counsel |
| Reveal reasons | 12 months, as the access row that names them | erased; digest kept | accountability (T55). Counsel |
| Notifier name and address | until six months after the last day korbat owed the notice a decision, a mail or a changed outcome (13 months after the notice if nothing moved it) | deleted by the auth janitor | DSA 16(4)-(5); `law-3c.md` 9.5 |
| Notifier confirmations | 30 days | deleted | G9 3.2 |
| Notifier suppressions | for as long as korbat runs notices (a keyed hash only) | - | G9 5. Counsel |
| Backups of the database and of the `media-store` volume (each holds every record above as it stood when taken, content erased since included) | 30 days after each was taken (`RETENTION.backupDays`) | deleted by the operator (README, Restoring), except a backup that korbat must keep for the authorities, which it keeps until they have answered (the backup a restore was made from while a seal recorded after it waits for its authority's answer); the rule for a backup that holds released images is 3d's (section 17) | G6 row 17; GDPR 5(1)(e). Counsel: the period |
| Decisions, actions, items, appeals as rows; audit rows | for good | - | the record (3a); ids (none of a reporter's account once its intake record is erased), handles (a snapshot's `author_handle` and `handle`, and an account item's `named_handle`; `handle_history` keeps every handle a profile held for good), enums, times and salted digests only (T56) |

## 7. Flows

### 7.1 Reporting and notifying

`POST /api/notices` (the moderation module's routes; a session is optional). One request is one intake.

1. **Validate.** The body is `NoticeRequest` (section 10): a ground (`rule`, `illegal` or `csam`), one to ten targets (a post by id, optionally with one of its images; an account by handle; or a korbat link), and the ground's fields. A signed-in request with a role other than `user` is refused (`staff_cannot_report`, 403): a moderator's own finding is `POST /api/staff/findings`. A session whose account has not finished onboarding (`Session.onboarded` false, `apps/api/src/auth/sessions.ts:34`; it has no profile row until onboarding, `apps/api/src/profiles/store.ts:13-18`) is refused with `onboarding_required` (403), as `requireSession` refuses it (`apps/api/src/auth/middleware.ts:50-54`); it is never treated as signed out. The signed-in routes of 7.2, 7.6 and 7.7 stand behind `requireSession` for the same reason. A signed-in request carrying a name or an email, and a CSAM request carrying either, are `invalid_request`.
2. **Limits and the human check.** Signed out, whatever the ground: Turnstile on the request's `turnstileToken` through `AuthForModeration.verifyHuman` (a missing or failed token is `human_check_failed`, 403; the API holds no contact address and answers the message "We could not verify that you are human. Try again."; the app words the code itself with the users' contact address, section 9), then 5 intakes an hour and 20 a day per network: the client identity middleware gains `clientNetHash`, the keyed hash of the IPv4 address or of the IPv6 address's first 64 bits (`apps/api/src/auth/client-ip.ts:32-39` today hashes the whole address, so each IPv6 address is a bucket of its own, `repo-3c.md` 10). Signed in, a token is ignored, and the limits are 10 an hour and 50 a day per account. Both through the shared limit store (`rateLimitStore.hit`, as the export's limit, `apps/api/src/account/flows/export.ts:46-55`), failing closed with 503. Every number is published in the terms.
3. **Resolve the targets**, outside any transaction. A link must be `APP_ORIGIN`'s `/post/<uuid>` or `/u/<handle>` (`notice_link_offsite` for another host, `notice_link_invalid` for another path, both 422). Which targets are live is one rule, `liveTarget(target, suspect)` in `intake.ts`, used by this route, the finding route and the re-examination routes (7.2) (P3); `suspect` is true for a CSAM request and for a finding citing R2 (6.1, the freeze). For a suspect intake a target is live while its row exists: a post in any state, one its author deleted or the purge blanked included, and an account whose profile row exists, purged or not (`findAccountByHandle` with `withDeleted`; the purge keeps the handle on the row, `apps/api/src/social/repo.ts:1112-1118`). An old handle still names its account on this path: for a handle that no profile holds now, `findAccountByHandle` with `withDeleted` answers the profile that `handle_history` records for it. `handle_history.handle_lower` is that table's key and carries the profile's id, and no other account can ever take the handle (`apps/api/src/social/repo.ts:1047-1050`), so the handle names that account and nobody else; `liveTarget` then finds its row, and the case, the freeze and the alert follow as for any account. So no report of child sexual abuse material about anything that exists is answered automatically, an account also under a handle it has left, which `handle_history` keeps reserved for it: it waits for an admin, whatever is left of its target. A finding citing R2 resolves the same way. For every other ground a post target is live when it exists and is not `deleted_by_author`, whatever its level, and an account target when a profile holds the named handle now and its `deleted_at` is null (an old handle names nothing, as 1b decided: no redirect from a handle an account has left; a re-examination names no handle, so it stays about the account of the item it re-examines while that profile's `deleted_at` is null); a deleted post or account is then a target with nothing live, so repeated reports cannot keep content its author deleted, nor put deleted images before staff for no protective purpose (T41, GDPR 5(1)(e)). Liveness reads no image. A CSAM request whose targets, once its links are parsed, name more than `CSAM_NOTICE_MAX_ACCOUNTS` (1) accounts is refused (`invalid_request`): one such target reaches every image on the account's posts. The accounts are counted by the handles as named, before any is looked up, so the refusal says nothing about which handles are live or name the same account. **The added item.** A post's card shows the images of the post it quotes (`apps/app/src/components/social/PostCard.tsx:36-46,189`), so a suspect item about a post whose `quote_of_id` names another post is a report about both. For each post target of a suspect intake the route reads `SocialForModeration.postRef` and, when its `quote_of_id` is set and the request names no target on that post, adds that post as a target of its own: an **added item**, at the position of the item it was added for plus 10 (the lowest such position when several named posts quote it). It lands in the quoted post's own case like any item, with that post's own snapshot and hold, so each post has its case, its subject and its statement, and the notifier reads an answer about each (7.2). Neither the deletion nor the purge clears `quote_of_id` (`apps/api/src/social/repo.ts:482-486`, `:1091-1094`), so a deleted quoting post brings it too. One level only, as a card draws one quote deep: an added item brings none of its own. It does not count against the ten targets. `intake_items_quote` refuses at commit an intake that lacks it (6.1). The targets are de-duplicated by `(type, id)`; an account link with nothing live has no id to compare (step 4.5 gives its item a random one), so such links are de-duplicated by their named handles. A signed-in reporter's target whose open case already holds an item of theirs on the same ground (alleging illegality or not) is dropped; when every target is dropped the answer is 409 `already_reported`. (Residual: a reporter whose case about an account is still open can so confirm a handle they guessed to be that account's later one. The profile under that handle shows them the same posts, so the answer tells them nothing the app does not; the report itself never shows a later handle, 7.2.) This de-duplication applies to the rule and illegal grounds only: a CSAM request, signed in or not, is never matched against the session's account or its items, inserts an item for every live target, and is never answered `already_reported`, so a person who sent an illegal-ground notice about a post can still report it on the CSAM path. The answer never says which targets were live (T43): the receipt is the same either way.
4. **The transaction** (API pool; retried up to three times on SQLSTATE 40001 from the attach trigger, on 40P01 (a deadlock) or 55P03 (a lock timeout), or after three empty reads in step 4.3; in each nothing was written, and a notice on the CSAM path has no sender to ask again; and answered 409 `already_reported` on a unique violation of `intake_items_reporter_once`). When the last retry fails the same way, nothing has been written and the route answers 409 `case_closed`. The app words it "Your report could not be saved because its case changed at that moment. Send it again." `POST /api/staff/findings`, which runs the same transaction, answers the same, and the console words it "The case changed while the finding was saved. Open the case again." Its steps:
   1. A signed-in reporter's profile row `FOR KEY SHARE`, refusing a missing or deleted one (`SocialForModeration.lockAccounts`). Not for a CSAM request, which has no reporter.
   2. The intake row: a fresh `ref` (`N-` and 10 base32 characters from 50 random bits, retried on a collision), for a signed-out or CSAM notice a 32-byte token whose sha256 is `lookup_hash`, the channel, `reporter_id` (never for CSAM), a fresh `contact_id` when a signed-out notifier gave a name or an email, and the ground's columns. A rule-only report gets category `STATEMENT_CATEGORY_OTHER_VIOLATION_TC`; the CSAM path `STATEMENT_CATEGORY_PROTECTION_OF_MINORS`, its keyword from `CSAM_KEYWORDS` (null for `abuse`) and `alleges_illegal = true` (it is an Art. 16 notice of illegal content, which 16(2)(c) exempts from name and email; its explanation and bona fide statement stay optional), so its decisions name the source `notice` and may answer it "illegality not evident"; an illegal-ground notice the chosen category or `STATEMENT_CATEGORY_NOT_SPECIFIED_NOTICE`.
   3. In two passes over the live targets, the named and the added ones together (step 3). First, for each in `(target_type, target_id)` order (G3 L12): `insert into cases (target_type, target_id) values (...) on conflict (target_type, target_id) where closed_at is null do nothing`; a read of the open case's id. If the read finds none (a decision closed the case after the insert; each statement of a READ COMMITTED transaction sees the latest commits), the insert and the read run again in the same transaction, which now creates the new open case, at most three times before the transaction is retried as on 40001. A live target's item is never inserted without a case. Then the item with the case, its position, its lane (the contracts' `laneFor`, section 10), its image hint and, for an account, the handle as the request named it, lower-cased (`named_handle`, from which 7.2 builds the address shown back), which the attach trigger numbers and marks `suspect`. Only then, in the second pass, the captures: for every post target, in id order, `capturePost` locks it `FOR SHARE` and reads it (after an erasure, without its body, section 5), and for every account `captureProfile` reads the display name, bio and handle. So every intake locks its cases in one order, and none holds a post while it waits for a case. A deleted post is captured as a tombstone (state `deleted_by_author`, an empty body, its `quote_of_id`).
   4. One `MediaForCases.hold(tx, pairs)` for the whole intake, after every capture (section 5): a pair `{ caseId, postId }` for each post target, which locks the post's image rows and places the case's evidence hold on every one that has none from that case and is not erased, for every ground alike; and, for a suspect item whose target is an account, the pair `{ ownerId }`, which only locks every image row on that account's posts. All of them are locked in one statement, in one global id order, `FOR KEY SHARE`. A suspect item needs no hold to keep what its case reaches: gate K keeps every image of a frozen post from the item's commit on, also one posted later and one on a post its author deletes or the purge blanks (6.1, the freeze). The lock is what makes a delete that races the intake wait and then read the committed item, so that the outcome is all or nothing; a delete that fired before the item was committed, on a row the intake could not lock, wins, as if it had come first. Locking in one order keeps two intakes from locking images in opposite orders (Task 3's lab). Then a snapshot row for each case that holds none yet, or, for an account, whose latest differs (a server-made 32-byte salt, `takenAt` read from the database as 6.1 says, the contracts' `snapshotDigest`, the images and alt texts from the hold's answer).
   5. For each target with nothing live: an item with no case and outcome `not_available` (an account's with its `named_handle`, like any account item).
