Its `target_id` is the uuid the link named when the link is a post's, and a fresh random uuid that names nothing when the link is an account's, whether or not a profile holds or once held the handle. So on no ground does a stored id or a de-duplication tie an old handle to its account outside the CSAM path.
5. **After the commit.** A signed-out notifier's contact is stored (`storeNotifierContact`, awaited: a database write on the auth pool). If that fails, the failure is logged and staff see "contact lost or forgotten" on the item (`notifierFacts` answers `found` false; G9 3.2's write order: no orphan contact can exist on the auth side). The answer of step 6 is then sent at once. The mails come after the answer, and the request does not wait for them: the route starts them as one background task, which catches and logs every error; the module keeps the task in a set until it ends, and `settle()` resolves once every such task has ended (the tests await it). In that task, when an item entered lane 0, the lane-0 alert of 7.12 runs first; then the receipt is sent (`mailNotifier` or, for a signed-in illegal-ground notice, `mailAccount`) and its result recorded in `receipt`. A relay failure, or a restart before the send, leaves the receipt `pending` for the dispatch job (7.8 step 1, after 15 minutes), `refused` is recorded `failed`, and `forgotten` is recorded `no_address`. So a stalled relay never costs a notifier the reference and token of step 6, which on the CSAM path are the only way to follow the notice.
6. **The answer** (201), sent before the mails of step 5: `{ ref, token }`, the token only for a signed-out or CSAM notice. The app shows both once and never again (section 9); the token opens the status page and re-examination; it cannot confirm or stop the address, which only the mail token of 7.2 can.

A moderator's own finding: `POST /api/staff/findings` (body `FindingRequest`, section 10; behind `requireStaff`, one of the three named exceptions to the step-up rule) runs the same transaction with an intake of kind `finding`, channel `staff`, `finding_by` the staff account, the rule and category chosen by the moderator, no explanation, cited law or good-faith statement and no receipt, and answers the case's id. Before the findings count and the transaction, the route resolves its target with `liveTarget`; a target with nothing live is refused with `not_found` (404), and nothing is written or counted (for a finding citing R2 a target is live while its row exists, an account also under a handle it has left, step 3). Before the transaction, the route counts the person's findings since midnight UTC (`intakes` rows of kind `finding` whose `finding_by` is the person and whose `received_at` is at or after the day's start); at `moderation_limits.findings_per_day` (50 by default) it refuses with `rate_limited` (429, Retry-After the seconds to midnight UTC) and writes nothing. Two concurrent findings may both pass at the limit, as two case reads may (7.3). A finding citing R2 on a post that quotes another gets the added item of step 3 like a notice: the answer carries the case of the post the finder named, the case page links the other (section 8), and the finder is `finding_by` for both cases, so `reviewer_conflict` holds in both (6.3). A finding that puts an item into lane 0 sends the lane-0 alert like a notice (7.12). A finding with its added item is one finding against the daily limit. It decides nothing.

### 7.2 The notifier's status page, contact and re-examination

- `POST /api/notices/status { ref, token }`: the intake whose `ref` matches and whose `lookup_hash` is the token's sha256 (compared in constant time), or whose contact's mail token the token is (`notifierMailTokenMatches`); `notice_not_found`, 404, for anything else; at most 30 an hour per network. It answers the received time and each item with its korbat link (`link`: for a post, built from its id; for an account, built from the item's `named_handle`, the handle as the notice named it, and never from the handle the profile holds now, so that on no ground does a report show its sender a later handle, which is 1b's reason for giving no redirect from an old handle: a harasser must not be able to follow a rename), its `scope` (section 10: `quote` when its post's `quote_of_id` is set; for an account, `account_suspect` when the item is suspect or, once its outcome is released, when its answering decision holds `seal_media`, and `account` otherwise; null for any other suspect item, also one whose post quotes another), whether it is suspect (`suspect`), for an item korbat added for a quoted post the position of the item it was added for (`quotedBy`), its `shown_outcome` and the day of the decision behind it, once its `outcome_sent_at` is set, and then, as `sealed`, whether that decision sealed images, and, as `unseen`, whether that decision's `unseen` is true (`decisions.unseen`, 6.2 step 6) while this item, about a post, is not suspect (section 10, `UNSEEN_NO_ACTION`; neither earlier, so the page and the mail tell the same at the same time), whether the outcome was changed on review or by a correction (`revised_sent_at` set) or answers a re-examination, and whether re-examination is open and until when (7.7's rule, which the trigger of 6.1 enforces: an item a moderator answered `no_violation` or `not_evident`, not re-examined, before `appeal_deadline(outcome_sent_at)`), and, for a contact, `given` (an address was given) and, only when the token was the contact's mail token, `confirmed` and `stopped` from `notifierFacts`. With the notice token both are null, so the answer to whoever typed an address never depends on what korbat knows about that address. Items are listed by position, an added item directly after its origin. A signed-in reporter's own reports are read in the app's list instead (7.6).
- `POST /api/notices/contact { ref, token, choice }`: only the contact's mail token is accepted; a notice token, a notice without a contact, or `confirmNotifier` or `stopNotifier` answering false gives `notice_not_found` (404). It shares the status route's limit. `confirm` records the address as confirmed for 30 days and lifts any suppression of it; `stop` suppresses it until a `confirm` with a mail token to that address, or the account's resume of 7.6, lifts it. Both are POSTs from buttons on the status page, never a link that acts on a GET (T42, W52).
- **Re-examination.** `POST /api/notices/reexamine { ref, token, position, statement? }` (either token, as for the status) and, for a signed-in reporter, `POST /api/moderation/items/:item/reexamine { statement? }` (behind `requireSession`; the item must be the viewer's own). The statement is optional, at most 2000 characters under the text rules, with no formal requirement; the server makes its salt and digest. At most five re-examinations a day per account or network (the shared limit store, as step 2 of 7.1); a request that re-examines two items (below) is one request and counts once. The route runs 7.1 step 4's transaction for the one target, with an item of the same intake whose `reexamines_item_id` is the item: liveness by `liveTarget`, the open case or a new one, a snapshot when the case has none, holds; the attach trigger checks, under the intake's lock and against `clock_timestamp()`, that the item may be re-examined now and copies its target, position and lane (6.1). It refuses `reexamination_not_open` (422) and `reexamination_exists` (409). A re-examination on the CSAM path is filed with the notice's token and, like its intake, carries no account or contact; it keeps the original's lane, is suspect like its intake and so is answered by an admin (6.2 step 6), and adds no item. A re-examination in lane 0 sends `moderation_urgent` like any lane-0 item (7.12). After it commits, the route calls `forgetNotifierAfter([contact], appealDeadline(now))`; a failure is logged, and the next daily dispatch moves the date anyway (7.8 step 3(b)). `position` is 1 to 20: an item korbat added for a quoted post is re-examined like any other, by its own position with its notice's token. A re-examination of a suspect item whose intake holds an added item on the post it quotes re-examines both in the one transaction when that added item is open to re-examination too (answered `no_violation` or `not_evident`, released, inside its window, not yet re-examined), its two items inserted in target order; `intake_items_quote` requires it (6.1). When the added item is unanswered, or its images are sealed, nothing more is needed: the quoted post is frozen. Should the daily release open the added item between the route's read and its commit, the commit fails with `quote_item_missing` and the route runs the transaction once more. It answers 201. The re-examination is then decided and told like any item: its outcome reaches the notifier at the daily release, and a restriction reaches the author like any restriction. A re-examination cannot itself be re-examined, with one exception: an item that reads `UNSEEN_NO_ACTION` (section 10: an item about a post that is not suspect, answered without action by a decision whose `unseen` is true) may be re-examined like any no-action item, also when it is itself a re-examination, since nobody could open the images when it was decided (6.1 `intake_items_attach`). Such an answer does not spend the re-examination; the daily limit stays. A re-examination shares its position with the item it re-examines, so with the notice's token the route takes the newest item at the named position.

### 7.3 The queue and the case, for staff

Every route below is behind the staff gate and `requireStaff` (3a section 7), in the staff module's route group, and reads through `ModerationForStaff`. Every route below that names a case (`/cases/:case` and its media, snapshots and texts) or an item (the reveal) first refuses with `staff_recused` (403) when `staff_case_conflict(person, case, false)` is true; a refused read writes no access row and does not count towards the daily case limit.

- `GET /api/staff/overview`: per lane the open cases and the oldest unanswered item's time; the appeals waiting, those this person may review (7.7's mark), and those no staff account may review now (`appeal_reviewer_state` not `available`); the identity reveals of the last 30 days, newest first and at most 100, with their total, each with its time, the staff handle and the case (`case_access_log` rows of kind `identity_revealed`); the decisions of the last 30 days for which 7.5 step 4 sends `moderation_csam_lifted`, read from `decisions` and `decision_actions` (a decision that holds `unseal_media`, or a `final` decision whose `suspect` is true and that holds no `seal_media`), newest first and at most 100, with their total, each with its time, the staff handle, the case and what it did (`unsealed` or `dismissed`); the choices not to seal of the last 30 days (`staff_texts` of kind `seal_declined`), the same way, each with its time, the staff handle and the case; the seals taken while deciding that are in force (`measures_in_force`), each with its time, the staff handle, the case and the posts and images counted from `seal_posts`; per staff handle the images viewed today and the findings opened today (UTC); the number of receipts, author mails and outcome mails `pending` for more than an hour (counted from `received_at`, `decided_at` and `coalesce(revised_sent_at, outcome_sent_at)`; counts only), and the same three counts of mails recorded `failed` whose due time (7.8 step 6) falls in the last 30 days; `releaseOverdue`, the number of items with an outcome and no `outcome_sent_at` whose answering decision's `decided_at` (for an item answered at intake, its `created_at`) is more than 26 hours old, plus the items whose `revision_pending_at` is more than 26 hours old (the daily release takes at most about 24 hours, so any of these means it is not finishing); `erasureOverdue`, one count per due rule of 6.4 of what a step of the retention job (7.9) or the auth janitor should have changed more than a day ago and has not: `caseEvidence` (candidate cases of 7.9, those holding a row `erase_case_evidence` would change, whose `case_evidence_due_at` is more than a day past; a case holding only snapshots of a frozen post is never counted), `intakeRecords` (unerased intakes whose `intake_due_at` is more than a day past), `statements` (decisions whose statement texts are due more than a day ago), `redactions` (decisions that `redact_r4_body` would change now, so none whose post is frozen, whose `decision_final_at` is more than a day past), `images` (`MediaForCases.overdueDeletions()`, since the moderation module never names `post_media`), `drafts` (unconfirmed texts other than notes and `seal_declined` texts made more than 48 hours ago), `accessLog` (access rows more than 12 months and a day old) and `contacts` (`AuthForModeration.overdueContacts()`, since the moderation module cannot read the auth schema); and whether the emergency switch is on. The retention job and the auth janitor each run every 10 minutes, so any count above zero means a step keeps failing, or the retention job is off (`MODERATION_RETENTION`, 7.9).
- `GET /api/staff/queue?cursor=`: open cases ordered by `lane`, then (only with the emergency switch on) whether the case is deferred (in lanes 1 and 2, every unanswered item comes from a signed-out intake or an account under 48 hours old; in lane 0, every unanswered item comes from a signed-out intake), then `oldest_open_at`, then `id`; 50 a page; each row with its lane, target kind, opened time, the oldest unanswered item's time, the count of unanswered items, the rules and categories they cite, whether any alleges illegality, is suspect or is a re-examination, and whether it is deferred. The cursor is the last row's sort key. The console's queue refreshes without the activity mark (3a section 9).
- `GET /api/staff/cases/:case`: the case (target, lane, versions, opened and closed times); the live target (the post's state and `moderation_level`; or the account's live handle, display name and bio); for a case that holds a suspect item or a `seal_media`, `suspicion`: `open` (a suspect item of the case is unanswered), `reach` (`MediaForCases.reach(target)`, section 5, without its `newestServedAt`) and `earlier` (`{ target: { count, lastOn, imagesOpenedOn }, author: { count, lastOn } }`: the final decisions of the last `SUSPECT_EARLIER_MONTHS` months that answered a suspect item without a seal, about this target and about its author's profile or any of its posts; and `imagesOpenedOn`, what records that staff looked: for a post target, the day an image of the post was last served to staff by the console's image route, read from the `image_viewed` rows of `case_access_log` on any case about the post, or null; null for an account target, whose images the console never shows; counts and days only); the snapshots, newest first, each with its salt and digest and its content unless `snapshot_withheld` (6.2) is true, then with `withheld` and no content; the items with their grounds, categories, cited law, explanations, re-examination statements and the image each names (`media_id`, the reporter's hint, or null), with `suspect`, for an item korbat added for a quoted post `quotedByPosition`, and for an item whose post quotes another `quotedCaseId` (the case of its added item), each source as a pseudonymous summary computed on read: signed in, "Reporter A", its letter given per case in the order of each reporter's first item in that case, so one person's letter on one case says nothing about another, then four bands computed over the reporter's rows kept, never exact counts: the account's age (`under_1_month`, `1_to_12_months` or `over_12_months`), the items it filed, this one included (`1`, `2_to_9` or `10_or_more`), and the share of its decided items that were acted on (`none_decided`, `none`, `under_half` or `half_or_more`; exactly half is `half_or_more`), where an item is decided once its `shown_outcome` is set and acted on when that is `restricted_rule`, `restricted_legal` or `partly_reversed`, except that an item that reads `UNSEEN_NO_ACTION` (section 10) is not among the decided ones: such items are counted apart, as "answered without the images", in the fourth band (`none`, `1`, `2_to_9` or `10_or_more`), never as decided without action; signed out, named or not, contact given, confirmed and suppressed, from `notifierFacts`, or "contact lost or forgotten" when the intake has a `contact_id` for which `notifierFacts` answers `found` false; CSAM, anonymous; staff, "found by a moderator" (G9 3.9). An item whose reporter link is null (erased with its intake record, or forgotten after the reporter's deletion, 6.4) is summarised as a signed-in reporter no longer linked. A re-examination item says which item it re-examines and, when the viewing person decided that item, carries `reexaminesYourDecision` (7.7). The answer also carries the decisions with their actions (each measure with `no_effect` and whether it is in force; a `seal_media` with `sealed`: the posts and images it lists, how many of the posts are deleted, and at most `SEAL_LIST_MAX` rows `{ postId, createdAt, images }` of `seal_posts`, oldest first, as text for the report to the police, never content) and texts, the appeals with their statements, the person's own unconfirmed texts, the notes (`notes`), the recorded choices not to seal (`sealsDeclined`: the case's `seal_declined` texts, each with its reason, its staff handle and its time, listed as the notes are, so that the admin reads why nobody sealed; they are erased with the case's evidence, 6.4), the history (`case_history`), and `imagesAllowed`: for a post case, `not post_frozen(post)` (gate V; 6.1, the freeze); for an account case, false, since a profile holds no image and the images of its posts are shown only on those posts' own cases. Images are judged by the material: a closed case never shows what an unanswered suspect item or a seal freezes. Every read appends a `case_opened` row to `case_access_log`. A person who has opened `moderation_limits.case_opens_per_day` different cases (400 by default) since midnight UTC is refused the opening of any other case that day (`rate_limited`, 429): the count is `count(distinct case_id)` of the person's `case_opened` rows since midnight UTC, and a case they already opened that day is never refused, so a reload or a decision's answer never meets the limit. Every staff account, the person's own included, gets `moderation_reads` once that day (the key `moderation-reads:<staff account id>`, whose window ends at midnight UTC; C22).
- **Images in the console.** `GET /api/staff/cases/:case/media/:media/:size` (`size` `small` or `large`) answers only an image of the case's post or of one of its snapshots, never when the image is sealed, its post is frozen at the request, or its own or its post's `erased_at` is set, on any case, open or closed (`image_refused`, 404; gate V, 6.1). `MediaForCases.staffImage`, the only function that opens an image file for staff, reads the row, its state, both `erased_at` and `post_frozen(post_id)` in one query and then opens the store's file with the media module's checks (`apps/api/src/media/files.ts`), reads its first 30 bytes and refuses a file that is not a RIFF/WEBP container with a `VP8 `, `VP8L` or `VP8X` chunk, whose longer side exceeds the variant's edge (2048 or 1024, `packages/media-protocol/src/limits.ts:23-26`), or whose large variant's size differs from the row's `width` and `height`; a refusal is logged as a store fault (T40). The answer carries `Content-Type: image/webp`, `Cache-Control: private, no-store`, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` and the API's own CSP and CORP (`apps/api/src/middleware/security-headers.ts`); each view appends an `image_viewed` access row. The console requests an image only when its person reveals it, the small size first, and draws it blurred and grey by a stylesheet class until a second click (C4; the classes are plain CSS under `style-src 'self'`, `research-3/console-lab.md`). The request carries no activity mark and counts against the global limit of 300 requests a minute (`apps/api/src/app.ts:65`), which a case's at most four images per snapshot never approach.
- **Revealing a source.** `POST /api/staff/items/:item/reveal`, behind `requireStaff` and `requireStaffStepUp("identity_revealed", (c) => c.req.param("item"))`, with `IdentityRevealedParams` (section 10). Before anything is written, `ModerationForStaff.reveal` refuses: `not_found` (404) when the item does not exist or has no case; `staff_recused` (403) when `staff_case_conflict(person, the item's case, false)` is true; `reveal_refused` (422) when the item's intake is a CSAM notice or a finding, or names nobody (no reporter link, never given, erased with the intake record or forgotten after the reporter's deletion, 6.4; and for a signed-out intake `notifierFacts` answering `named` false); `text_not_found` (409) unless `reasonDigest` is the digest of an unerased, unconfirmed `reveal_reason` text of the item's case, written by this person, that no `case_access_log` row names yet; `rate_limited` (429, Retry-After the seconds to midnight UTC) when the person's `identity_revealed` rows since midnight UTC number `moderation_limits.reveals_per_day` (10 by default) or more, read before the transaction, as the findings route reads its count, so two concurrent reveals may both pass at the limit. Then one transaction on the API pool: `boundLockWaits(tx, confirmed.expiresAt, 3)` (the account's row, the key's row and the audit head); the `case_access_log` row (kind `identity_revealed`, the case, the item, the text's id); last, `select public.append_confirmed_audit(...)` with action `identity_revealed`, target `intake_item` and the item's id, and `metadata: { params }`, which also confirms the reason text (6.2); then COMMIT. The identity is read only after the commit: the reporter's handle from `profiles` for a signed-in intake, or `AuthForModeration.notifierName(contact_id)` for a signed-out one. After the commit, `moderation_reveal` goes through `mailStaff` to every staff account, the person's own included, with the person's handle, the reveal's time, the number of their reveals since midnight UTC (this one included) and the limit; it is best effort and has no throttle key (7.12): a failed send is logged and not repeated, since the home screen lists every reveal. The answer is `RevealResponse`; both members are null when the name was forgotten in between, and the console then says so. `staff_confirmation_ended`, `staff_confirmation_late`, a lock timeout and a deadlock answer as in 7.5.
- `POST /api/staff/cases/:case/snapshots` (a named exception): takes a fresh snapshot of the target as it stands (a tombstone for a deleted post; without content after an erasure), in the intake's way, when it differs from the newest or the newest is withheld or erased. It answers `StaffSnapshotTaken { id, digest }` (the new snapshot's, or the newest's when nothing differed), never its content. The console then reads the case again, whose answer carries the snapshot, so every read of a case's content goes through that route's access row and daily limit. The moderator uses it before deciding on a profile that changed since the report; a decision on stale fields is refused anyway (`profile_changed`).

### 7.4 Saving a text

`POST /api/staff/cases/:case/texts { kind, text, salt, digest }` (a named exception): the console normalises the text if it wishes (trim, NFC), makes a 32-byte salt with `crypto.getRandomValues` and computes the digest with WebCrypto through the contracts' `textDigest`. The server never changes the text: it must be a well-formed string (`isWellFormed`), with no NUL and no C0 control but the line feed, no invisible direction or zero-width character (`text.ts:6`), at most 5000 UTF-16 units (`text_invalid`, 422). For every kind but `note` and `seal_declined` it is refused (`text_quotes_user`, 422) when, both lower-cased with every run of whitespace made one space, any 32-character run of an explanation, a cited law, a re-examination statement or an appeal statement of someone else on this case appears in it (C19's overlap check; staff paraphrase, never quote). The server recomputes the digest and refuses (`text_invalid`) one that differs from the request's `digest`, stores the row (`staff_texts`; the CHECK holds digest, salt and text together) and answers `{ id, digest }`. A note is complete when saved, and so is a `seal_declined` text, whose text is its reason and may not be empty (7.12); every other kind waits for the decision or the reveal that names it and is deleted after 24 hours if none does. `GET /api/staff/cases/:case/texts` answers the person's own unconfirmed texts with their salts, so a draft survives a reload or a sign-in (3a section 9's promise, which G1 exceeds); the console recomputes each digest and drops one that does not match. Saves are limited to 60 a minute per staff account.

### 7.5 Deciding

In the console (section 8): the moderator chooses the outcome, the ground, the rule or the legal reference, the category and the measures or reversals, writes the facts and the reason (saved as texts, 7.4), and reads the preview, the statement exactly as the author will receive it (`statementParagraphs`). Then:

1. The console reads `GET /api/staff/me` with the activity mark for the session's handle (3a section 15), builds the action `{ action: "case_decided", target: { type: "case", id }, params }` from what it shows (the case's `state_version`; as `throughSeq` for a final decision the newest item's `seq`, or, on a case that holds an unanswered suspect item, the `seq` of the newest unanswered suspect item and not of the newest item, so that the items of another ground that arrived after it stay open and are judged with the images once the freeze has ended (6.1, the freeze; no trigger differs for it: 6.2 step 2 accepts any `through_seq` up to `intake_seq`, and step 6 asks only that no suspect item lies above it); the snapshot's id and the digest it computed itself from the snapshot it displayed; in a case whose newest snapshot is withheld or erased, the console first takes a fresh snapshot (7.3), reads the case again to show it, and names that one, never a snapshot it was not shown; the digests it computed for the texts; the sorted actions), asks `POST /api/staff/step-up/options` for options, checks them with `checkStepUpOptions` (unchanged), asks the key, and sends `POST /api/staff/cases/:case/decisions` with the params and `stepUp`.
2. `requireStaff`, then `requireStaffStepUp("case_decided", (c) => c.req.param("case"))` (unchanged) verifies the confirmation over exactly these params and leaves `staffStepUp` on the context.
3. Before the transaction, the route refuses (`invalid_decision`, 422) a `redressVersion` other than the API's `REDRESS_VERSION`, and, for an `interim` or `final` decision, a `rulesVersion` other than its `RULES_VERSION`. `ModerationForStaff.decide(session, confirmed, ctx)` then runs one transaction on the API pool:
   1. `boundLockWaits(tx, confirmed.expiresAt, 15)`: `lock_timeout` the lesser of 15 seconds and a fifteenth of the challenge's remaining life by the database's clock; nothing written when under a millisecond is left (`challenge_expired`, 400). The divisor is the transaction's count of waits: the subject's profile, the case, the deciding account's `auth.users` row, the cap's advisory lock, up to three staff texts, the subject's `auth.users` row, the post, up to four images, the key and the audit head. It only aims to end the transaction before the expiry: the guarantee that nothing commits late is `append_confirmed_audit`'s own check, so a wait the count misses can only cost a retry or turn the answer into `challenge_expired`. A decision that holds `seal_media` or `unseal_media`, or that ends a seal taken while deciding, also locks every image row its seals list. That number has no bound, so for such a decision the divisor does not count the waits. A transaction that outlasts the challenge is rolled back whole by `append_confirmed_audit` and answered `challenge_expired`.
   2. The subject account's profile row `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` (`lockAccounts`), the first lock of the transaction (section 2, Lock order); for a correction or a review, the subject of the reviewed decision.
   3. The `decisions` row, from the confirmed params and `decided_by` = the session's account; its triggers lock the case, check the version, the range, the snapshot, the texts, the parties, the role and suspicion, the caps and the ground, store whether the decision is `unseen` (a final decision on a post case while the post is frozen), answer the items, move the case and confirm the texts (6.2).
   4. One `decision_actions` row per measure or reversal, in the params' order; their triggers check each, force its target and effect, and derive the post's and images' state (6.2). `MediaForCases` is not called.
   5. Last, `select public.append_confirmed_audit(...)` with the session's account, the key, `case_decided`, target `case` and the case's id, `metadata: { params, decisionId }`, the session's handle, the action's hash, the assertion, the request id and the challenge's expiry in milliseconds.
   6. COMMIT, at which the deferred `decisions_check` runs: the audit link and the decision's consistency.
   A 40P01 (deadlock) or a 55P03 on a wait the bound cut short retries the transaction once while more than a minute of the challenge is left, with the same confirmation (nothing was written, and the challenge was spent in Valkey, so no other request can use it); the retry runs the whole transaction again from step 1. `decide` answers `ended` for `staff_confirmation_ended`, and `late` for `staff_confirmation_late` and for a 40P01 or 55P03 it does not retry (the second one, or one met with a minute or less of the challenge left), as `revokeConfirmedStaffSession` answers its outcomes; the staff route group (`apps/api/src/staff/cases.ts`) throws `new AuthError("staff_session_required", 401)` for `ended` and `new AuthError("challenge_expired", 400)` for `late`, `AuthError` imported from `../auth` as `apps/api/src/staff/routes.ts:4` imports from it, exactly as `revokeStaffSession` does (`apps/api/src/auth/staff/step-up.ts:206-208`), because `requireStaff` clears both cookies only for an `AuthError` with that code (`apps/api/src/auth/staff/middleware.ts:44`); the review route (7.7) and the reveal route do the same; every trigger code answers its `ModerationError` (section 10).
4. **After the commit**, outside the transaction: the author's statement is mailed (`mailAccount(subject, moderation_decision)`) when `author_mail` is `pending`, and the result recorded (`sent`, `no_address` for a purged account, `failed` for `refused`; a relay failure stays `pending` for the dispatch job); the person's decisions in the last 15 minutes are counted in Valkey, and at 20 every staff account, the person's own included, gets `moderation_burst`, at most once an hour per person (the key `moderation-burst:<staff account id>` with a one-hour window, throttled as in 7.12). When the committed decision holds `unseal_media`, or is `final`, has `suspect` true and holds no `seal_media` (it lifts a seal, or ends a freeze unseen), `moderation_csam_lifted` goes through `mailStaff` to every staff account, the person's own included. It carries the person's handle, the decision's time and what it did (`unsealed` when it holds `unseal_media`, else `dismissed`), read from the committed decision and its actions. It is best effort and has no throttle key (7.12): a failed send is logged and not repeated, since the home screen lists every such decision for 30 days (section 8). The answer is 201 with the case as 7.3 reads it (`CaseView`), so the console shows what now stands.

What the record holds for a decision: one `case_decided` row with the staff session's handle, the key, the action's hash, the assertion, and `metadata.params` of ids, enums, integers and digests only, plus `metadata.decisionId`. `audit-verify` checks it as any 3a step-up row (V1 to V7, G1 2). What it never holds: a text, a snapshot's content, a reporter's or notifier's id (they are not in the params, and items are named only through `throughSeq`).

### 7.6 What the author and the reporter see

- `GET /api/moderation/cases` (signed in): the decisions whose subject is the viewer's account and whose `author_mail` is not `none` (every decision the author is told of, section 2, row Decisions and statements), newest first, each with its kind, decision day (never the case's opening, T47), for a decision about a post the post's creation time, measures, rule or "the law", and appeal state. Other decisions are not listed.
- `GET /api/moderation/decisions/:id` (404 unless the viewer is the decision's subject and its `author_mail` is not `none`, so no decision the author was not told of opens by its id): the statement's data (section 10, `DecisionView`), rendered by the app with `statementParagraphs`; the content actioned, from the decision's snapshot unless `snapshot_withheld` is true, under the post's creation time, which every post decision's screen shows ("Your post of {createdAt}"); a sealed image as "Sealed: not shown to anyone, you included", beside the post's text, which is never sealed; "Content erased when the R4 decision became final" for any decision whose snapshot an R4 redaction erased (a decision about the same post or account has `redacted_at` set), whichever decision is viewed; for any other decision whose snapshot is erased (by `erase_case_evidence`), "korbat's copy of the content this decision was made on was erased on {contentErasedOn} under its retention rules (Terms, Your data).", followed, for a decision about a post that its author has not deleted and whose `erased_at` is null, by "The post itself stays in your profile while you keep it."; for a post the author deleted, the snapshot's text under "You deleted this post. korbat keeps this copy for the case for the periods in its terms." and its images as "Images kept for the case, not shown" (the image route serves nothing of a deleted post, `serve.ts:29-35`, and 7.10 keeps it so); for a field reset, the value the snapshot held, which is the value the reset removed (its compare-and-set, 6.2), readable while the case's copies are kept and at least 30 days after a reset that stops before they are erased (6.4), unless an R4 redaction erased it first: an R4 redaction of the account erases it at that restriction's finality (6.4), and the screen then says so; other images served by the app's own image route, which serves a withheld or removed image to its author (`serve.ts:31-35`) and never a sealed or erased one; and the appeal's state, its deadline, and the review's statement when answered.
- `GET /api/moderation/reports?cursor=` (signed in): the viewer's own intakes, while their intake record is kept (6.6), and their items, each with its link, `scope`, `sealed` and `unseen` as 7.2 gives them (an account's link under the handle the report named, never a later one), its `shown_outcome` and decision day once released, whether it was changed or answers a re-examination, and whether re-examination is open and until when, as the status page shows them (7.2), in the same words as the mails (C16). Every page carries `reportMailStopped` from `accountReportMailStopped`.
- `POST /api/moderation/report-mail/resume` (signed in, behind `requireSession`): `AuthForModeration.resumeAccountReportMail` for the session's account; answers 204 whether or not a suppression existed.
- The post chip: `PostView` gains `moderation` on the viewer's own withheld or removed posts only (`{ decisionId, ruleId, legal, interim }`, from `ModerationForSocial.authorFacts`, section 5), which the app shows as "Under review: R4" or "Removed: R3" linking to the statement. A post whose images are sealed keeps its place and its text; it has a chip only when the post itself is withheld or removed, and the decision that sealed its images is under Settings, Cases.

### 7.7 Appeals, reviews, re-examinations and corrections

- **Filing an appeal.** The author: `POST /api/moderation/decisions/:id/appeal { statement? }` (behind `requireSession`). The statement is optional, at most 2000 characters under the text rules, with no formal requirement (recital 58); the server makes its salt and digest. One transaction inserts the `appeals` row, whose trigger (6.3) decides whether the appeal is open now; the unique index refuses a second (`appeal_exists`, 409); `appeal_not_open` (422) otherwise. Five appeals a day per account. An interim decision cannot be appealed: its statement says that its measures end with the case's decision, which can be appealed if it restricts (section 10).
- **Waiting.** The console lists the pending appeals (`GET /api/staff/appeals`), each marked whether this person may review it: not if they decided anything in the case (read from `decisions`) or opened a finding in it (read from `intakes.finding_by`), nor when `staff_case_conflict(person, case, true)` is true, nor, when `review_needs_admin` of the appealed decision is true, when the person is not an admin. The list carries no statement: the reviewer reads it on the case page, whose read 7.3 logs and counts. With no eligible reviewer, the appeal waits (T60), and `appeal_reviewer_state` says which case applies; the console's home counts the pending appeals whose state is not `available`. The author's screen then says:
  - for `none_single_staff`, "Your appeal waits for a moderator who did not take part in the decision. korbat has one moderator at the moment.";
  - for `none_eligible`, "Your appeal waits for a moderator who took no part in this case. None is available now; your appeal stays open until one is." This text gives no reason, because a moderator may be ineligible through a relation to someone in the case (6.2);
  - for an appeal whose review needs an admin (`needsAdmin`, section 10: the decision cites R2 or sealed images), "Your appeal waits for an admin who did not take part in the decision.", followed by "korbat has one admin at the moment." while korbat has one admin (`none_single_staff`, counted over admins, 6.3);
  - for an appeal of a decision that sealed images, the seal's sentence (Reviewing, below) instead.
- **Reviewing.** `POST /api/staff/decisions/:decision/review` with the step-up action `appeals_reviewed` (target `decision`; params: the case's `stateVersion`, the `appealId` of the decision's one pending appeal, the snapshot's id and the reviewer's digest of it (a fresh snapshot when the newest is withheld or erased, as in 7.5), the `outcome` (`kept`, `partly_reversed` or `reversed`), the reviewed decision's `category` and `rulesVersion`, the `redressVersion`, the sorted reversals, and the `reasonDigest` of a `review_reason` text). The route makes 7.5's redress check, refuses `case_changed` (409) before the insert when the decision's pending appeal is not `appealId`, and runs 7.5's transaction with a `decisions` row of kind `review`; its trigger forces and compares the appeal again under the case lock (6.3), and the audit link compares `appealId` at commit (6.2). The trigger refuses a reviewer who decided in the case or opened a finding in it, and a party. Reversals are new rows that name what they reverse; the post or image shows again only if nothing else holds it, and a field is not written back (row No moderation state without a decision). A seal for good is lifted (`unseal_media`; 6.2, the seal) only after the authority korbat reported the images to has answered that they are not child sexual abuse material, by an admin whose review reason says that the authority has answered and gives the date of its answer; the reason never holds the authority's reference or anything that names a person (the reference is kept only in the operator's R2 records, off the server); until that answer the appeal waits, and the author's screen says "Sealed images are reviewed once the authorities have assessed them. Your appeal stays open until then." An appeal of a restriction under R5 is reviewed by anyone eligible; a review needs an admin when `review_needs_admin` says so (the reviewed decision cites R2 or holds a `seal_media`). A review, like a correction, that turns on an image of a post that is frozen is decided on text alone or waits until an admin has answered the suspect items (6.1, the freeze): the case page shows no image of such a post. After the commit the review route does what 7.5 step 4 does, `moderation_csam_lifted` included.
- **Re-examinations, for staff.** A re-examination is an item of its case, in the queue like any other, decided by a final decision that answers it (7.5). When the viewing person decided the item it re-examines, the case page marks it ("You decided the item this re-examines. Leave it to another staff member when there is one.") and the README asks another staff member to decide it when one exists. The database does not refuse that person (section 2, row Appeals and re-examination). An item on another ground about a post that a decision whose `unseen` is true answered without action, and so without the images (an admin's answer to a suspect item of its case, or a decision taken on text alone while the post was frozen through its author's account's case or its images were sealed), can be re-examined, also when it is itself a re-examination (7.2; section 10, `UNSEEN_NO_ACTION`): the re-examination is decided with the images once the freeze has ended (6.1).
- **Corrections.** A moderator may reverse a measure of a final restriction, of their own or another's, without an appeal: a `case_decided` decision of kind `correction` (its params name the corrected decision), reversals only, by anyone eligible but not a party. It needs an admin when the corrected decision needed one, and when it lifts a seal (6.2); the reason of a correction that lifts a seal for good follows the rule of a review's (Reviewing, above: that the authority has answered and when, never its reference). A correction of an **interim** decision lifts a measure taken while deciding, early, by anyone eligible, without an admin (P5 law condition 1) unless the measure is a seal, which only an admin lifts, and only when it was taken in error (6.2, the seal); the author is told at once (`ended_interim`). A correction that reverses everything answers the corrected decision's pending appeal as `reversed`; one that reverses part leaves it for a review of what remains.
- **Telling.** The author gets the statement of every review (it answers their own appeal, whatever its result) and of every correction, in the app and by `moderation_decision`. When a review or a correction reverses a measure of a final decision, every notifier and reporter whose item that decision answered gets the item's new outcome at the next daily release (`release_outcomes`, 6.2): in `notice_update` or the app's list, and on the status page (7.8). For them that outcome is final within korbat, and the line names the courts and the Digital Services Coordinator. The review's reason reaches only the author: a notifier or reporter gets the item's outcome with its rule or "the law", never a staff text. A notifier's or reporter's item stays released until every restriction it led to is final for its author (6.4 `intake_due_at`), so a reversal on the author's appeal reaches a signed-in reporter too. A correction made after the intake record was erased reaches signed-out notifiers on the status page, and by mail while korbat still keeps their contact; it reaches no signed-in reporter, whose link is gone (6.6): their line records `no_address`, and their list no longer shows the report. Flagged for counsel: a review or a correction may reverse a rule-ground restriction that answered a notice of illegality; unless another measure holds it, the content is then public again without a decision on the law. The notifier is told the new outcome and may send a new notice, which is decided on its merits.

### 7.8 Delivering statements and outcomes: the dispatch job

`moderation-dispatch` (`apps/api/src/moderation/dispatch.ts`), built with `createJob` (`apps/api/src/lib/jobs.ts`) under the lock `job:moderation-dispatch`, runs every 10 minutes. Each round runs step 5 first, then step 3, then steps 1, 2, 4 and 6 in that order, so the daily release, which writes only to the databases, never waits behind the mails of steps 1 and 2. Each step runs in a try of its own, and so does each mail of steps 1, 2 and 4: an error other than `MailUnavailable` (a database error, or a ciphertext that no configured key opens, `apps/api/src/auth/crypto.ts:64`) is logged with the step and the row's id and leaves the row as it was, and the round goes on, so a row that keeps failing never stops the alerts, the daily release or another row's mail; it stays pending until step 6 gives it up. Every delivery state, in this job and after a commit (7.1 step 5, 7.5 step 4), is written by an `UPDATE` that also requires the row to still hold `pending` (a carried receipt: `pending` or `folded`), so a late answer never overwrites one already recorded: the later writer waits for the first one's commit and changes nothing (measured on PostgreSQL 18.6).

1. Receipts still `pending` whose intake's `received_at` is more than 15 minutes old, oldest first, 100 a round: mailed (`mailNotifier`, or `mailAccount` for a signed-in illegal-ground notice), and their state recorded (`failed` for `refused`, `no_address` for `forgotten`). A relay failure leaves them `pending` for the next round.
2. Decisions whose `author_mail` is `pending` and whose `decided_at` is more than 15 minutes old: mailed as after a commit (7.5).
3. The daily release. A round's cut-off is the latest `MODERATION_DISPATCH_HOUR` (UTC) at or before the round's start. Until the cut-off's Valkey marker (`moderation-dispatched:<YYYY-MM-DDTHH>`, the cut-off's day and hour, kept 48 hours) is set, each round runs this step; because the key holds the hour, a changed `MODERATION_DISPATCH_HOUR` gives a new cut-off whose release runs, and a cut-off earlier than a release already made releases nothing new. The marker is set once (a) and (b) have both finished, so a round that fails during the step runs it again. The marker lives in Valkey, which a restore of the database does not bring back; the README's Restoring step 5 deletes the markers before the API starts again (Task 12(a)).
   - (a) `select public.release_outcomes(cutoff)` (6.2), in one transaction: every item answered before the cut-off is released with its outcome as of the cut-off, and every released item whose answering decision a review or correction changed before the cut-off gets its new outcome, so running it again never releases a decision made after the cut-off before the next cut-off (T47). From that moment the outcome shows on the status page and in the reporter's list, and a no-action item's re-examination window runs.
   - (b) `forgetNotifierAfter(contacts, appealDeadline(now))` for every contact whose notice is still owed something: an item unanswered (a re-examination included), an item's `outcome_mail` `pending` or `revision_pending_at` set, or its receipt `pending` or `folded` (a `failed` line or receipt is owed nothing more).
4. In every round whose cut-off's marker is set (so (b) has moved each contact's date before that day's lines go), the outcome mails: every item whose `outcome_sent_at` is set and whose `outcome_mail` is `pending`, whichever round set them. An item's line gives its `shown_outcome` with the rule or "the law" (section 10's phrases; an item answered by a decision that holds `seal_media` is worded with the `sealed` phrase of `SUSPECT_OUTCOME_PHRASES`, whatever its own ground, and an item about a post that a decision whose `unseen` is true answered `no_violation` or `not_evident` with `UNSEEN_NO_ACTION`), and, when it is `restricted_rule` or `restricted_legal` and every measure of its answering decision has `no_effect` set, the phrase that it was already not shown; for an item whose target is an account, `ACCOUNT_ITEM_SCOPE` follows the outcome, or `ACCOUNT_SUSPECT_SCOPE` in its place when the item's answering decision holds `seal_media`, and for an item whose target is a post whose `quote_of_id` is set, `QUOTE_ITEM_SCOPE` does, each after the already-not-shown phrase when that applies (section 10). "Final within korbat" is one rule, stated here, and the mail (7.15), the status page and the app's list (section 9) refer to it: the sentence goes with a line whose item cannot be re-examined (again) and that is a re-examination's answer or an outcome changed on review or by a correction. A line that reads `UNSEEN_NO_ACTION` is never such a line, since that answer leaves the re-examination unspent (7.2), also once it has been asked again; `NoticeUpdateLine.unseen` marks it (section 5), so that the mail prints the sentence for `changed`, and for `reexamination` without `unseen`.
   - Signed out, they go by contact to `mailNotifierUpdates`, which sends one `notice_update` per address.
   - Signed in (illegal ground), they go as one `notice_update` per account through `mailAccount`. An item whose reporter was forgotten records `no_address`.
   - A contact's receipt that the 24-hour cap folded rides in that update with its page link. In the round that sets the cut-off's marker (the daily round), a contact whose receipt is folded and who has nothing else is passed too, so its address gets an update that carries only receipts. In later rounds a contact's folded receipt is passed only with that contact's own retried lines, so an address gets at most one update a day; a receipt-only update the relay could not take waits for the next daily round.
   - Each line records its result in `outcome_mail`, and each carried receipt in `receipt` and `receipt_sent_at`: `sent`, `suppressed`, `no_address` (also for `forgotten`) or `failed` (for `refused`). Where the relay could not take a contact's or an account's mail (`unavailable`, which also answers a contact the auth module could not read, or `MailUnavailable` thrown), nothing is recorded, so the next round sends it again until step 6 gives it up.
   - The cap never holds back a `notice_update`. Rule-only reports and CSAM notices get no mail (their `outcome_mail` is `none`): their outcomes are in the app's list and on the status page.
5. Every round, the lane-0 repeat and the queue-age alert of 7.12.
6. Every round, the mails given up, recorded `failed` without another attempt: a receipt still `pending` or `folded` five days after its intake's `received_at`; a decision whose `author_mail` is still `pending` five days after its `decided_at`; and an item whose `outcome_mail` is still `pending` five days after `coalesce(revised_sent_at, outcome_sent_at)`. Each is logged as `moderation: mail given up` with its kind and id, never an address; the status page and the app's lists still show every outcome. Outcome lines are given up only in a round in which step 4 ran, after it, so each is tried at least once even when step 3 kept failing for more than five days.

### 7.9 Retention: the retention job

`moderation-retention` (`apps/api/src/moderation/retention.ts`), lock `job:moderation-retention`, runs these steps every 10 minutes, each in a transaction of its own on the API pool. It runs only while `MODERATION_RETENTION` (read at start, default `on`) is `on`. The README's Restoring step 5 turns it `off` for a restore window. Meanwhile the job erases, releases and deletes nothing, and the overview's `erasureOverdue` rises. Its candidates are read from indexed columns only (P8). A candidate case holds a row that `erase_case_evidence` would change (round 3's rule, r3-22). That is any of: an unerased snapshot that is not of a frozen post (`evidence_snapshots_unerased_idx`, with `post_frozen` read when `target_type` is `post`); an unerased note or unconfirmed text (`staff_texts_open_idx`); an appeal of one of its decisions whose statement is not null (`appeals_statement_idx`, with the decision read by its primary key); or an unreleased hold (`media_holds`' `(case_id) where released_at is null`). Candidate intakes are those whose `erased_at` is null (`intakes_unerased_idx`). So a round's work follows what is left, not every case ever closed. A case left holding only snapshots of a frozen post, which gate E keeps (6.1, the freeze), is not a candidate; it becomes one again when the freeze ends. A case whose snapshots `redact_r4_body` erased, and whose holds it released, before the case was due stays a candidate while its author's appeal statement is unerased.

- `sweep_staff_texts(now - 24 hours)`;
- for each candidate case whose `case_evidence_due_at` is at or before `now()`, `erase_case_evidence(case)`, which also releases the case's holds (it erases no snapshot of a frozen post and frees no image of one, 6.1);
- for each candidate intake whose `intake_due_at` is at or before `now()`, `erase_intake(intake)`;
- for each `final` decision citing R4 whose `redacted_at` is null, one of whose R4 measures is in force and whose `decision_final_at` is at or before `now()`, `redact_r4_body` (it answers false and changes nothing while the decision's post is frozen, 6.4);
- for each decision whose subject's profile was deleted at least 12 months ago and one of whose statement texts is unerased, `erase_statement_texts`;
- `delete_access_log(now - 12 months)`;
- `MediaForCases.dropDisposable(tx)`, the one deleter of images a case or a freeze kept (P3), in batches. Each call is a transaction of its own, ending with its own audit row. Its files are requested with `MediaForCases.requestDeletion` right after its commit and before the next call. The step calls it again until a call deletes fewer than `DISPOSAL_BATCH` rows. A `post_media` row is **disposable** when it is not retained (its `hold_until` null or past and its post not frozen; 6.1, the freeze) and its post is `deleted_by_author`, its owner is purged, its post's `erased_at` is set, or its own `erased_at` is set (an R4 `remove_media` made final). The candidate query applies the whole predicate, `post_frozen` included, so kept rows never fill a batch and stall the step; `post_media_keep` is what decides for each row (lab T9: a filter alone reads the statement's snapshot). It reads the post's state as `fileAccess` already joins `posts` (`apps/api/src/media/repo.ts:73-81`). G6 E6: a held row outlives the deletion that would have taken it, so something must take it when the hold ends.

A step (for the disposal, each batch) that changed anything ends with one `moderation_retention` audit row, written as the system actor through `appendAuditRow` as its transaction's last statement (`apps/api/src/staff/audit.ts:38-42`), with metadata `{ step, caseId, intakeId, decisionId, count }`, each id null where the step has none: ids and counts only, never text (G1 5.3). It is not a step-up action. A step that changed nothing writes nothing. A step that raises is rolled back alone, logged, and tried again at the next round. The image files of the rows a batch deleted are requested with `MediaForCases.requestDeletion` after its commit, as the author's deletion requests them (`apps/api/src/social/flows/posts.ts:63-69`). They are never requested before it, as the account purge does, as the last act of its transaction (7.10), because a disposable row is not disposable for good: a suspect item may freeze a deleted post's images at any moment (6.1), so a batch rolled back after its request could lose the files of rows a freeze then keeps. A request lost to a crash is made good by the reconcile pass (`apps/api/src/media/sweeper.ts:64-89`), because it names at most one batch: half of the 50 orphans (`RECONCILE_MAX_ORPHANS`) the pass deletes without `MEDIA_RECONCILE=force` (`sweeper.ts:24-25,76-83`).

### 7.10 Deletion and moderation

The invariant is section 2's row Deletion and cases; this section says what each path does under it.

- **The author deletes a post.** Unchanged for the author and the public: the body is blanked at once and the post becomes `deleted_by_author` from any state (`repo.ts:480-497`), except that `deletePost` now takes the author's profile row `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` before the post (section 2, Lock order). `detachAll` takes the post's image rows in id order, with no filter of its own: `delete from post_media where id in (select id from post_media where post_id = $1 order by id for update) returning id`. `post_media_keep` skips every retained row (one a case holds, and every row while the post is frozen; 6.1, gate K), so `RETURNING` omits them and no deletion is requested for them. The ordered form is there for the lock order: a plain `delete ... where post_id = $1`, which takes rows in its scan order, deadlocked with an intake's `select ... order by id for key share` over the same rows, and the ordered form did not (lab, PostgreSQL 18.6). Kept images of a deleted post are served to nobody, listed nowhere and exported to nobody until the disposal takes them (7.9), and `find` and `status` answer for them as for a row that does not exist (Readers fail closed, below), so a freeze tells the author nothing. A suspect item that arrives after the deletion still opens its case on the tombstone and freezes whatever is left of the post's images (7.1 step 3).
- **The account is deleted.** `purgeProfile` takes the account's posts in id order (`update posts ... where id in (select id from posts where author_id = $1 and state <> 'deleted_by_author' order by id for update)`), so it cannot deadlock with an intake that holds two posts of the account `FOR SHARE` in id order, which the added item makes common (a post that quotes its author's own post). It runs the media step as the last act of its transaction, after the likes, the follows, the blocks and the handle: `purgeOwner` takes the owner's rows in id order with no filter (`... where owner_id = $1 order by id for update`; the plain `delete ... where owner_id = $1` deadlocked in the lab), `post_media_keep` skips every retained row, and the files of the rows it returned are requested there, inside the transaction, as today (`apps/api/src/account/flows/deletion.ts:13-26`). So files follow rows; what is left is a request written by a purge whose commit then fails, whose rerun finds the same rows. The trigger reads the freeze once for each row, so the purge of an account with several thousand image rows takes seconds (section 3). The account module's purge calls `ModerationForAccount.forgetAccount(userId)` after `purgeProfile` has marked the profile deleted and before `finishPurge`; it runs `forget_reporter`, which acts only on an account whose auth row the take-down marked deleted, and, like every step of the purge, changes nothing when run again. Open cases about the account go on and are decided on their snapshots; their measures are recorded and change nothing (`no_effect` `deleted`), and the author's mail answers `no_address` (`apps/api/src/auth/repo.ts:505-522` makes the address `deleted:<id>`). While an open case about the account holds an unanswered suspect item, every image on the account's posts is frozen, so `purgeOwner` keeps them, unserved, and the case's seal still lists them (6.2). Statements are erased 12 months after the deletion.
- **The author edits the profile.** Unchanged: `editProfile` (`apps/api/src/social/flows/profile-edit.ts:22-30`) sets any display name or bio within `PROFILE_EDITS_PER_HOUR`, including a field korbat reset; 3c adds no check to it (section 2, row Case targets and measures). A report about the new value joins the account's open case, or opens one, with a new snapshot (7.1 step 4), and a new decision resets it again, while deciding too (6.2 step 5).
- **Readers fail closed** (P1 security condition (a)). `postAccess` (`apps/api/src/social/visibility.ts`) becomes an exhaustive switch over the stored state with a `never` check: a block hides everything; `deleted_by_author` is deleted; `visible` is shown; `withheld` and `removed` are shown to the author and hidden from everyone else; so a state added later does not compile until a reader decides it. No reader of posts learns a new state in 3c: a seal is a state of images. `visibleTo` (`repo.ts:318-321`) keeps naming only `visible` and, for the author, `withheld` and `removed`. Images follow both their post and their own state (gate S; 6.1, the freeze): `serve` answers a public image only when both are `visible`, a `withheld` or `removed` one (of the post or of itself) to its author only (`private, no-store`), and 404 to everyone for anything else: a sealed image, an image of a deleted post, and an image whose own `erased_at` or whose post's `erased_at` is set (content an R4 redaction erased, which a hold may still retain). These three are tested before the author's branch, so a sealed image of a withheld or removed post answers 404 to its author too. `fileAccess` reads both marks and the image's state with the post's state. `forPosts` returns each image's state and never gives an image whose post is `deleted_by_author` or whose own or post's `erased_at` is set, to anyone, its owner included (it joins `posts`, as `fileAccess` does, `apps/api/src/media/repo.ts:73-81`), so no view and no export names a kept image of a deleted post; for a sealed image it returns the id and the state only, without size, `blurhash`, alt text or path. The social view builder gives the author their withheld or removed image with its state and gives anyone else, for a visible post with a withheld or removed image, a placeholder (`HiddenMedia`, `{ id, hidden: true }`, no paths, the same for both states); a sealed image is that placeholder for every viewer, with `sealed: true` added only for its author, and the export's `posts[].media` (`apps/api/src/social/flows/export.ts:58-70`) names no sealed image. `readyImages` (the export's files) leaves out sealed images, images of deleted posts and images whose own or post's `erased_at` is set, and the export reads the row again with the same condition when it opens each file (`apps/api/src/media/index.ts:53`), so a seal that commits while an archive streams leaves no sealed file in it from that point on. `find` and the `status` flow answer a sealed image without its `blurhash`; for an image whose post is `deleted_by_author`, or whose own or whose post's `erased_at` is set, they answer as for a row that does not exist, to its owner too, so a freeze tells the author nothing through any route. `existing()` is unchanged, so the reconcile pass sees retained rows (G6 7.4). A copy that Cloudflare's edge or a browser cached before a seal is served for up to five minutes more, until 3d's purge (section 11). The contracts' `PostState` keeps its three values.

### 7.11 The export

The export (`POST /api/account/export`, 1b) gains a member `moderation`, last in `export.json`, from `ModerationForAccount.exportFor(userId)`: `decisions` (each decision about the account that the author's screen lists, with its day, for a decision about a post the post's id (the entry of `posts` it concerns), kind, outcome, ground, rule and version, measures with their `no_effect`, facts, reason and legal reference while they are held, and the appeal's state and result) and `reports` (the account's own intakes while their record is kept (6.6): ref, received time, ground, category, explanation while held, and per item its link (as 7.2 builds it: an account under the handle the report named) and, only once its `outcome_sent_at` is set, its `shown_outcome` and whether re-examination is open, as the list of 7.6 shows them). Never another person's report, never a reporter, never a notifier, never a staff handle (C35). The format stays `korbat-export-1`: the member is additive, as 2c's was (`packages/contracts/src/account.ts:65-69`). In `posts`, a post whose images are sealed is exported with its text and without those images, in the list and in the files (gate S; 6.1, the freeze), a redacted R4 post has its blanked body and no media, and no image an R4 redaction erased is in the media list or the files, and other removed posts keep their body while it is held.

### 7.12 Alerts before 3e

Every staff alert goes through `mailStaff` to every live staff account. `moderation_reveal` goes once for every reveal (7.3), without a throttle, since the daily reveal limit bounds it, and a failed send of it is only logged. `moderation_csam_lifted` goes once for every decision 7.5 step 4 names (a lifted seal, or an admin's final answer to a suspect item without a seal). It has no throttle, since each is an admin's (6.2: `decisions_record` step 6 and `decision_actions_check` step 9) and the decision caps bound them, and a failed send of it is only logged. Like a reveal, none can be undone once the images are public (`apps/api/src/media/flows/serve.ts:6`). The moderation module, which never sees an address, throttles each other alert with one key of the shared limit store: `rateLimitStore.hit(key, window)`, and the mail goes when the count is 1. When `mailStaff` answers 0, the module gives the hit back (`rateLimitStore.release(key)`, `apps/api/src/middleware/rate-limit.ts:10-15`), so the next dispatch round, decision or refused case open that meets the alert's condition sends it again; a failed release is logged. The mail also goes when the store cannot be reached, since an alert fails open.

**Lane 0.** When an intake puts an item into lane 0, a re-examination and a moderator's finding included (P5 security condition (c)), the moderation module sends `moderation_urgent` under the key `moderation-urgent`, with a 15-minute window, giving the number of lane-0 cases waiting and the oldest one's time; a notice about a post that quotes another puts two cases into the lane and sends one mail that counts both. That send is best effort and comes after the commit and the answer (7.1 step 5), so the dispatch job repeats it (7.8 step 5): in each round, while an open case holds a lane-0 item that is unanswered, older than 15 minutes and still calls for someone, the job sends `moderation_urgent` under the same key. Which items still call for someone is one of three cases. (1) A suspect item (6.1) whose case reaches an image that is still served (`MediaForCases.reach(target).served` above zero): until a staff text of kind `seal_declined` is saved on the case after the item arrived, and then only while no post with a served image that the case reaches was created after the newest such text was saved (`reach`'s `newestServedAt` against the text's `created_at`). Nothing else ends it short of the item's answer: not a note, not a withhold of the post, not an image measure, not a field reset, since after each of them some reader, the uploader included, is still served the images. A seal ends it by leaving no served image, and an image posted later starts it again by itself, after a recorded choice not to seal as after a seal: a `seal_declined` text, whenever it was saved, ends the repeat only for the served images of posts created before it. (2) A suspect item whose case reaches no served image: until an interim decision is made, or a `note` or `seal_declined` text is saved on the case, after the item arrived; so a second report about images already sealed is acknowledged with a note, not with a second seal that would mail the author again. (3) Any other lane-0 item: while it arrived after the case's latest interim decision (or the case has none) and, when the case is about an account or its post is not `visible` (`withheld`, `removed` or `deleted_by_author`, as 7.3's case read gives it), after the case's latest `note` text (or the case has no note). "After the item arrived" compares the item's `created_at` with the decision's `decided_at` or the text's `created_at`, the comparison of 6.1's `measures_in_force`, so an item that no seal was taken for goes on alerting until a person seals again. So a final decision through an earlier item, a review or a correction never silences an item it did not consider. The dispatch starts 10 minutes after each round ends (`apps/api/src/lib/jobs.ts:107-113`) and the key's window is 15 minutes, so a lost alert goes out within about 20 minutes, and the alert repeats about every 20 minutes until one of these ends it or a final decision answers the item. A `seal_declined` text, saved from the composer with its reason (section 8), is the recorded form of the power to refuse that a person has and a program lacks: without it the mails would stop only with a seal, which would push whoever is on duty to seal a plainly unfounded report; the home screen lists each for 30 days (7.3). The note, normally the Art. 18 check that the case page asks for (section 8), records that someone on duty has read the new item; in case (3) it is kept beside the interim decision (P2 security condition (c)) because an interim measure on a post that is already not visible changes nothing and mails its author at once, which would tell them a report arrived minutes earlier (T47). On an account case that holds no suspect item the only measures taken while deciding are field resets, which protect nothing when what was reported lies in a handle and which mail the author at once, so a note ends the repeat there too; a reset while deciding stays the step when the display name or bio itself carries what was reported. On a post case a note never ends the alert of case (3) while the post is visible: the post is then withheld in the interim or the item decided. On a case holding a suspect item or an item citing R5, only an admin makes the final decision (6.2 step 6); the case still counts for `moderation_waiting` (below).

**Queue age (C23).** The response times, which the README publishes (Task 12(a)), are 24 hours in lanes 0 and 1 and seven days in lane 2, counted from a case's `oldest_open_at`. An interim decision answers no item (6.1, `decisions_interim`), so a lane-0 case withheld in the interim and waiting for an admin's final decision still counts. An open case holding an interim measure in force also counts once that measure is 24 hours old, whatever its lane, since its author cannot appeal it (P5 security condition (d)). In each round, when any case is past its time, the dispatch sends `moderation_waiting` under the key `moderation-waiting`, with a 24-hour window, giving per lane the number of cases past it and the oldest one's time, and the number of cases with an interim measure older than 24 hours.

No staff alert carries a link or content. 3e replaces the mails with the phone channel.

### 7.13 The command line

`staff.js` gains the commands below. Each sets the 10-second `lock_timeout` in every transaction it opens, as 3a's do (`README.md:125`). Each writes its audit row in the same transaction as its change and as that transaction's last statement (`apps/api/src/staff/audit.ts:36-43`). Each exits 1 with a message when it changed nothing it was asked to. Each writing command but `access-request` runs as the migrator in the migrate service, as `enroll` runs; `access-request` runs in the API service, as its bullet says:

- `staff.js link <staff email> <personal handle>` and `staff.js unlink <staff email> <personal handle>`: record or remove a staff account's personal account in `auth.staff_personal_accounts` (the staff account must be staff, the personal one live and not staff); audit rows `staff_link_added` and `staff_link_removed` (system actor; target the staff account; metadata empty, since the link itself is what must not spread). `staff.js keys` lists, per staff account, how many personal accounts are linked, never which, read on the auth role through `auth.staff_link_counts()`.
- `staff.js limits <decisions per hour> <decisions per day> <case opens per day> <findings per day> <reveals per day>`: upserts row 1 of `moderation_limits` (`insert ... values (1, ...) on conflict (id) do update set ... where` any of the five values differs, `returning`), so it also works on a database whose row is missing, as the integration setup's truncate leaves it (`apps/api/test/integration/global-setup.ts:28`). It writes the audit row `moderation_limits_set`, with the five numbers, only when a row was inserted or changed, and exits 1 with "the limits already are ..." when all five were already set.
- `staff.js text-verify` (the API's role, read-only): for every row of `case_decided`, `appeals_reviewed` and `identity_revealed`, each digest in `metadata.params` must name a text still held whose digest recomputes in Node, an erased text, a snapshot still held whose digest recomputes (with the contracts' `snapshotDigest`), or an erased snapshot; anything else is reported and exits 1. `audit-verify` is unchanged (G1 5.4: a missing text is a retention fault, not a broken chain). It also checks every decision: exactly one audit row of `case_decided` or `appeals_reviewed` names it in `metadata.decisionId`, with the actor, target and params `decisions_check` (a) compares, and the decision's actions equal that row's `params.actions` as a set; a decision named by no row or by two, or differing from its row, is reported and exits 1. Last, it recomputes every post's level and every image's state from `measures_in_force`, `seal_posts`, the case closures, the items and the `erased_at` marks, as `moderation_apply` would, and for every seal in force it checks that each `seal_posts` row's post still has `images` image rows. It reads `posts` itself, and each image's stored `moderation_state` and `erased_at` through the media module's `imageModerationStates` (section 5). It reports any row or count that differs (a change made around the triggers, by the migrator or by hand).
- `staff.js account-facts <handle> <case id>` (as the migrator, read-only but for its audit row): prints the account's handle, email address and account creation time for the runbook's Art. 18 step. It refuses an account that is not the case's subject (its post's author or the account). Audit row `account_facts_read` (system actor; target the account; metadata `{ caseId }`).
- `staff.js access-request <@handle | email>` (in the API service, on the API role and the auth role, read-only but for its audit row). It drafts the moderation part of an answer to a GDPR access request (`law-3c.md` 9.6) and prints it as JSON for the operator.
  - For a handle: every case whose target is the account or one of its posts, whatever its outcome, no-action cases included. For each, every item's source category ("a report by another user", "a notice under Article 16", "found by a moderator"), ground, rule and category; the snapshots, with their content unless `snapshot_withheld` (an erased one with the day it was erased and no content; a sealed image is named by its id and state, never served); every decision with its statement's data and texts while held; the account's own appeals with their statements. It also gives the account's own reports and notices, as in the export.
  - Under `withheld`: every reporter's and notifier's identity, never printed, with the reason "rights of others: GDPR Art. 15(4), recital 63". Each report's explanation and cited law, and each re-examination statement, is printed and marked "send only after a person has weighed the writer's rights (C-203/22)".
  - Every case holding an item that `laneFor` puts in lane 0 (the CSAM path, R2, R5, violence, public security, self-harm), open or closed, is marked, with everything printed for it: "check the R2, Art. 18 and order records before sending: an authority may need the person not to be told yet, or national law may restrict access (GDPR Art. 23(1)(d))" (`law-3c.md` 9.6). Without it the draft would hand the reported person, for example, an unanswered suspect item on a post already not visible whose case reaches no served image, which the case's note kept from them in place of an interim decision (7.12; P4 security condition (e)). Flagged for counsel: whether, and under which national law, access may be restricted or deferred for such a case, what the answer and any extension under Art. 12(3) then say, and whether it names the authorities told as recipients (Art. 15(1)(c)).
  - For an email: the contacts `findNotifierContacts` finds, with the name given and the notices filed under them (ref, received time, ground, category, explanation and cited law while held, each item's link as 7.2 builds it, shown outcome and re-examination).
  - Audit row `access_request_drafted` (system actor; target the account for a handle, none for an email; metadata `{ cases, contacts }`, counts only), through `appendAuditRow`.

### 7.14 Authorities' orders before 3e

3c builds no path for authorities' orders: no order finding, no order source, no order outcome. korbat is not open to the public before 3e (section 2, row Splitting 3c, the hard gate), so it disseminates nothing to the public, and no authorities' contact point is published. An order that reaches korbat anyway is handled by the runbook (README, Task 12(a)), which gives effect to nothing by SQL or by editing rows (P6 security condition):

1. The operator verifies it through contact details found independently: for a TCO removal order, the Commission's register; for any other order, the authority's published details, never the order's own (`law-3c.md` 4.4, 8.9).
2. The operator acknowledges it without undue delay (DSA Art. 9(1), 10(1)), with Annex II once effect is given or Annex III when it cannot be executed, where the TCO Regulation applies (TCO Art. 3(6) to (8)), and records the order and every answer off the server and puts it on counsel's list with the operator's answer, among them whether to ask the home authority to scrutinise a TCO order from another Member State within 48 hours (TCO Art. 4(4)).
3. An order to act against content that exists on the private instance is never left without effect. The operator has its author, a staff member or a tester the operator knows, delete it, and records that. When the author cannot be asked in time, a moderator withholds it in the interim on a finding under a korbat rule where one fits, an ordinary confirmed decision; otherwise the operator takes the instance off the network (`TUNNEL=off`, and the port that reaches Caddy closed to everyone but the operator).
4. An order to provide information (DSA Art. 10) is acknowledged and recorded as step 2 says; korbat cannot produce anything under it before 3e's command line, which the answer says.
5. When the content gives rise to a suspicion under DSA Art. 18, that step applies on its own terms (README, Art. 18 and TCO Art. 14(5)).

3e designs orders as records of their own, with their clock, preservation, information and non-disclosure duties (section 16).

### 7.15 Mails

Nine templates join the closed union (`apps/api/src/mail/mailer.ts:1-27`, 25 to 34), each with a `case` in `renderTemplate` and an entry in `apps/api/test/mail/templates.test.ts`. User mails carry only korbat-generated fields and staff texts written for the statement, never a reporter's or notifier's words, the content, a count of reports or a staff handle, and each ends with "korbat staff never ask for your password or a code, and korbat does not read replies to this email." The staff mails carry no link and end, as 3a's do, with "korbat never puts a link in a staff email." Times print as `noticeTime` does (`apps/api/src/auth/flows/notify.ts:17-20`). Every outcome phrase and redress sentence comes from section 10's constants (P9).

- `notice_received` {ref, time, items, pageUrl?, termsUrl}: subject "korbat received your report {ref}"; "korbat received your report {ref} at {time}. It names: {items}."
