- **Console**: server-rendered checks of every new screen (among them the composer offering a measure on a post that is not visible with its no-effect sentence and the note advice on a lane-0 case, offering only "While deciding" to a non-admin on a case with an unanswered suspect item or one citing R5, and, on a case with an unanswered suspect item, "Seal the images this case reaches" with its fixed drafts and "Do not seal now", the seal for good and the answer without one for an admin only, with the sentence that the answer also answers the earlier reports on other grounds unseen, on a post case only, and the page's word that the later ones stay open, the unseen sentence beside No breach and Illegality not evident on a post case whose `imagesAllowed` is false, for a moderator too, and not where the images may be opened, and "Lift the seal" for an admin only; the Lift and Correct controls; the review composer with no restricting outcome; a re-examination item and its mark; the banner of a case with an unanswered suspect item: the counts of `reach`, the post sentence only on a post case, the account sentences only on an account case, the no-image sentence, the quoting-post sentence with its `CaseLink`, the added-item sentence, the `earlier` sentence only above zero, with the day staff last opened an image of the post when there is one, no link into the app anywhere on the page, and none of it on a case without a suspect item) and the browser check of section 8.
- **App**: server-rendered checks of the report form (each ground, the profile sentence and the one-profile limit of the CSAM path, the illegal ground's pointer to the third answer, the CSAM path hiding the contact fields, the signed-out fields, Turnstile shown signed out for each ground), the form opened without an item and the terms page's link to it, the success screen of a signed-in CSAM report showing the link and not the Settings sentence, the status page (each phrase of `SUSPECT_OUTCOME_PHRASES`, `ACCOUNT_SUSPECT_SCOPE`, also after the `sealed` phrase of a rule report about an account, `UNSEEN_NO_ACTION` with "Ask for a re-examination" beside it, also on a re-examination's answer and then without the final sentence, an added item's label; the token read from the fragment and sent in a POST body; "Ask for a re-examination" only while open; a re-examination's answer and a changed outcome with the final sentence; the stopped state with its undo), Your reports with its re-examination and stop notice, an account's item shown there and on the status page under the handle the report named after the account changed it, and `UNSEEN_NO_ACTION` in the list, the cases screens (an interim decision's screen with the interim redress and no Appeal; a final restriction's with Appeal; a post whose images are sealed keeping its text and its place in its author's views, each sealed image drawn as "Sealed: not shown to anyone, you included" for its author and as the placeholder of a withheld image for anyone else; a decision screen with the `erased` reason and its day after the retention job erased its case's copies, with the profile sentence for a post the author kept and without it for one they deleted), the chips (each of their four texts) and the placeholder (the same text for a withheld and a removed image of a visible post, and the author's "Under review" and "Removed" labels); the viewer opened from an image in a quote embed linking `/report?post=<quoted post's id>&media=<id>`; the terms page's Contact section and the `human_check_failed`, `rate_limited` and `service_unavailable` sentences naming the build value; the Dockerfile's and compose's contact-address lines pinned as the badge's are (`apps/app/test/lifecycle.test.tsx:180-190`).
- **Mail**: the nine templates pinned in `apps/api/test/mail/templates.test.ts`: no URL in the staff ones; a page link only in `notice_received`, in the folded receipts a `notice_update` carries, and in a signed-out `notice_update` once for each notice it has a line for, always with the mail token and never the notice token; no page link in a signed-in `notice_update`; the re-examination sentence only for lines with a moderator's `no_violation` or `not_evident` decision on an item neither re-examined nor a re-examination, and for a re-examination's line that reads `UNSEEN_NO_ACTION` and is not re-examined itself, pointing to the page when signed out and to Your reports when signed in; "final within korbat" for changed outcomes and for re-examinations' answers, but not for one that reads `UNSEEN_NO_ACTION`, re-examined again or not (7.8 step 4's rule, from `unseen`); `ALREADY_NOT_SHOWN`, `ACCOUNT_ITEM_SCOPE` or `ACCOUNT_SUSPECT_SCOPE`, `QUOTE_ITEM_SCOPE`, the `sealed` phrase and `UNSEEN_NO_ACTION` where 7.8 step 4 says; only case-less lines called automatic; the courts and the Digital Services Coordinator in every update with an outcome line; no template carrying a report's words; `moderation_decision`'s two subjects for a seal and `moderation_csam_lifted`'s two texts for `what`; no mail line for a suspect item.
- **Smoke** (`scripts/smoke-auth-stack.mjs`): after the existing steps, a signed-out notice on a post with Turnstile's test key (the development configuration's), its status page through the token, and a signed-in report; the console's case routes answer 401 without a staff session. A decision needs a staff key, so it is the dry run's browser walk-through, not the smoke test.

## 13. Tasks

Each task is one commit, written tests first, dry-run in the scratch clone with the gated runner, and leaves the stack working.

1. **The ground.** Migrations 0014 and 0015 (6.1): first 0014, generated with the old tables, their enums and `profiles.reputation` removed from the schema and nothing added; then 0015, generated once the new schema is in place and completed by hand (the grants, the forced-field triggers, `appeal_deadline`, `intake_items_attach` with its re-examination checks, `intake_items_quote`, the view `measures_in_force`, `frozen_for`, `post_frozen`, `post_media_keep`, `post_media_guard`, `media_holds_apply`, the auth grants and the `moderation_limits` row); the Drizzle schema (`packages/db/src/schema/moderation.ts` new, with `media_holds` and `seal_posts`, and `intake_items` with `suspect`, `named_handle` and the check `intake_items_named_handle`, and `decisions` with `suspect` and `unseen`; `safety.ts` without the old tables; `content.ts` with `posts.moderation_level` and `erased_at` and `post_media`'s three columns; `identity.ts` without `reputation`; `auth.ts` with the four auth tables; `helpers.ts` with `xid8`); `packages/db/test/schema.test.ts` (39 tables and the pins of 6.5); `packages/contracts/src/canonical.ts` (the writer and helper moved out of `staff.ts`, with `stepUpActionText`'s existing tests unchanged) and `packages/contracts/src/moderation.ts`'s constants and digests (`TDB_CATEGORIES`, `TDB_FORM_CATEGORIES`, `CSAM_KEYWORDS`, `DECISION_KEYWORDS`, `RULE_DEFAULT_CATEGORY`, `laneFor`, `NOTICE_LIMITS`, `CSAM_NOTICE_MAX_ACCOUNTS`, `SUSPECT_EARLIER_MONTHS`, `SEAL_LIST_MAX`, `APPEAL_MONTHS`, `DAILY_LIMITS`, `MEASURES`, `RETENTION`, `textDigest`, `snapshotText`, `snapshotDigest`, `appealDeadline`) with their tests. **The image readers fail closed** before any code can write `sealed` (P1 law condition 1, build condition 1): `serve`'s whitelist, `forPosts` and `readyImages` skipping sealed images, and `status` answering one without its `blurhash`, each with a test on an image the migrator set to `sealed` (Task 3 turns `forPosts`' skip into the sealed placeholder and adds the export's second read). `postAccess` becomes an exhaustive switch over the stored states with a `never` check; no reader of posts learns a new state. `scripts/clean-dev-fixtures.mjs` keeps, as it keeps an account with a staff key, every account that decided anything, appears on either side of `auth.staff_personal_accounts` (whose `on delete restrict` keys would otherwise fail the whole cleanup), or is the subject of a case that a decision or an `identity_revealed` access row names, and counts them as kept; for the accounts it deletes it first deletes every case about their posts or profiles, each case's `intake_items` rows first, since deleting an unanswered suspect item is what ends its freeze, then its `media_holds` rows after releasing them, and its `case_access_log`, `staff_texts` and `evidence_snapshots` rows, and then every intake left without an item, before it deletes the profiles, so that `post_media_keep` lets the cascade through, counting the cases deleted (posts and profiles are never deleted in production, 6.1, so such a case could never be decided and would otherwise stay in the development queue after every smoke run); it never meets a `seal_posts` row, since a seal belongs to a decision and the subject of a decided case is kept. The database tests that need no flow (section 12: grants, forced columns, the attach trigger's races and its re-examination checks, `intake_items_quote`, the freeze's predicate as far as no decision is needed, `post_media_keep` with its two-session races against an intake's lock, `post_media_guard`, the first database pins of 6.5, `media_holds_apply`, the `staff_texts` CHECK against the digest vectors, each CHECK held to its constant, the checks of `intakes`), each run on a fresh database and on one at 0013, committed. Gates: the generate gate prints "No schema changes"; the contracts, db and integration database suites pass. `scripts/test-db.mjs` gains two options, given before its `--`. `--fresh` drops the test database (`drop database if exists :"db" with (force)`; the name must still end in `_test`, `scripts/test-db.mjs:46`), creates it again with its grants and runs every migration in one batch, as a new installation does. `--upgrade-from <n>` does the same drop and create, then migrates through `MIGRATIONS_DIR` (`apps/api/src/migrate.ts:6`) with a temporary copy of `packages/db/migrations` whose `meta/_journal.json` keeps only the entries with `idx` up to `<n>`, which commits them. It then migrates again with the real folder, so every later migration runs as one batch over the committed database, as an upgrade of production does. A task runs "on a fresh database and on one at 0013" when the integration suite (`pnpm --filter @korbat/api test:integration`) passes after `node scripts/test-db.mjs --fresh` and again after `node scripts/test-db.mjs --upgrade-from 13`. The second run is the batch an upgrade of production runs. The script's usage comment gains the line "Usage: node scripts/test-db.mjs [--fresh | --upgrade-from <n>] [-- <docker compose command>]".

the dispatch job (`dispatch.ts`: receipts, statements, the daily release through `release_outcomes`, the per-address `notice_update` with `outcome_mail` and its retries, `forgetNotifierAfter`, the lane-0 repeat of 7.12 in its three cases (the first reading `newestServedAt`), the outcome lines' words with the `sealed` phrase, `UNSEEN_NO_ACTION` and the scopes of 7.8 step 4, step 6, the isolation of steps and rows, the step order 5, 3, 1, 2, 4, 6, the cut-off and its marker), the template `notice_update` with `MailMessage`'s union, `NoticeUpdateData` and `renderNoticeUpdate` (section 5) and `AuthForModeration.mailNotifierUpdates`; the template `moderation_waiting` and the queue-age alert of 7.12, its interim-measure count included; `@korbat/rules` added to `apps/admin`; the browser check's decision, seal and lift runs; tests, among them the path's flow from the seal taken while deciding to the admin's two answers, the range of an admin's answer through the composer, what the sender of another report reads after it (`UNSEEN_NO_ACTION`, in the mail's line and on the status page), the same phrase after a moderator's no-action decision on a post frozen through its author's account's case and on a post whose images are sealed for good, and the repeat after a recorded choice not to seal once an image is posted.
7. **The app.** The routes `GET /api/moderation/cases`, `/decisions/:id`, `/reports` (`author.ts`, `author-repo.ts`, `reports.ts`); `ModerationForSocial.authorFacts` and `PostView.moderation` through the social module's options; the report form, the notice status page, the cases screens and the decision screen; "Report" on posts, images and profiles; `/report` without an item and the terms page's link to it; the chips and the author's "Removed" and "Under review" labels on their own images; `SettingsNav`'s "Cases" (`SettingsPage` gains `"cases"`; the screens under `apps/app/src/app/settings/cases/`); the app's messages; `@korbat/rules` added to `apps/app`; `EXPO_PUBLIC_CONTACT_EMAIL`: `WEB_CONTACT_EMAIL=contact@korbat.local` in `infra/.env.example` and `EXPO_PUBLIC_CONTACT_EMAIL=contact@korbat.local` in `apps/app/.env.example`; the compose service passes `EXPO_PUBLIC_CONTACT_EMAIL: ${WEB_CONTACT_EMAIL:?set WEB_CONTACT_EMAIL in infra/.env}`; the Caddy Dockerfile takes it as a build argument, refuses it when it does not match `*?@?*.?*`, and with `KORBAT_ENV=production` refuses a domain ending in `.local`, `.localhost`, `.test`, `.invalid` or `.example`, or equal to `example.com`, `example.org` or `example.net`, as it refuses a Turnstile test key (`infra/caddy/Dockerfile:14-16`), and passes it to `expo export`; the terms page's Contact section; `POST /api/moderation/report-mail/resume` and `ReportsPage.reportMailStopped`; the status page's stopped state and the Your reports notice; the app's tests, among them the author's decision view of a post whose images are sealed, the sealed placeholder, the status page's words for suspect items and its added item, `UNSEEN_NO_ACTION` on the status page and in Your reports, an account's item shown under the handle the report named after a rename, and the report form's profile sentence; the web export.
8. **Appeals and re-examination.** Migration 0017 (6.3); `appeals.ts`; `POST /api/moderation/decisions/:id/appeal`; the re-examination routes `POST /api/notices/reexamine` and `POST /api/moderation/items/:item/reexamine` with their limits, `forgetNotifierAfter` and the lane-0 alert; `GET /api/staff/appeals` and `POST /api/staff/decisions/:decision/review` (`appeals_reviewed`) with the review route's after-commit step, `moderation_csam_lifted` included; the console's appeals screen, review composer and the re-examination mark on items; the app's appeal form and states, and "Ask for a re-examination" on the status page and in Your reports; `notice_update`'s re-examination sentences; the overview's number of appeals this person may review and `appealsWithoutReviewer`, the home screen's whole appeals line, and `CaseAppeal.needsAdmin`, each through `review_needs_admin`; the appeals list's review mark through `staff_case_conflict`, with no statement in the list; tests (windows, one and two moderators, reversal effects, appeals of a decision reversed in full, concurrency, `review_needs_admin` from its two stored facts, a review that lifts a seal (`unseal_media`, accepted for an admin and refused to a moderator; the correction that lifts one is Task 2's), re-examinations on every path, an added item's and the double one included, and the re-examination of an item that reads `UNSEEN_NO_ACTION`, also when it is itself a re-examination, 7.2).
9. **Evidence lifecycle and the command line.** Migration 0018 (6.4) with `erase_snapshots` and the trigger `users_deleted_mark` on `auth.users` and its database test as `korbat_auth`; `apps/api/test/integration/staff-schema.test.ts:316-331`, which sets and clears the mark as the auth role around `auth.forget_staff_keys`, rolls its transaction back instead of clearing the mark; the retention job (`retention.ts`) with its candidate reads, its audit rows and `dropDisposable` in batches, each batch's files requested after its commit; `ModerationForAccount.forgetAccount`, called by the account module's purge after `purgeProfile` (7.10); `staff.js link`, `unlink`, `limits` (five numbers), `text-verify` (with the recomputation of levels and of image states over `seal_posts`, and the check that every seal in force still has its listed image rows, both read through `imageModerationStates`, which this task adds to `apps/api/src/media/index.ts` with its test), `account-facts` with its test (refused for an account that is not the case's subject; one audit row), `access-request` with its tests (an erased snapshot printed without content; a case holding a lane-0 item, open and closed, marked for the authorities' check, and a lane-2 case not marked), and `staff.js keys` counting links through `auth.staff_link_counts`; `erasureOverdue` in the overview, `StaffOverview` and the home screen (its contacts term through `AuthForModeration.overdueContacts`, built in Task 4); the contention lab extended with `redact_r4_body` against `moderation_apply` and against an intake locking the post's images; the database pins after 0018 (the callers of `post_frozen`, the one writer of `evidence_snapshots.erased_at`); section 12's enumeration of deleters through every step of the retention job and an R4 redaction made final; tests with the clock moved through every row of 6.6, and `text-verify` reporting a decision changed as the migrator, a post whose level the migrator set by hand and a seal in force one of whose listed posts has lost an image row. The integration suite after `node scripts/test-db.mjs --upgrade-from 13`, which applies 0014 to 0018 as one batch over a committed 0013, the batch production runs, and after `--fresh`. `MODERATION_RETENTION` (`blankAsUnset.pipe(z.enum(["on", "off"]).default("on"))`), added in three places: in `config.ts` with unit tests (a blank value is `on`; `yes` is refused); in `apps/api/.env.example` and `infra/.env.example` as `API_MODERATION_RETENTION=on`, with the comment 'off only for a restore window: README, Restoring, step 5'; and in the api service of `infra/compose.yml` as `MODERATION_RETENTION: ${API_MODERATION_RETENTION:-on}`. A test that `startJobs` with it `off` logs the warning and runs no retention round while the dispatch job's rounds run: with the clock moved past 10 minutes, a due case keeps its snapshot and a due R4 decision's post keeps its body.
10. **Export, terms and the stack.** `ModerationForAccount.exportFor` and the export's `moderation` member (`ExportFormat`, `apps/api/src/account/flows/export.ts`); `terms.ts`'s sections of section 9 with the table tests; the smoke test's steps; the whole stack rebuilt and smoke-tested; the dry run's browser walk-through of the console and the app (a report signed out, a decision with the virtual authenticator, the author's screen, an appeal and its review by a second staff account, a re-examination and its decision).
11. **What the adversarial review of Tasks 1 to 10 and the browser walk-through upheld.**
12. **Documentation.**
    - (a) The README's moderation section: how reports and notices arrive; the queue and its lanes with their response times (7.12: 24 hours in lanes 0 and 1, seven days in lane 2; targets for a team of one or two, not promises; staff get the queue-age mail when a case passes them, or when a measure taken while deciding is older than 24 hours); deciding and the preview; measures on content that is already not shown (recorded, told, in force should the other restriction be reversed); the limits on staff (the decision caps, the daily case-open, findings and reveal limits, all set with `staff.js limits`; every reveal, and every decision that lifts the protection of reported images, is mailed to every staff account); reveals; appeals and the one-moderator rule; re-examination (when the item re-examines your own decision and another staff member exists, leave it to them); evidence and its retention by consumer; the emergency switch (set `API_MODERATION_DEFER_UNPROVEN=on` in `infra/.env`, then run `docker compose -f infra/compose.yml up -d api`, which recreates the API with it, since the API reads it at start; `off` and the same command end it); the mails (the urgent-lane alert repeats about every 20 minutes until a decision answers the item or withholds the post in the interim; on a post already withheld, removed or deleted by its author, and on an account case, it stops once someone saves a note on the case after the item arrived, normally the Art. 18 check, and the case then waits for its final decision, an admin's for suspect items and items citing R5, under the queue-age mail; on a report or finding of child sexual abuse material whose case reaches an image that is still served it repeats until the images are sealed, someone records that they do not seal, or an admin answers, and starts again when an image is posted afterwards that the case reaches, and where its case reaches no served image a note or a decision taken while deciding ends it too (7.12); a mail is not a pager); and `staff.js link`, `unlink`, `limits`, `text-verify`, `account-facts` and `access-request`. It gives the rotation rule for `API_NOTIFIER_ENC_KEY` beside the TOTP one (`README.md:174`): "To rotate `API_NOTIFIER_ENC_KEY`, put the old key in `API_NOTIFIER_ENC_KEYS_PREVIOUS` and a new `v2:` key in `API_NOTIFIER_ENC_KEY`. Contacts are never re-encrypted, so the old key stays listed until no contact made under it remains, that is until `select count(*) from auth.notifier_contacts where name_enc like 'v1:%' or email_enc like 'v1:%'` answers 0. A contact can outlive 13 months while its notice is still owed a decision or a mail. Keep the old key off the server for as long as any backup holds such a contact."
      Its runbook:
      - linking each staff member's personal accounts at onboarding and whenever they say they opened another;
      - **Reports and findings of child sexual abuse material (R2)**: nobody opens the images such a report concerns, in the console or in the app, before or after a seal, until an admin has answered without one. As soon as the urgent mail arrives, whoever has the server runs `staff.js account-facts` for the case's subject and keeps its output with the R2 records until the admin's answer; it is destroyed after an answer without a seal, and the seal never waits for it. Whoever is on duty opens the case and seals what it reaches with "Seal the images this case reaches", under rule R2, with the composer's drafts, and never writes that the images are such material. They seal unless the report is plainly unfounded, and then record why with "Do not seal now": an earlier answer without a seal was as unseen as this one will be, so it is a reason not to seal only when the report's words add nothing and the case reaches no image posted since that answer (the day of `earlier`'s last answer against the day of the newest post the case reaches) and staff have opened the images since the last answer (the case page shows the day for a post; for an account, a note or the colleague says so); a case that reaches no image cannot be sealed. A report about a post that quotes another has a second case, on the quoted post, linked from the first and sealed the same way. Nobody opens a post's page in the app while a report of this kind about the post is unanswered or its images are sealed (the rule of 6.1): it shows the post it replies to and its replies with their images, which the case does not reach. Nobody opens an account's profile in the app while a report of this kind about the account is unanswered or its images are sealed: it shows posts of other accounts that it reposted or quoted, which the case does not reach, and images posted since the seal; when the page shows images that are not sealed, whoever is on duty seals again. When the post's text links to or offers such material, the post is withheld as well. When the words of a report on another ground describe such images, whoever reads it opens a finding citing R2 on the post before opening any image. While such a report is unanswered, every other procedure about the same images (a report on another ground, a correction, the review of an appeal) is decided on text alone or waits for the admin's answer (6.1). A report about a post that is answered without action meanwhile, or while the post's images are sealed, is answered unseen, as the composer says there: its sender is told so and may ask again. An admin answers every such case, all its suspect items at once, without seeing the images: seal for good, and report, when the report's words, the post's text or alt texts, or the account's conduct give a concrete reason to believe the report; answer without a seal when they give none; say which it was in the decision's facts; in doubt, ask the operator before answering; never open the images to settle it. On a post's case the admin's answer also answers the reports on other grounds that arrived before the newest report of this kind, unseen; their senders are told so and may ask again (on an account's case such reports concern the display name and bio, which the admin reads). After an answer without a seal the case's images may be opened: whoever is on duty opens the case's target the same day (a post's images in the console, on its case; an account's in the app, which is then the only place they are shown, 6.1) and, where an image breaks a rule or the law, opens a finding on the post and decides it (if it is what the report said, a finding citing R2, which freezes again). For an account, and whenever a look found nothing, whoever looked writes a note on the case ("Images opened on <date> after the answer of <date>: nothing that breaks a rule"): the case page shows only the day a post's image was last opened in the console, nothing else on the server records a look at an account's images in the app, and the note goes with its case's evidence. When a new report of this kind arrives before that decision is made, the admin's next answer carries the measure for what staff saw, and its facts say when the images were seen. An admin's answer never restricts under another rule on a report's words alone about an image nobody at korbat has opened: that report is answered unseen, and its sender may ask again. An admin who can tell from an account report's words which post is meant opens a finding citing R2 on that post, seals there, and answers the account's item without a seal. An admin seals for good only what korbat then reports: on every seal for good the operator reports at once to the national police or the authority the Member State names, never to a hotline, with, for a post, its address, its times, the author's handle, the account facts and the text and alt texts of its snapshot, and, for an account, the handle, the account facts and the address, time and number of images of each post whose images are sealed, as the case page lists them (never an image). The operator records their reference with the address, the case and the seal's time, off the server, and tells them that korbat keeps the images sealed, that its software has no function yet to hand them over or to erase them, and that the operator does either by hand on their written instruction. On such an instruction, before sub-project 3d, the operator copies out or destroys the files of the sealed images by hand, as the server's administrator, without opening them (for a destruction, also every backup that holds them); the read-only query `select m.id from seal_posts s join post_media m on m.post_id = s.post_id where s.action_id = '<the seal's action id>' order by m.id` lists the images, whose files lie in the `media-store` volume under those ids; nothing in the database is changed, no admin lifts the seal for it, and the operator records off the server what was done, when and on which instruction. An admin lifts a seal for good only on the authority's answer that the images are not child sexual abuse material, and says in the reason that the authority has answered and the date of its answer, never its reference or anything that names a person (the reference stays in the operator's R2 records, off the server); a seal taken while deciding ends with the admin's answer, and before it an admin lifts it, by a correction, only when it was taken in error (on the wrong case, or on a report that the standard above gives no reason to believe). Every staff account is mailed `moderation_csam_lifted` after a lifted seal and after an admin's answer without a seal; the operator checks each against their R2 records and, when one was not expected, follows the mail's steps. A post already removed or deleted has its images sealed all the same (a seal of its own, P2);
      - a seal that lists more posts than the case page shows: the read-only query `select s.post_id, p.created_at, s.images from seal_posts s join posts p on p.id = s.post_id where s.action_id = '<the seal's action id>' order by p.created_at, s.post_id` gives every post the seal lists, with its creation time and its number of images, for the report;
      - a notice of illegality or any report about a post already removed or deleted is decided on its merits: a finding of a breach is recorded as a measure of its own;
      - a breach that lies only in a handle (impersonation, a doxxed name, a threat) is never answered "No breach" in 3c: the display name is reset when it carries the content, and otherwise the case stays open for 3e's handle retirement (its Art. 18 note ends the urgent-lane mails; the queue-age mail then reminds staff once a day) (P7 law condition 2);
      - **Art. 18 and TCO Art. 14(5)**: whoever on staff first reads a lane-0 case, or any case whose content suggests a threat to someone's life or safety, decides at once whether it gives rise to a suspicion of a criminal offence involving a threat to the life or safety of a person (DSA Art. 18(1); a suicide threat is not such an offence, `law-3c.md` 3.1, 3.4). If yes, or unsure, and they are not the operator, they wake the operator at once, at any hour. They record the check in a note on the case ("Art. 18 check: no suspicion" or "Art. 18 check: operator told at <time>"), and nobody gives such a case its final decision before that note exists. If yes, the operator informs promptly, at any hour, (i) the police of the Member State concerned (where the offence is suspected, or where the suspect or the victim lives or is), (ii) when that state cannot be identified with reasonable certainty, the police of korbat's own state, Europol, or both, through the channel obtained beforehand from the national police or the Digital Services Coordinator (`law-3c.md` 3.2), and (iii) for terrorist content with an imminent threat to life, the authorities of the states concerned, or else the home state's TCO Art. 12(2) contact point and Europol (TCO Art. 14(5), `law-3c.md` 3.5); the operator sends the content as the case page shows it, its publication time with the time zone, why it is suspected, the account's handle and what `staff.js account-facts` prints, and says what korbat does not keep (raw IP addresses, location) (`law-3c.md` 3.3); images reported as child sexual abuse material are never sent this way, and are reported as the R2 paragraph says; the operator records the authority, the channel, the basis for the state, the time, what was sent and the reference received, in their own records until 3e;
      - **authorities' orders before 3e**: the steps of 7.14; korbat stays private until 3e's order path and 3d's handling of sealed images have both shipped;
      - each January the operator counts the past calendar year's decisions marked terrorist content (with the reviews and corrections that reversed them) and the orders in their own records, and publishes the TCO Art. 7(2) report before 1 March when it applies;
      - **the users' contact mailbox**: the operator reads it at least each working day, in a mail client set to load no remote content and to show no inline image or attachment preview. Attachments are never opened, previewed, downloaded or forwarded, and no link in a mail is followed: a korbat link is copied into the report form, never opened. Every report or notice found there is entered through the report form, signed out in a private window, from the mail's text alone, with the sender's links, ground, rule or category, explanation and cited law, and, for a notice that is not on the CSAM path, the sender's name and email; the good-faith box is ticked only when the mail states the sender's good-faith belief that it is accurate and complete. It becomes a `user` intake of channel `signed_out`, so the sender gets `notice_received` with the status link, the daily `notice_update` and re-examination. A mail that says it concerns the sexual abuse or exploitation of a child, child sexual abuse material or the grooming of a child is entered on the CSAM path, with the choice its words name (`csam` when they name none) and without the sender's name or email. When the entered intake carries no contact (a rule-only report, or the CSAM path), the sender gets the fixed reply with the reference and the status page's link from the success screen. The mail is then deleted with its attachments and removed from the trash the same day; the fixed reply that carries a CSAM-path reference is deleted from the sent folder, and from the trash, once sent. A finding is never used for a mailed report or notice. A mail from an authority is handled under 7.14, its attachment opened only after 7.14 step 1 has verified the sender. No report or notice is answered by hand except with the README's fixed replies (one asking for the good-faith sentence or for the form; one giving the reference and the status link to a sender whose intake carries no contact; and one to a mail about a decision: "korbat answers a decision only through its own procedures: the person a decision restricts can appeal it in korbat under Settings, Cases, and a person who reported can ask for a re-examination of a decision to take no action on the page their receipt links to, or under Settings, Cases, Your reports, within six months of being told. korbat's emails never discuss a case."); the only other mails sent from this address are the GDPR answers (below) and the answers of 7.14; at most 20 can be entered a day from one network (7.1's limit), and while Turnstile is down they wait;
      - "illegal on the face of it" and when to answer "not evident" (`law-3c.md` 1.4, 1.6);
      - the statement's texts (facts, reason, legal reference, review reason) never hold personal data of anyone but the author: describe it ("the home address of another person"), never copy it, since they are mailed, exported and kept while the account exists, also after an R4 redaction has erased the content (`law-3c.md` 2.5 and its obligations table); a saved draft that copies it is not confirmed: save a corrected one;
      - machine translation stays local; the console in a browser profile of its own with no extensions, nothing pasted into third-party tools (C3); the daily case and reveal limits and raising them with `staff.js limits`;
      - a `staff_recused` refusal may mean one of the case's reporters is close to your own account: do not try to learn which, and leave the case to another staff member; when no other staff member can take it (with one staff member, always), the case waits in the queue and its lane-0 and queue-age mails keep coming, and the operator makes another person staff (grant-role and enrolment) to decide it; nobody unlinks an account, or unfollows, unmutes or unblocks one, to get round a refusal;
      - a case about a staff account's content or profile fields: every final restrictive measure on a staff account is refused (`staff_target`); whoever is on duty withholds the post or image, seals reported images, or resets the field, while deciding, as for any account, and tells the operator, who makes the account a user with `grant-role.js <email> user` (which ends its staff keys and sessions, 3a), after which a moderator who is not a party decides the case;
      - a count of waiting mails that stays above zero means the relay cannot take mail: check the API log for `MailUnavailable`; if it shows none, the dispatch fails before the send (its daily step, or a contact it cannot read): read the API log for `moderation-dispatch` and fix it the same day. A count of mails given up means the relay answered that an address does not exist (RFC 3463 5.1.1, 5.1.2, 5.1.3, 5.1.6 or 5.1.10), or a mail stayed unsent for five days; korbat does not try it again, the person still sees the outcome on the status page or in the app, and the API log names each one (`moderation: mail given up`). A relay that refuses korbat itself (relaying denied, missing credentials, a sender it refuses, a quota) raises the count of waiting mails instead, with `MailUnavailable` in the log: fix `API_SMTP_URL` or the relay within five days and nothing is lost;
      - a count of decisions not released means the daily release keeps failing: notifiers are not told and re-examination windows do not open, so read the API log for `moderation-dispatch` and fix it the same day. A count of late erasures names the due rule whose step keeps failing, so evidence, an intake record or a notifier's name and address outlives its published period: read the API log for `moderation-retention` and for `auth janitor: purge failed`, and check that `API_MODERATION_RETENTION` was not left `off` after a restore (the API logs it at every start);
      - a GDPR access request at the contact address: confirm that the requester holds the account (a mail from its address) or the email (the answer goes only to that address); run `staff.js access-request`; for every case it marks for the authorities' check, look in your R2, Art. 18 and order records. A case still open with a lane-0 item first goes through its R2 or Art. 18 step. For a case reported to an authority, or one an order concerns, ask that authority whether telling the person now would harm its work, and hold that case's part of the answer until it answers or counsel advises (GDPR Art. 23(1)(d) and the national law that applies; DSA Art. 9(5), 10(5)), within the periods of Art. 12(3); a person decides each item marked for weighing and records why; reply within one month (GDPR Art. 12(3)), saying what was withheld and why; keep the draft with the reply for the period counsel sets, since a withheld item may have to go before the supervisory authority or a court (C-203/22); any other request under GDPR Articles 16 to 22 at the contact address: confirm the requester as for an access request; answer within one month (Art. 12(3)), saying what korbat did or, when it does nothing, why, and that the person may complain to a supervisory authority or go to court (Art. 12(4)); evidence that a case still needs is kept as `law-3c.md` 9.7 says (GDPR Art. 17(3)(b) and (e), 21(1)); no answer names a reporter or notifier, quotes a report or a notice, or says whether a given person reported;
      - checking `text-verify` with `audit-verify` after each deploy and each week.
      Records the operator keeps off the server, beside 3a's list of staff: each staff member's linked personal handles, updated at every `staff.js link` and `unlink`; every R2 report with the post's or the account's address, the case, the seal's time and the authority's reference; what `staff.js account-facts` printed for a case on that path, kept until the admin's answer and destroyed after an answer without a seal; every act done by hand on sealed files on an authority's written instruction (what, when, on which instruction); every authority's order received, its verification, the answers sent and what was done (7.14); and the numbers last set with `staff.js limits`. Under Before going public, first, the hard gate: "korbat is not opened to the public and sign-up is not opened to people the operator does not know until sub-project 3e's order path and sub-project 3d's handling of sealed images have both shipped, and no authorities' contact point is published until 3e's order path ships. The TCO Regulation applies to a host insofar as it disseminates information to the public (Art. 1(2)) and its removal orders must be executed within one hour (Art. 3); DSA Art. 9 and 10 orders presuppose a service offered in the Union; 3c cannot meet either. 3c can seal images reported as child sexual abuse material, but it has no function to hand them over, to erase them or to carry a seal across a restore. Moving sub-project 4 before 3e or before 3d reopens this." Then: the contact addresses (users' and, with 3e, authorities') published, the authorities' one with the languages it may be addressed in, among them an official language of korbat's home state (DSA Art. 11(3), TCO Art. 15(2)), and notified to the national authorities where the home state requires it (`law-3c.md` 8.9, 8.11); the users' contact mailbox set to load no remote content, and to keep deleted mail no longer than its host's shortest setting; the dispatch hour (`API_MODERATION_DISPATCH_HOUR`, UTC); `NOTIFIER_ENC_KEY` generated, backed up off the server and rotated like `TOTP_ENC_KEY`; the national police's and Europol's Art. 18 channels obtained and recorded; the existing `API_CONSOLE_ORIGIN` bullet (`README.md:169`) gains "From sub-project 3c only the console decides reports and notices; with it off they are taken and wait, and staff are alerted to them." Counsel's list gains the period for which an access request's draft is kept, whether a removal of terrorist content on a notice makes korbat a provider that has taken action under TCO Art. 7(2) (`law-3c.md` 8.6), whether a final decision on content its author already deleted is mailed (section 2), and whether, and under which national law, an access request may be restricted or deferred for a case korbat reported to an authority under DSA Art. 18 or for images it sealed for good, or that an authority's order concerns (GDPR Art. 23(1)(d)), what the answer and any extension under Art. 12(3) then say, and whether it names those authorities as recipients (Art. 15(1)(c)); whether 30 days suits the content backups hold; and every question that section 2 flags for counsel on the path for child sexual abuse material (rows Suspected child sexual abuse material, Evidence, What the author sees of removed content, Case targets and measures). The README's Restoring section gains, after its existing `MEDIA_RECONCILE` paragraph and before the staff steps, the sentences "Keep no backup of the database or of `media-store` more than 30 days after it was taken (`RETENTION.backupDays`, which the terms publish), and delete older ones, except a backup that korbat must keep for the authorities (step 5), which you keep until they have answered. A backup holds what korbat erased after it was taken, among it content erased under rule R4.", and a fifth step: "5. Moderation. Every decision, review and seal confirmed after the backup is gone with its audit rows, and what it restricted is back: sealed images are served again, and a removed post is public again. Reports, notices and re-examinations received after the backup are gone too, and so is everything `staff.js` wrote since: links and unlinks and limits. So are the stops that people pressed on a report's page after the backup, and the report mail that accounts resumed in Settings since; korbat may mail such an address about reports again, though its holder asked it to stop, until they press stop again, and an account that resumed its report mail gets none until it resumes again. Nothing on the server or in your records names them, so none can be applied again. Until 3e's off-server copy of decisions, nothing on the server names the lost decisions either. So before the api service starts again after step 3, take the app's public hostname off the tunnel and leave the console's on (with `TUNNEL=off`, close the port that reaches Caddy's 8080 to everyone but yourself). Before it starts, also delete the moderation dispatch's daily markers, which live in Valkey and are not restored with the database: `docker compose -f infra/compose.yml exec -T redis sh -c 'export VALKEYCLI_AUTH="$REDIS_PASSWORD"; valkey-cli --scan --pattern "*moderation-dispatched:*" | while read -r k; do valkey-cli del "$k"; done'`. The dispatch's first round then releases again every outcome and changed outcome that was answered before the day's dispatch hour and not yet released at the backup, so the status pages, the re-examination windows and the mails agree again; a person whose outcome mail or statement went out after the backup's time gets it a second time. Before it starts, also set `API_MODERATION_RETENTION=off` in `infra/.env`. The restored database no longer holds the cases, freezes and seals made after the backup. From its first round, 10 minutes after the start, the retention job would make R4 redactions final, erase case evidence and delete the images that these cases, freezes and seals no longer protect. That includes images sealed and reported to the police since the backup, long before an admin has enrolled and sealed them again. While the switch is off nothing is erased or deleted, and the console's count of late erasures rises. Before anyone decides anything, run `staff.js link` and `staff.js unlink` until the links match your records, and check that the count `staff.js keys` prints for each staff account equals your records'; then run `staff.js limits` with the numbers you last set. Once the staff have enrolled again, an admin first opens a finding citing R2 on every post or account that your R2 records show was sealed after the backup's time, unless its case is open in the restored database, and seals its images again. Only then, once every such seal has been applied again, set `API_MODERATION_RETENTION=on` in `infra/.env` and run `docker compose -f infra/compose.yml up -d api`, which recreates the API with it, and put the app's public hostname back on the tunnel. Its next rounds make up the erasures that fell due meanwhile. Then tell the authority each of these was reported to what was lost and when it was sealed again, and keep the backup you restored from until that authority has answered, also past the 30 days. Sub-project 3d adds the release of sealed images and its replay after a restore. Then the cases that were open at the backup are open again in the queue: decide them again at once, lane 0 first. Each moderator also opens a finding on any other target they remember restricting since the backup's time."
    - (b) The README lines 3c makes false (reputation, the moderation states nothing sets, "relies on sub-project 3's moderation states", `README.md:176`).
    - (c) A dated amendment in the main design's section 9 (section 15's bullets).
    - (d) Sections "Changed by 3c" in the 2a design (write limits stay on age; the author's chip; `postAccess` fails closed), the 2b design (images follow their own state; holds, the freeze and sealed images; the disposal predicate; the sweeper and retained rows; `post_media_guard`; no DELETE on `posts` or `profiles` for the API's role), the 1b design (the export's member, and no sealed image in the export; the purge taking its posts in id order and its images last; deletion leaves held evidence; reporters forgotten; the take-down's mark is the database's time; an old handle names its account on the path for child sexual abuse material and nowhere else; a report shows back the handle as it was named, never a later one), the 2c design (unchanged formula until 3e; R6) and the 3a design (the three exceptions to the step-up rule; "followed only by deferred checks that read"; `ConsolePath`; the staff answers' new shapes; `korbat_auth` can no longer clear or back-date `auth.users.deleted_at` (0018's `users_deleted_mark`), so 0013's note that it may is superseded).
    - (e) This design's amendments from the dry run.

## 14. Success criteria

1. Anyone can report a post, an image or a profile as breaking a rule or as illegal, signed in or not, and the CSAM path stores no account and no contact; every report or notice lands, as its own row with its own receipt and outcome, in exactly one open case per target, which the database enforces under concurrency.
2. No post or image is restricted or released except as the database derives it from the measures that decisions recorded, each decision confirmed by the deciding person's staff key in the same transaction, which the database refuses otherwise; the result does not depend on the order in which cases decide; every decision is named by an audit row that `audit-verify` checks, whose actor, target and params equal the decision and its actions (checked at commit and again by `text-verify`, which also recomputes every level from the ledger).
3. Every restriction reaches its author at once with every Art. 17(3) element, the content actioned and its redress (the appeal for a final restriction; the interim text for a measure taken while deciding), says when it changed nothing anyone sees, and is rendered from versioned rules exactly as the moderator previewed it; every end of a measure taken while deciding is told; no author-facing surface carries a reporter, a report's words or a count.
4. Evidence survives the author's deletion, the account's deletion and the sweeper while its case needs it, and each kind of data is erased on its own published schedule; no image and no snapshot of a frozen post is deleted or erased, and no staff member is served such an image; a sealed image is never served, shown or exported to anyone, its author included, and is kept until sub-project 3d releases it.
5. Every final restriction can be appealed for six months by the person it is about, while any of it is in force, and the database refuses a review by anyone who decided in the case or opened a finding in it; every decision that took no action on a notifier's or reporter's item (`no_violation` or `not_evident`) can be re-examined once, within six months of its release.
6. The chain never holds a personal free text (it holds no typed text at all), a reporter's or notifier's id or a snapshot's content, and `text-verify` proves that every text still held is the one a key confirmed.
7. The console runs under 3a's policy unchanged, with no violation in the browser check over every new screen with hostile fixtures, and images reach it only from its own origin, only when revealed, and never while frozen or sealed.

## 15. Changes to the main design

Each promise of `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-03-korbat-design.md` this design changes, with the reason; Task 12 writes them as the main design's amendment.

- **Context (line 5) and section 4's rulebook (line 167):** "Everything else stays up" and "the complete list of reasons" become "everything else stays up unless it is illegal under EU or national law and korbat is told so in a notice" (an authority's order joins with 3e). Reason: Art. 16 notices on a legal ground bind korbat, and keeping evidently illegal content up after a notice costs the hosting shield (`law-3c.md` 1.4, 1.5). Removals for law name the law, never a rule, and are counted apart.
- **Context, line 10 ("Every removal cites a public rule, shows the author exactly what happened, and can be appealed to a different human"):** a removal on a legal ground cites the law; images reported as child sexual abuse material can be sealed without anyone at korbat having opened them, and are then shown to nobody, their author included, while the post's text stays, and an R4 body is blanked once the removal is final; what korbat does while it decides cannot be appealed and ends with the case's final decision, which can be (a seal taken while deciding ends with the admin's answer to the case's suspect items, 6.1); people who reported get a re-examination of "no action" instead of an appeal. Reason: Art. 16 notices (`law-3c.md` 1.5), Directive 2011/93/EU Art. 5, GDPR Art. 15(4), and one appeal shape (section 2, rows What the author sees of removed content, Appeals and re-examination).
- **Section 2, trust and safety (lines 105-111):** `reports`, `cases` with one `action`, `appeals` one per case, `strikes` and `transparency_log` are replaced by intakes, items, cases, decisions with several measures in a ledger from which every restriction is derived, the author's appeals per decision, re-examination items, and evidence (section 6); strikes are 3e's; `transparency_log` becomes 3d's to define in the Transparency Database's shape. Reason: none of them could hold a notice from someone without an account, a decision with several measures, or a statement's elements (`repo-3c.md` 1.6).
- **Lines 146, 179, 183-184 (reputation, weighting, automation raising priority):** reports are not weighted, reputation is not computed and its column is dropped, and the queue orders by severity and age; automation in 3d may flag, never weight. Reason: weighting slows the legal path for strangers by design, rewards aged brigades, and turns strikes into hidden penalties (G9 3.8; T53). Write limits stay on account age (also 2a and 2b).
- **Line 184 ("R2/R5 go to a senior queue"):** every final decision that answers a report or finding of child sexual abuse material or an R5 item, or that cites R2 or R5, whatever its outcome, every lifting of a seal, and every correction of such a decision, is an admin's, and so is the review of a decision that cites R2 or sealed images; a review of an R5 restriction is any eligible moderator's. Any moderator may withhold such content, or seal reported images, while deciding, and they sit in lane 0; brigading-flagged cases are 3d's. Reason: a team of one or two has no separate senior moderators.
- **Line 185 ("Decision: moderator picks an action ... must write a public_reason"; "suspension requires 3 active strikes or an R2/R5 case"):** a decision takes several measures, carries a statement of reasons whose every element is data, and names a snapshot. The promise about strikes and suspension moves wholly to 3e (P7). "The author sees the exact content that was actioned" holds while korbat keeps the case's copies (6.6: until every restriction of the case is final for its author, about six months after they were told when they do not appeal), except for sealed images (the author sees the decision and the post's text, and each sealed image as a placeholder) and R4 once final (the body blanked, the chip and reason kept). Afterwards the decision screen says on which day korbat's copy was erased, and a removed post or image itself stays in its author's own view while they keep it (line 191).
- **Line 186 ("One appeal per case ... reviewed by a different moderator"):** one appeal per final restriction, by its author, for six months, while any of it is in force; the database enforces the different reviewer; with one moderator, appeals wait; measures taken while deciding are not appealable and end with the case's final decision, a seal taken while deciding with the admin's answer to the case's suspect items (6.1); while a post is frozen, a review or a correction that turns on one of its images is decided on text alone or waits until an admin has answered the suspect items (6.1, the freeze); notifiers and reporters may ask once for a re-examination of "no action", and an answer given while nobody could open the post's images does not spend that request (7.2; P5; recital 58's aim, Art. 20 not binding, Art. 19(1)).
- **Line 190 ("Under review for R1, usually within 24h"):** a withheld post shows the rule of the interim decision that withheld it; the README publishes response-time targets per lane (7.12), every staff account is alerted when a case passes them, and they are targets, not promises.
- **Line 190 (a withheld post's content stays visible to its author):** holds, except for images sealed while deciding on a report or finding of child sexual abuse material, which nobody is shown, their author included, from the first seal. Reason: serving a file reported as such material back to its uploader is making it available (Directive 2011/93/EU Art. 5(5)). Flagged for the owner, who may choose r7's form, in which the author loses sight only when an admin seals for good (section 2, row Recusal and who may decide).
- **Line 191 (removed posts stay in the author's view):** holds, except R4 (redacted when final). A post whose images are sealed stays in its author's views and export with its text; only the sealed images are shown to nobody, the author included. Reason: the seal is a state of images, and text is never sealed (section 2, row What the author sees of removed content); r7's amendment of this line, which hid a sealed post from its author entirely, is withdrawn.
- **Line 192 ("Suspension shows the case list"):** 3e's.
- **Line 174 (R4: "Withhold first when the report is credible"):** a moderator's interim decision, never report volume.
- **Line 172 (R2: "Cloudflare CSAM hash match -> auto-remove, account suspended, NCMEC report"):** until 3d, whoever is on duty seals reported images by hand without opening them, and an admin seals them for good or answers without a seal, and the operator reports every seal for good to the national police or the authority its Member State names (never a hotline, section 2), not NCMEC; suspension is 3e's; the hash match and an automatic seal are 3d's.
- **Line 176 (R6: "Reputation signals help triage"):** reputation no longer exists; R6 cases are ordered like every other case, by lane and age.
- **Section 6, row 3 and the 2026-10-01 amendment:** 3c is split into 3c and 3e; the order becomes 3c, 3e, 3d, 3b, 2d, 4, 5; korbat is not opened to the public, nor sign-up to strangers, nor an authorities' contact point published, before 3e's order path, and neither of the first two before 3d's handling of sealed images (section 2's first row; section 17).
- **Section 3d ("Every action lands in the append-only audit_log"):** holds; decisions, reviews and reveals are step-up rows naming their decision, and the chain holds only ids, enums, times and salted digests.

## 16. 3e, account sanctions and authorities' orders (scope only)

A design of its own, after 3c and before 3d, dry-run, reviewed and replayed on its own. What it covers, from the research already done:

- **Strikes and handle retirement** (moved from 3c, P7): `action_kind` gains `strike`, `overrule`, `retire_handle` and `unretire_handle` with `ALTER TYPE ... ADD VALUE`, as ledger measures and reversals; a strike's `expires_at` (90 days after its decision, written by the database) and the `active_strikes` view; a strike only with a breach of a korbat rule, never with a decision on the law alone; `handle_history.retired_at`, a retired handle refused to everyone, its former holder included, by `changeHandle` and onboarding, and the rules that a retirement comes first in its decision and that its reversal gives the handle back only while the profile holds the placeholder; their statements (a new rules or redress version), their lines on the chips and the Cases screen, the export's strikes, the terms' section "Strikes"; G6's row 14 (strike history after expiry); and the main design's line 185. 3c's decisions carry no strike, so the ladder counts from 3e's launch; nothing is public before 3e, so nothing is lost. Strikes never reach notifiers' lines (T43).
- **Suspensions** (G5, G7): `suspensions` and `suspension_lifts`, insert-only, with start and end written by the database; `profiles.suspended_from` and `suspended_until` derived by a trigger and kept off the API role by column grants; one predicate "live and not suspended" read from the locked profile row in `lockLive`, profile edits, the handle change and uploads (made one statement), exported for 2d and 3b; the suspension taken under `FOR UPDATE` on the profile as the decision's first lock, and the follow, unfollow, post and deletion paths reordered profile-first (G5's list); staff bridges both ways (`account_is_staff` from 3c; `auth.account_suspended` for grant-role). The flow: a third active strike lets a moderator propose a suspension (3, 7 or 30 days within 12 months, shorter allowed), mailed and shown as a notice with a 72-hour reply window during which nothing is restricted; a reply cancels the scheduled start and returns the case to the queue; no reply starts it at the deadline on the moderator's signed proposal; immediate suspensions only for a closed list (R2, R5, terrorist content, automated spam floods); the statement names the Explore effect; the account keeps sign-in, its cases, appeals, export and deletion, blocks and mutes, and its posts stay visible (G7 6.4, 6.5). Termination (closed like a deleted account after its appeal window) and the restriction of reporting (Art. 23-style, after a warning, never for Art. 16 notices, computed from per-account counts, since 3c forgets who reported once an intake record is erased) are its other account measures. New step-up actions and mails; the route-walk test as a suspended account. Whether an account whose images are sealed can be deleted before korbat has reported it (a hold on the auth row) is decided here, with suspensions.
- **Explore formula version 2** (G10): suspended authors are not candidates and suspended engagers' likes and reposts do not count, judged at the round by the database's clock; restored posts resume their window and age (`posts.hidden_at`, `restored_at`, `explore_from` written by a trigger, `explore_from` = `created_at` plus every hidden span, read from the ledger's measures in force); `EXPLORE_V2` replaces `EXPLORE_V1`, the skip rule counts only rankings of the current version, the status gains `suspended`, and How Explore works and the change log say so.
- **Authorities' orders** (G8, `law-3c.md` 4, 8; moved from 3c, P6): the contact point as a mailbox at an EU mail host watched by a small `notify` service over IMAP IDLE (headers only; `received_at` from `INTERNALDATE`); a self-hosted ntfy for phone alerts (priority 5, escalation to the second person at 15 minutes without a staff sign-in, a reminder at 45, a daily test), which also carries the lane-0 and queue-age alerts that 3c sends by mail; `legal_orders` and their append-only events with the clock (an Annex III stops it; a fresh hour after the grounds cease, TCO Art. 3(7), 3(8)); one console step-up action "Disable for an order" usable from a phone with a security key (a `disable_by_order` measure in the ledger that keeps the item reinstatable, hides it from its author and its export, and preserves it six months, TCO Art. 6, with extensions on an authority's or court's request); that preservation period as an entry of `RETENTION` and the terms' sentence on it in "Terrorist content" ("content removed under an order is kept for six months as the Regulation requires"); `decision_source` `order` and an order's redress text in a new redress version, its outcome phrase for notifiers, lane 0 for an order's item, and its admin rules (who may decline an order on its withdrawal or annulment, TCO Art. 4(7)); the Annex II and III records as step-up actions; the 48-hour scrutiny decision for an order from another Member State (TCO Art. 4(4)); the order's document, verification, non-disclosure period (TCO Art. 11 information and non-disclosure: 42 days plus one extension of 42, ends set by the database; DSA Art. 9(5) and 10(5) information to the person concerned) and productions only from the command line, among them the hand-over of sealed images on an authority's request, with its record (section 17); the author's notice held only as the database allows; whether recusal's relation test (`staff_party_conflict`, 6.2) applies to a decision that gives effect to an order, which leaves no discretion, so that the hour never waits for a second staff member; the TCO Art. 7(2) counts of orders; and the restore step that re-applies orders given effect after a backup.
- **Records and recovery:** Art. 18 and TCO Art. 14(5) notification records, among them the record of every report of sealed images to an authority (which seal, which authority, their reference, when); `reverse-window`; an off-server daily copy of decisions for the keeper (C37) and the restore step that re-applies them.
- **What 3c leaves ready for it:** account cases and their decisions, the ledger and `moderation_apply` (a new measure only joins the maximum), the `account_is_staff` bridge, the enums that `ADD VALUE` extends, `append_confirmed_audit`'s action list (a constant 3e extends), holds, the freeze and `seal_media` (6.1, 6.2), `case_access_log`, the lane-0 alert seam, `KEYWORD_TERRORIST_CONTENT` on decisions answering illegal-ground notices, the README's hard gate, and the terms' sections that 3e completes with strikes, suspensions and the orders path.

## 17. Left for 3d: sealed images and the public pages (scope only)

What 3c leaves for 3d beside the path below: the public transparency and rules pages built from `decisions` (every enumerated field in the Transparency Database's shape, `puid`, days for publication, no user identifier in any of them), R1 scoring writing measures through automated decisions, upload hashing, the Cloudflare cache purge on every restriction and every seal, brigading signals that defer or flag cases, the Art. 24(3) count of average monthly active recipients, kept without personal data and ready to give on request, and the head's publication.

**The gate.** korbat is not opened to the public, and sign-up is not opened to people the operator does not know, until 3e's order path and 3d's handling of sealed images have both shipped (section 2, row Splitting 3c; the README's Before going public). Until then a seal can only come from a tester's upload or from a false report on a private instance. Should an admin uphold one, the runbook's R2 paragraph applies: the operator reports it, and acts on the files only by hand, on the authority's written instruction.

**What moved out of 3c**, each with the finding that showed 3c could not carry it alone: the release of sealed images (a release function, its command and a release mark on image rows), whose replay after a restore covered only seals taken after the backup (review round 6, r6-6); the handling of released material in backups (r6-8); and every step of the restore that depended on a release. 3c keeps the protective half: the freeze, the seal, the admin's answer, the alert, `MODERATION_RETENTION` and the Restoring step that takes lost seals again from the operator's records.

**Which sub-project owns what.** 3e, which ships first (section 16): the record of every report of sealed images to an authority, the hand-over on an authority's request, the off-server copy of decisions with the restore step that applies them again, the phone alert, and the decision whether an account whose images are sealed can be deleted before the report is made. 3d: the automatic seal, an automated decision holding `seal_media` on a hash match, the main design's one automated removal, with a statement that says so (Art. 17(3)(c)), and the Cloudflare purge on every seal; the quarantine: the files of sealed images moved out of `media-store` and out of the API's reach, which also ends the residual that an API under an attacker's control can read them or request their deletion; the release: a one-way end of a seal, recorded with the authority's reference, and its replay after a restore, in which every release recorded after the backup's time is run again for every seal the restored database holds, whenever that seal was taken; how long a backup that holds released images may live, and whether it must be destroyed at the release; the period for which sealed images are kept, and its legal basis, with counsel. An upload on no post is never reached by the freeze (6.1). A hash match found before the image is on a post has nothing to freeze, and the sweeper deletes the upload after a day: 3d holds or refuses such an upload itself.

**What 3d receives.** `intake_items.suspect`, which is what makes a report freeze: a hash match is a finding citing R2 with `automated_detection` true, and it freezes by the same rule. `public.frozen_for` and `public.post_frozen`, the one predicate, with their callers pinned. `seal_media`, `unseal_media` and `seal_posts`: what each seal covers, post by post, with image counts. The image state `sealed` and gate S; gate K (`post_media_keep`) and gate E (`erase_snapshots`). `decisions.suspect`, `decisions.unseen` (which a post frozen through an automatic seal sets by the same rule), `needs_admin`, `moderation_csam_lifted` and the home screen's lists. The enums that `ADD VALUE` extends and `append_confirmed_audit`'s action list.

**How the release fits without changing a table of 3c.** One clause in the seal arm of `frozen_for`: a post is frozen through a seal while that seal is in force and has not released it. The mark is a table of 3d's own, insert-only, keyed on `seal_posts`' key, with the authority's reference. The release sets `post_media.erased_at` on the listed posts' rows, so gate V and `serve` refuse them and the disposal's existing predicate takes the rows. Gates K and E, the R4 redaction, the disposal's candidates and the case answer then follow with no clause of their own. 3d extends two pins of 3c's tests: the writers of `post_media.erased_at` and the callers of `post_frozen`.

**What the automatic seal needs.** A decision of 3c needs a staff profile as `decided_by`, passes the role and recusal checks of `decisions_record` steps 5 to 7, and is linked at commit to a step-up row of the audit log (`decisions_check` (a)); `automated_decision` is forced to `not_automated`, and a finding needs `finding_by`. So 3d replaces `decisions_record` (steps 5 to 7 and the forced `automated_decision`) and `decisions_check` (a), gives the system a decider (the system actor has no profile), and adds an intake kind or relaxes `intakes_finding`. The freeze, `seal_media` and `seal_posts` need no change for it.

## 18. Amendments from the dry run (2026-10-12)

The dry run built this design task by task in a scratch clone (2026-10-03 to 2026-10-12). Where the code had to differ from the text above, or the text left something open that the code settled, the rule the code follows is recorded here; where a bullet and an earlier section disagree, the bullet is the later and verified word.

- **The statement checks (6.1, `intake_items_statement` and `appeals_statement`).** Each requires `statement_digest is not null` beside the equality with the computed digest: with a NULL digest the equality alone is NULL, and PostgreSQL accepted a statement with no digest, also on an item that is no re-examination.
- **Two checks written null-safe (6.1).** `intake_items_case` is `case_id is not null or outcome is not distinct from 'not_available'` and `decisions_keyword` is `category_keyword is null or ground is not distinct from 'illegal'`: with `=` each was NULL, and so passed, for a null outcome or a null ground. The attach trigger still refuses a case-less item with a null outcome, with SQLSTATE 23514 naming the constraint. The catalogue prints the form as `NOT (x IS DISTINCT FROM y)`, and the pin expects that.
- **`post_media_insert` refuses an attached row (6.1, the freeze).** It raises `moderation_column` (42501) for every role when a row is inserted with `post_id` set: an image row is created on no post and put on one only by `attach`'s update, which `post_media_guard` checks, so the owner of an attached row is its post's author by construction also against a direct insert.
- **`staff_texts_digest` holds the salt to its form (6.1).** The check includes `salt ~ '^[0-9a-f]{64}$'`, as the statement checks do; an empty or upper-case salt with a matching digest passed before.
- **The limits row is the migration's (6.1).** The integration setup neither truncates `moderation_limits` nor writes its row, so the row a test reads is the one migration 0015 inserted.
- **The image readers of Task 1 (7.10, section 13).** `serve`, `forPosts`, `readyImages`, `find` and `status` give only an image whose own state is `visible`, so an image's own `withheld` or `removed` reaches nobody until Task 3 teaches the readers; `find` and `status` answer a kept row of a deleted or erased post as a row that does not exist, a clause section 13 listed under Task 3 and Task 1 builds with its tests. `MediaStatusResponse`'s ready variant has `blurhash` optional (section 10), since a sealed image is answered without it.
- **`owner_id` (section 12).** A change of `owner_id` is refused for `korbat_api` by its column grant ("permission denied for table post_media") and for the migrator by `post_media_guard` (`moderation_column`).
- **Keys, names and bounds (6.1).** Every public moderation table's uuid key defaults to `gen_random_uuid()` (`auth.notifier_contacts.id` has none). Checks and indexes the design left unnamed got names (`cases_lane`, `intakes_ref`, `intake_items_case_seq_idx` and others, pinned by the schema test). Three text bounds are CHECKs: `intake_items_statement_len`, `appeals_statement_len` and `staff_texts_text_len`, the last in code points, an outer bound of the contracts' UTF-16 count. `decision_actions_once` is a UNIQUE NULLS NOT DISTINCT constraint.
- **`measures_in_force` (6.1).** Its decision's id, kind and case are the columns `decision_id`, `decision_kind` and `case_id`. A `REVOKE ALL` precedes its `GRANT SELECT`, because the default privileges of `0001_grants.sql` cover views.
- **`laneFor` (section 10).** The category counts only where `allegesIllegal` is true; elsewhere the rule alone decides the lane. So a finding citing R1 with the category Violence is lane 2. Flagged (owner).
- **`MEASURES` and `RETENTION` (section 10).** A `MEASURES` entry is `{ interim, final, lifts, target, label }`; the twelve labels are section 8's words ("Remove", "Remove one image" and so on) and the contracts' test pins all twelve. `RETENTION`'s member names are the implementation's apart from `backupDays`; `evidenceHours` (24) is 6.6's day.
- **The attach trigger's order (6.1).** For a re-examination it locks the intake first and reads it once afterwards for steps 1 and 2; an item naming no intake raises 23503. `intake_items_quote` (b) also requires the added item's own target to be a post.
- **`appealDeadline` against PostgreSQL (section 12)** is an integration test (288 instants over two years) beside the unit vectors, since the comparison needs the database.
- **Stricter helpers (section 10).** `textDigest` refuses a malformed salt and a lone surrogate; `snapshotText` refuses a time that is not in `toISOString`'s form; `sortedJson`'s error says "a canonical text".
- **The development cleaner (Task 1).** Beside the accounts the design lists it keeps the finder of an intake that stays and a staff text's author or an access row's staff in a case that stays, repeated until it adds nobody, and counts them as kept: their `on delete restrict` keys would otherwise fail the whole cleanup. It names tables of 0015, so it runs only against a database at 0015 or later.
- **Races that need a decision (section 12).** "A report against a closing case retried into a new one" needs a committed close, which only a decision makes: Task 1 tests the refusal and the retry inside a rolled-back transaction and an item waiting on a held case, and the committed race is Task 2's. "A re-examination racing `erase_intake`" is Task 9's. The re-examination tests and the `decisions_keyword` test write an answered item or a decision as the migrator (`fabricateDecision`, `answerItem` in `moderation-fixtures.ts`) until Task 2's triggers land, which fire for the migrator too; Task 2 replaces them by real decisions. The freeze's predicate under a seal and the cascade for a row frozen through a seal are tested in Task 2.
- **Database tests clean up (section 12).** Single-session database tests run inside a transaction that ends in a rollback; a two-session test, which must commit, deletes what it committed in a `finally`; each new file's last test compares the row counts of the moderation tables with those before the file and expects no difference. Every explicit transaction sets `lock_timeout`.
- **`scripts/test-db.mjs --upgrade-from` (Task 1)** removes its temporary copy of the migrations also when a migration fails: `migrate()` throws and the script exits after the `finally`.
- **The deferred check is queued again for every ledger row (section 3, 6.2, section 11).** The check of a decision against its audit row is one owner-only function, `public.decision_holds(decision)`, called by `decisions_check` for the decision's row and by a second deferred constraint trigger, `decision_actions_recheck`, for every `decision_actions` row. Before, a caller could run the one queued check early with `SET CONSTRAINTS ... IMMEDIATE` and then add a measure that committed outside its audit row's params: section 3's "never skip them" was not true of a row added after the early check. A decision with N ledger rows now runs the check N+1 times at COMMIT.
- **3a's chain vector (section 12).** `case_decided` is a step-up action from Task 2 on, so `audit-verify` reports 3a's pinned "nested metadata" vector, a `case_decided` row with no step-up: `audit-verify.test.ts` expects two problems there, and the vector, whose hash is pinned, carries a comment that says why.
- **The moderation repository's inserts are raw SQL (section 5).** Not Drizzle builders: a builder names every column, the ones the triggers force included, and 6.1 wants that no insert must name a forced column.
- **The retry on a closed case (6.1, 7.1 step 4.3), as measured.** Once a decision has closed the case in its open transaction, a report waits at `insert into cases ... on conflict do nothing` and opens a new case with no retry; the retry on 40001 is needed only when the decision has locked the case and not yet closed it. Both paths are tested.
- **The newest snapshot (6.1, 7.3).** Snapshot ids are `uuidv7()` and "newest" is `order by taken_at desc, id desc`: two snapshots of one case in one transaction share `taken_at`.
- **The redress texts (section 10).** `REDRESS_VERSION` 1 holds three texts (`REDRESS_TEXTS`, read with `getRedress`); the appeal's wording, with `{deadline}` as its placeholder, is the implementation's and agrees with section 9's Appeals terms; the rules test pins all three word for word, since every decision records the version it was made under.
- **Choices 6.2 left open.** A cut-off in the future is refused with SQLSTATE 22023; `snapshot_withheld` answers true for an unknown id; `decisions_record` forces `redacted_at` to null; a final decision on a closed case is `invalid_decision`; the trigger checks "at most one post measure" itself (a unique violation would otherwise answer 23505); the intake's lock timeout is 10 s; a review is refused in 0016 with the detail "A review waits for the migration that brings appeals."
- **Step 3's version check (7.5)** runs at the start of `decide`, before any read or transaction, not in the route.
- **The contracts in Task 2 (section 10).** `ModerationErrorCode` holds the 12 codes this task raises and grows with the tasks; `CaseDecisionResponse` (`{ decisionId }`) is the route's answer until Task 6 replaces it with the case read; the params schema refines what the database also holds (an image measure names its image; a correction names its decision and carries a reason). A unit test pins each code's status.
- **Post snapshots list no image until Task 3** (`media: []`): the moderation module may not name `post_media`, so the images come with Task 3's hold, which also moves the snapshot inserts after the hold.
