- **`decideForTest(apiDb, postId, state)` (section 13, Task 2)** makes its decision on an admin's own R1 finding, and `closeDecisions` removes what it committed (cases, items, decisions, measures, texts, snapshots, intakes, the staff profile) and derives the posts' levels again; the audit rows and the staff account's auth rows stay, since the chain is append-only. Each of the seven suites that use it ends with the row-count test.
- **Step 4.5's random id (section 13).** The case-less account item's random `target_id` is built in Task 2 with the intake's transaction; Task 4 adds the de-duplication of such links by their named handles.
- **`append_confirmed_audit` and a taken-down account (6.2, section 12).** `decisions_record` reads only the decider's role, so a taken-down staff account's decision passes it; the audit function's own account statement is what ends it (`staff_confirmation_ended`), and Task 2's tests pin that statement and its order before the key's. 3a's verification refuses a challenge with a minute or less left, so section 12's "one met with a minute or less of the challenge left" is reachable over HTTP only when the first bounded wait itself crosses the minute.
- **The contention lab (section 12).** `apps/api/test/lab/contention.lab.ts`, run through `apps/api/vitest.lab.config.ts`, with its numbers in `apps/api/test/lab/README.md`; every measured transaction rolls back. On a machine 17 to 28% busy: an account's seal at 5,000 image posts 420 ms while deciding, 463 ms for the answer that ends it, 503 ms for a seal for good, 465 ms for lifting it; the purge of 20,000 image rows 3.2 s free and 2.9 s frozen; 18 pairs of 40 two-session rounds of a decision against a like, a follow, the author's deletion, the purge, a CSAM notice naming the post and its author's account, and a rule report: no deadlock, no lock timeout, longest transaction 124 ms.
- **Two tests moved to Task 6 (section 12).** Two moderators deciding one case in two committed sessions, and the two-session form of an item that arrives while a seal's decision waits for its locks; Task 2 holds both at database level (`case_changed`; the view's time comparison).
- **`dropDisposable`'s statement (section 5, 7.9).** It is `with batch as materialized (select ... order by m.id limit $1 for update of m) delete from post_media where id in (select id from batch) returning id`: with the limit inside a plain sub-select, a nested-loop plan runs the sub-select again for every row and lets further rows in (one call deleted 60 rows under a limit of 25). Its candidates, and `overdueDeletions`', test the disposal marks before they read the freeze (`case when <marks> then (m.post_id is null or not public.post_frozen(m.post_id)) else false end`), so `post_frozen` is read for marked rows only; a plan pin holds that no scan filter names it. Measured: with nothing disposable a call fell from 3.3 s to 18 ms at 5,000 attached rows and from 14 s to 62 ms at 20,000.
- **`purgeProfile` takes every post of the account first (7.10).** Right after the profile lock: `select id from posts where author_id = $1 order by id for update`, deleted posts included, then the reply and quote counters, then the tombstone update. The counters write the posts the account's posts reply to or quote, which for a self-quote or self-reply are its own, deleted ones too, and a CSAM notice holds deleted posts like any other: with the counters first the purge deadlocked with such a notice 10 times of 10 when staged, and with only live posts locked it still did for a deleted quoted post. PostgreSQL ended the purge each time, not the notice, and the purge job finished it within five minutes. What remains: counters on other accounts' posts come after the locks, and `deletePost` keeps its post-then-quoted-post shape. Random two-session rounds never found the window (200 rounds, no deadlock, on the code that deadlocked when staged), so the lab keeps both kinds of run.
- **A frozen account's deleted posts cost a read of the freeze for each kept row (7.9, 7.3).** About 0.2 ms a row at every disposal round and every `overdueDeletions` call: 1.2 s at 5,000 kept rows and 3.7 to 4.1 s at 20,000, for as long as the freeze lasts, and under a seal for good that has no end. The overview reads `overdueDeletions`, so Task 5 and Task 9 must not read it on every page load of the home screen without a bound. Flagged.
- **Tests that wait for 0017 and 0018 (section 12).** Released holds and erasure marks are written as the migrator inside rolled-back transactions in Task 3; the retention job's steps and the R4 redaction against a frozen post run in Task 9, with the three committed batches and their audit rows.
- **A hidden image is never loaded (section 13, section 9).** "Never opened in the viewer" means never loaded, as 6.1 defines "to open": the grid's placeholder is not a control, and when the viewer steps onto a hidden image it shows the words and loads nothing. In a quote's row the 64-pixel squares show "Hidden" and are hidden from assistive technology, and the sentence stands once as text under the row (the sealed sentence when a hidden image there carries `sealed`).
- **`staffImage`'s answer (section 5).** `{ postId, file } | null`: it opens a held image of a deleted post and a withheld or removed image, and refuses a frozen post's, a sealed, an erased or an unattached image and one that fails the WebP check, which is logged as a store fault. Task 5's route checks the image against its case before it calls and closes the handle on every refusal.
- **The export's second read (7.10, 7.11).** `isExportable` reads the row again when the export opens a file; an image sealed while an archive streams is listed in `missingMedia`, the same answer as for a row deleted meanwhile, so a freeze tells the author nothing and a seal nothing its statement did not.
- **`overdueDeletions` and an unattached upload (section 5).** A purged owner's upload on no post that a hold kept carries none of the three marks and is counted at once; no API path creates such a hold.
- **The contracts in Task 3 (section 10).** `HiddenMedia` is a strict object with the helper `isHiddenMedia`; `MediaView.moderation` is carried and not drawn until Task 7's labels.
- **The lab of the lock statement (section 12).** `apps/api/test/lab/hold.lab.ts`: `MediaForCases.hold` with one owner pair took 21 to 36 ms and about 280 kB of WAL at 5,000 attached rows and 69 to 114 ms and about 1.1 MB at 20,000 (the machine 14 to 40% busy); a whole CSAM notice naming the account 72 to 95 ms and 104 to 238 ms; the purge right after such a notice deleted no row.
- **The row-count rule's edge (section 12).** A change that only wires a new member into a test's setup needs no row-count test. `media-cases.test.ts` first removes, in a committed transaction, the disposable image rows other suites left, so that strays cannot fill a batch.
- **The status answer's `scope` never tells a live target from a dead one (7.2, section 10).** An account item's scope comes from what the notice named and from the item's `suspect` alone, with or without a case: `account_suspect` when it is suspect (and once a released answer held `seal_media`), `account` otherwise. A post item's `quote` scope is shown only with its released outcome, since without the post's row it cannot be known: before the release a live quoting post and a link that names nothing read the same. The page and the app drop the scope sentence for a released `not_available`.
- **When an added item is listed (7.2).** The status answer lists an item korbat added for a quoted post from the first release that shows its origin's outcome or its own; before any release of the pair it holds the named items alone, so a notice about a quoting post, about a plain post and about a link that names no row list the same number of items. An added item answered before its origin is shown with its outcome at its own release, since the page is this path's only channel and its re-examination window runs from then. The API keeps an account item's scope on a released `not_available` item, and the page and the app drop the sentence there (Task 7).
- **What the limits count (7.1 step 2).** They count requests, not stored intakes: every request that passed step 1 and, signed out, the human check is counted before any target is looked up, so one that step 3 refuses (`notice_link_invalid`, `notice_link_offsite`, `invalid_request`, `already_reported`) still counts, and the limit also bounds step 3's lookups. A request that failed through no fault of its sender (`case_closed` after the last retry, an unexpected failure of the transaction) gives its hits back, since the answer tells the sender to send again.
- **The urgent mail's sentence (7.15).** For a count of 1: "1 case in the urgent lane (...) is waiting, since {oldest}."; otherwise the plural sentence. The same rule belongs on `moderation_waiting`'s lines in Task 6.
- **The auth-key pattern (section 13, Task 4).** It also names `NOTIFIER_ENC_KEYS_PREVIOUS` and `TOTP_ENC_KEYS_PREVIOUS`: a contact is never re-encrypted, so after a rotation the previous keys are the only keys of every stored contact. `scripts/gen-dev-env.mjs` needed no change: it already generates any `v1:change-me-key-<name>` placeholder and shares it between the two env files.
- **The dispatch hour's schema (section 13, Task 4).** `blankAsUnset.pipe(z.coerce.number<string>().int().min(0).max(23).default(7))`: without the type argument the coerced number takes `unknown` and the pipe does not typecheck.
- **The moderation module's files (section 5).** `alerts.ts` holds the alerts' throttle and the lane-0 alert; the notice, status and contact flows are in `intake.ts`; `routes.ts` holds the public routes. `createModeration` gains `auth` and `rateLimitStore` in Task 4 and `requireSession` in Task 7.
- **A notice's fields (section 10, section 12).** `explanation`, `citedLaw` and `name` are refused, never changed, when they hold a control character other than the line feed or only spaces; the name's bound is `NOTICE_NAME_MAX` (100); `email` is the contracts' `Email` (trimmed, lower-cased); ids and handles are lower-cased by the schema. "A signed-in notice with an email" is refused by the route (`invalid_request`), not by the schema. The contracts gain `REPORT_RULE_IDS`, `NoticeRef`, `NoticeItemStatus`, `ITEM_OUTCOMES` and `ItemOutcome`, held to the database's enum by a test.
- **A link's host and the items' positions (7.1 steps 3 and 4.3).** The host is compared with its port, the scheme is not, and a link with a user name or password is `notice_link_invalid`. Positions number the stored named targets from 1, after de-duplication and after a signed-in sender's already-reported targets are dropped; the receipt lists the stored named items.
- **`mailNotifier` and its neighbours (section 5).** The 24-hour cap is a key on the address hash in the auth module's key-value store: a failed send gives it back, a refused one keeps it. `stopNotifier` leaves an existing confirmation in place; `mailStaff` answers 0, logged, when the staff accounts cannot be read; a mail token that no key opens matches nothing, logged with the contact's id. `sendReceipt` rebuilds the receipt from the stored notice, so the dispatch can retry it with the same links.
- **Small answers (7.1, 7.2).** `POST /api/notices/contact` answers 204 with no body. `decidedOn` of an item answered at intake is its own day. A signed-in notice on the CSAM path is stored with the channel `signed_in` and no reporter: it records that some account was signed in, never which.
- **Tests left to their own tasks (section 12).** `imagesAllowed`, `contactLost`, the refused reveal and the findings are Task 5's reads and routes; "a case closed between the intake's insert and its read" is held at the transaction by Task 2.
- **The snapshot route's lock order (section 2, Lock order; 7.3).** It takes the post before the case's row: `korbat_api` holds no UPDATE on `cases`, so it cannot lock a case itself, and the case is locked only through the foreign keys of the route's inserts, against a decision's case-then-post. The route's transaction is therefore run again on 40P01 and 55P03, up to three times, like the intake's, and answers 409 `case_closed` when the last run fails. Section 2's row names this one transaction as taking the other order and the retries that absorb it.
- **No staff text is saved empty (7.4).** The text route refuses a text that is empty or holds only white space, for every kind (`invalid_request`), so a confirmed reason, the facts of a decision and a recorded choice not to seal always say something. In the console the reveal's Confirm is off until a category is chosen (it starts unselected) and the trimmed reason is not empty.
- **The staff image route (7.3).** It refuses first an id that no unerased snapshot of the case lists, with the same 404 `image_refused` and no access row, and only then asks `MediaForCases.staffImage`, comparing the image's post with the case's after the call and closing the handle on that refusal: the moderation module may not name the image table, so the post is known only from the answer. No staff route answers HEAD: the group refuses it with 404 `not_found` before the session is read, so a HEAD writes no access row, counts against no limit and opens no file.
- **The console's digest is its own (section 8, 7.4).** `saveStaffText` returns the digest the console computed and refuses a save whose answered digest differs (`invalid_answer`), so the key never confirms a reason the API substituted; the browser check's fixture answers one save with another text's digest.
- **Text of a frozen post is on the page (section 8, 6.1).** A case whose images cannot be opened still shows each image's entry: its number, its size as text and its alt text in an author frame; only the image, its placeholder and "Show image" are left out, under the sentence that says why. On a case citing R3 or R5 the alt texts and an account's live fields open collapsed like the snapshot's texts.
- **Frames and words in the console (section 8).** A frame's label has the form "{what}. Written by {who}" ("Alt text. Written by the author", "Explanation. Written by Reporter A"). The collapsed-text labels are the console's own (`words.ts`: "R5: direct threats" and its R3 counterpart), since the rulebook's titles read differently. A revealed handle or given name stands alone in a frame with "Show hidden characters". A re-examination item reads "Re-examination of item {n} of its notice" (`reexaminesPosition` is the position in its notice, since the item can lie in an earlier, closed case). An address whose host the console cannot parse is listed as "an address this console cannot read". Every refusal's words are in `messages.ts`, one table each.
- **The home screen's overdue line (section 8, Task 12).** `RELEASE_OVERDUE_RUNBOOK` holds the runbook's sentence for decisions not released, shown when the count is above zero ("Decisions not released after 26 hours: {n}. ..."); Task 12's README test holds the runbook to the same constant. The count is of items, as 7.3's `releaseOverdue` has it.
- **A page that fails to read again keeps what it showed (section 8).** Only the first read of a case can put the page into its failed state; after a reveal, a note or a snapshot a failed re-read keeps the page, the revealed name included, and says so in its error line. A refused or failed image puts "That image is not shown." in its placeholder and reads the case again.
- **The queue's refresh (section 8).** The 60-second refresh runs on the idle timer's injected clock and carries no activity mark, pinned by a test; the browser walk allows an unmarked read of the queue.
- **What Task 5's answers hold (section 10).** `StaffOverview` in Task 5: `lanes`, `appealsWaiting`, `reveals { total, items }`, `imagesToday`, `findingsToday`, `mailsWaiting`, `mailsFailed`, `releaseOverdue`, `deferUnproven`. A finding answers 201, a saved text 201, a snapshot 200. The contracts export `FINDING_CATEGORIES` (every category but the not-specified one). The finding form lists rule ids until Task 6 adds the rulebook's titles, and `/appeals` is a placeholder until Task 8.
- **The snapshot route and holds (7.3, 6.4).** A fresh snapshot is taken in the intake's way, through `MediaForCases.hold` with a post pair, so on a closed case whose holds were released it holds the post's images again; the case's evidence rules release them with the snapshot.
- **The reveal (7.3).** The count of the day's reveals is read inside the reveal's transaction, after its access row; the two staff mails are started as background tasks so that the answer never waits for the relay. Over HTTP a signed-out notifier's given name is revealed through the real auth module, and a CSAM item and a notifier without a name are refused (`reveal_refused`).
- **Recusal's residue (section 11).** A recused staff member can tell 403 from 404, still sees the case's row in the queue, and gets the open case's id from a finding on a target they name: the design puts neither the queue nor the finding route under recusal. Flagged (owner).
- **The route-table test's walk (section 11).** It asserts that no schema kind is left unread (a marker for an unknown kind fails it, proved by a deliberate violation), and the HTTP tests hold every raw staff answer strictly equal to its parsed form, so a member outside the contract fails.
- **A seal taken while deciding that would not be in force is refused (6.1, 6.2, 7.5).** A decision's time and an item's time are the starts of their transactions, and `measures_in_force` holds a seal taken while deciding in force only through an unanswered suspect item created no later than the decision. A seal confirmed on a case whose only unanswered suspect items began after the decision's transaction (they committed while it waited for its first locks; the console never builds this decision, since it offers the seal only beside an unanswered suspect item, but another client with a staff key could) would be recorded, not in force, and told to its author, and could be neither lifted nor ended. `decide` therefore asks the view for the seal after the ledger rows and before the audit row and refuses with `case_changed` when the view does not hold it: nothing is stored, the console reads the case again and the person confirms again in a transaction that begins after the item. Migration 0017 (Task 8) gives the database the same refusal in `decision_actions_apply`, and the API's read goes. Recorded residue: an item whose transaction began before a seal taken while deciding and that attaches after an admin's answer ended that seal makes the view list the seal again; it needs an intake transaction open across the seal's commit and the admin's key confirmation, the outcome fails closed, and the admin's next answer ends it.
- **What follows a decision's commit (7.3, 7.5 step 4).** The person's decisions of the last 15 minutes are counted from `decisions` by the database's clock, not in Valkey (a fixed window in the store would let 38 decisions in 15 minutes pass without the mail); the alert's throttle stays in the limit store under `moderation-burst:<staff account id>` for one hour. The route's answer is the case read after the commit: logged as a case read, and exempt from the daily limit whether or not the person had opened the case that day. Step 4 runs through the module's background tasks, each part in a try of its own, and the API's shutdown waits up to five seconds for them.
- **The statement's input and sentences (section 10).** The contracts hold `DecisionStatement`, what `statementParagraphs` renders: the decision's kind, outcome, ground, rule, versions, texts, source, time and post; its measures with `noEffect` and a seal's `sealed { posts, images, deletedImages }`; its reversals and ended measures with `resetDecisionId`, `oldValueKept` and `resetRuleId`; and `reviewed { kind, ground, ruleId, legalReference, decidedAt, rulesVersion }`, so that a review or a correction renders the reviewed decision's rule under that decision's rulebook version. Task 7's `DecisionView` extends it. Its `facts` and `reason` are read only for a decision that restricts, a correction or a review: the facts and the reason of a decision without a measure are the case's record, for staff, and reach neither the mail nor the author's screen nor the export. The seal's sentences read in the singular for one: "({images} on 1 post)" and "1 of them is on a post you had deleted". A seal's numbers in the preview come from `reach` and in the statement from `seal_posts`, so they can differ, and the composer says so under the preview.
- **The mails of Task 6 (section 5, 7.15, section 12).** `moderation_csam_lifted` leaves the cross-reference "(6.1)" out of its sentence. `moderation_waiting` may carry `interimCount` alone (a measure taken while deciding that is a day old while no lane is past its time), and its interim sentence reads in the singular for one, as the lane lines do. In `notice_update` the sentence "No automated means were used to decide." goes only with an outcome line: an update of receipts alone decides nothing. The four flat templates of this task are entered in `templates.test.ts`; `notice_update`, which is not a flat message, is pinned in `moderation-templates.test.ts`. `MailMessage` is a union of the flat message and the update, and the memory mailers keep flat messages in `sent` and updates in `updates`, so earlier callers and tests are unchanged.
- **The dispatch's daily release (7.8).** The cut-off's marker lives in the jobs' lock store, which is Valkey, under the design's key: a round asks whether it is set by taking the key for 30 seconds and letting it go, and sets it for 48 hours after steps (a) and (b). `release_outcomes` is called with the lesser of the cut-off and the database's now, since the function refuses a cut-off in its own future and the job's clock is the API's. When the store cannot be asked the release runs all the same (it is harmless twice), but only a round whose own write of the marker was accepted is the daily one, so an address still gets at most one update a day.
- **The dispatch's alerts and logs (7.8, 7.12).** The urgent repeat and the queue-age alert each run in a try of their own. A lane-0 case whose reach cannot be read counts as calling, since an alert fails open, and the failure is logged with the case's id. Case 1 of the repeat compares the newest recorded choice not to seal with `newestServedAt` to the millisecond, and a tie repeats. No log line of the dispatch carries an address, a handle or an account id: a failed update is logged with its items' ids. `sendReceipt` catches only a relay failure; any other error is logged by its caller with the step and the intake's id.
- **The composer (section 8).** Every outcome this person may choose is listed from the start; choosing No breach or Illegality not evident makes the decision final, and "While deciding" or "Decide the case" is asked only for a restriction. The category is on the page for every decision that is not a correction, since the params always carry one. A final decision without a measure offers the facts and the reason: optional, except that an admin's answer without a measure on a case with an unanswered suspect item needs the facts ("Say which it was in the facts"). The statement of a decision without a measure carries neither text (it is sent only when the decision ends or lifts something its author was told of, and then says no more than that), so the line under its fields ends "The author never receives this text: a decision without a measure tells them at most that what korbat did while deciding has ended. It stays in the case's record, for staff." in place of "The author receives this text by email and keeps it while their account exists." "Never write that the images are child sexual abuse material: nobody at korbat has seen them." stands under the facts and the reason whenever the case holds an unanswered suspect item, whatever is chosen. "Do not seal now" is offered only while deciding. Rule R2 is offered only on a case with an unanswered suspect item (6.2 step 6 refuses it otherwise). The preview shows a seal's `noEffect` as far as the page can tell, says under a seal that its numbers, the sentence about deleted posts and whether it changes anything are settled when it is recorded, is not drawn for a post case without a snapshot the page can show, and draws every paragraph through `UserText`. That sentence also covers a final decision that leaves its case open and lifts a withhold taken while deciding: it holds no measure, its statement tells the author that the restriction was lifted, and it carries neither text.
- **The console's binds and drafts (section 8, 7.4, 7.5 step 1).** The API client refuses (`invalid_answer`) an answer that carries a case other than the one it asked for, and a decision's target is the id in the page's address. A kept draft is brought back to what the case and the role offer in its kind, outcome and ground and also in its measures and its rule. The page reads the case again after `profile_changed`, `snapshot_erased` and `snapshot_mismatch` as after `case_changed`, the draft kept. A text is saved when its field loses focus, and again at Confirm when it changed since, so a reload before the first Confirm brings the texts back; a restored text that the console's own check refuses, or whose digest does not recompute, is dropped; a correction's restored reason is carried into its draft; and the slots are cleared when `/me` answers another handle. A refusal's words under a field go when the field is typed in again or when a later save of that text succeeds; a text whose save was refused or never happened is gone after a reload, as 7.4 has it. The busy flag of a confirmation is raised and lowered in one place (`confirmWhileBusy`), whatever way the confirmation ends.
- **Section 12's tests that later tasks own.** Task 7: the app's Your reports with its words and `reportMailStopped`. Task 8: an outcome changed by a review (Task 6 tests the same lines with corrections), a re-examination through the status page's link, and the re-examination lines end to end (Task 6 tests them at unit level with made-up rows). `releaseOverdue` staying 0 after a release is not asserted, since it reads the database's clock.
- **The browser check's walk (3a design, section 12).** Its list of statuses a console request may meet gains 422 (a text the server refuses) and 429 (a save over the minute's limit), which the composer's runs cause on purpose; the check has 30 runs after Task 6 (an admin's composer, a case that changed behind the page, saved drafts answered with one wrong digest, and the saves at blur counted).
- **What the status answer carries for a restriction (7.2, section 10).** `NoticeItemStatus` gains two optional members, present only on a released outcome: `rule { id, title }` and `noEffect: true`, since the words of a restriction name the rule and say when the measures changed nothing. The contracts gain `itemOutcomeWords(item)`, which the status page and Your reports both call; the no-effect read is one SQL fragment shared with the mail's lines, and a test holds the list's words equal to the mail's.
- **`requireSession` (section 5).** `createModeration`'s `requireSession` is optional, with a default that answers 401 on the four signed-in routes, because nine test constructions build the module without it; `apps/api/src/index.ts` passes the auth module's, and a unit test pins that line.
- **Images inside a quote (section 9, section 12).** A quote is one link in the app and its squares are not controls, so no viewer opens from an image in a quote embed; `QuoteEmbed` passes the quoted post's id, so a report made from the quoting post's card names the right post. An author is not offered "Report this image" on their own image.
- **What Task 7 shows of appeals and re-examination (7.6, section 9).** The decision screen shows "Appeal" with its deadline, or the sentence of a decision reversed in full; `appealUntil` is null once an appeal exists (the form is shown "while `appealUntil` is set"). An item shows until when a re-examination can be asked, without the control. Both controls, `DecisionView`'s `appeal` member and an item id on `ReportsPage` items are Task 8's.
- **Words the design did not give (section 9).** They are in `apps/app/src/lib/moderation-words.ts`: the 14 category names (the Transparency Database's), the contact's two state lines on the status page, headings, list labels and the form's hints for what is missing. A rule is listed in the form with its title and the first sentence of its text.
- **Small things (section 9, section 13).** The production refusal of a contact address lower-cases the domain before matching. `/report` sends a signed-in account without a handle to `/handle`. The success screen links `/settings/cases?tab=reports`. CI's plain export step carries no contact address.
- **A seal taken while deciding that would not be in force: the database's own refusal (6.2, Task 6's amendment).** Migration 0017 replaces `decision_actions_apply`: once the seal's list is written, a `seal_media` of a decision taken while deciding that `measures_in_force` does not hold raises `case_changed`. The read Task 6 put into `decide.ts` is removed; `recordConfirmed` is one function shared by decisions and reviews.
- **Appeals over the API (7.7, section 10).** An appeal of someone else's decision, or of one that does not exist, answers 422 `appeal_not_open`, never 404: the trigger decides, and the answer does not say whether the decision exists. The appeal route answers 201 with the decision as it now reads (`DecisionView`, which gains `appeal`); `AuthorCaseRow` gains `appeal` (the list's appeal state). `appeal_reviewer_state` is declared STABLE.
- **The appeals list and who may review (7.7, section 8, section 10).** Each row's deadline is the end of the appealed decision's appeal window (`appeal_deadline(informed_at)`). `CaseAppeal` carries `needsAdmin`, `reviewer` and `mayReview`, since the console cannot work out by itself who opened a finding in a case; the overview carries `appealsYouMayReview` beside `appealsWithoutReviewer`, because the home screen's line needs both numbers. Task 6's test that a review "waits for appeals" now expects `reviewer_conflict`.
- **Re-examination over the API (7.2, section 10).** Both routes answer 201 with `{ positions }`, one or two entries (two for an origin with its added item). The signed-out route names an item by its position and takes the newest item there, so a second request answers `reexamination_not_open`; `reexamination_exists` is answered on the signed-in route, which names the item by its id, and to concurrent requests. Each item of Your reports carries its `id`; the status page's items carry none.
- **What earlier tasks had already built (section 13, Task 8).** The console's re-examination mark on items, `notice_update`'s re-examination sentences and the statement's words for a review existed since Tasks 5 and 6; Task 8 tests them end to end (an outcome changed by a review, a re-examination through the status page's link, the lines through a real dispatch round) and changes none.
- **Migration 0018's small choices (6.4).** `release_case_holds(case_id, media)` answers the number of holds it released. `redact_r4_body` sets `erased_at` to `coalesce(erased_at, now())` on posts and images, so a second R4 decision about the same post does not move the day the content went. The R4 redactions' and the statements' candidates are read by scans of `decisions` and `profiles` (no index serves them, and 0018 is a custom migration that adds none); cases and intakes are read from indexed columns.
- **The retention job (7.9).** It asks for candidates with the database's `now()`, since every erase function checks against that clock, where the dispatch job passes the API's. It writes one `moderation_retention` audit row for each case, intake and decision it changed, since the row's metadata names one id. The setting is `MODERATION_RETENTION=on` in `apps/api/.env.example` and `API_MODERATION_RETENTION=on` in `infra/.env.example`.
- **The overview's late erasures (7.3).** `erasureOverdue`'s eight counts are read at most once a minute for all staff: a read in flight is shared and a failed read is not kept, so the overview never runs the counts for every request.
- **Modules and the command line (section 5, 7.13).** `createAccount`'s `moderation` member is optional, as Task 7 made the social module's (twelve test set-ups build the account module without it); `apps/api/src/index.ts` passes it, and a purge built without it skips the forgetting. `apps/api/src/moderation/access.ts` builds the access-request drafts, since the command line may not reach into the module, and `apps/api/src/cli/staff-cases.ts` holds the six commands' work while `staff.ts` parses and dispatches. `access-request` reads the API's whole configuration, because a contact is opened only with the API's keys (`notifierContactsFor` on the auth module). `staff.js keys` prints its count on a line of its own after each account's keys; `text-verify` also reports an audit row that names a decision korbat no longer holds. A sealed image's state is read one row at a time through `imageModerationStates`.
- **Section 12's retention tests left for Task 11.** An R4 removal made final while a suspect item waits in the same open case (the freeze is tested through the account and through a seal); a closed no-action case's snapshot erased at the first round after a review lifts a seal for good; an R4-redacted image held by a second case, across the readers and a later report; the correction that lifts a final `reset_bio` after the copies were erased; the real account purge end to end with open cases (`forgetAccount` is tested directly and its place in the purge by a unit test).
- **The export's moderation member (7.11, section 10).** `ModerationForAccount.exportFor(userId)` answers `{ decisions, reports }`, read before the archive's first byte, from the author's list read with the statement's data and from the reporter's list reads (with `noticeRecords`, one read shared with the access-request drafts), so nothing a screen does not show can be exported. Beyond 7.11's list a report carries `ruleId`, an item `position`, `reexamines` and `outcomeText` (the sentence the list shows, without which an answer given while the images were out of sight would read as a plain no-violation), and a decision its `id` and `reversals`. Left out, as 7.11 does not list them: the cited law, a re-examination's statement, an appeal's statement and the statement's source. An account module built without the moderation module writes the member with two empty lists.
- **The terms (section 9, section 12).** The version stays `draft-2026-09`. "What you can post" lists the rules by the rulebook's titles (R1 reads "sexual or pornographic content"), and a section "The rules" prints each rule's id, title and text from the rulebook with its version. A case's copies go "24 hours after the case closes, or after its newest copy if that is later" (`RETENTION.evidenceHours`), not "about a day". "Your data" also states the periods section 9's text left out, since every number of `RETENTION` is in the terms: `contactMaxMonths`, `confirmationDays`, `statementMonths`, `accessLogMonths`, `draftHours` and `mailGiveUpDays`. One number is typed, the 48 hours of the emergency switch's account age, which has no constant (the queue's SQL holds the same number); the table test allows exactly that one. The Account screen's sentence on what `export.json` holds is unchanged.
- **The smoke test (section 12, section 13).** Beside section 12's list it checks the receipt with its mail token, the export's member, and that the console's site refuses an app route. The lifecycle account sends the signed-in report before its export (a third account would have cut the runs an hour from five to three). Each run leaves one open lane-2 case on the first account's post, with a report and a notice that no run decides; one network can run it five times an hour as before and now twenty times a day, since the signed-out notice counts against `NOTICE_LIMITS.signedOut`.
- **Lock order for posts (section 2, Lock order; found by the review of the built code).** An author's deletion of a post that replies to or quotes another, and a post that both replies and quotes, read the posts they name first and lock them and the post itself in id order before writing, the same order an intake takes; the delete flow runs once more on a deadlock (40P01), because the account purge updates other accounts' post counters in no fixed order. "The R4 redaction takes the case, then the post" becomes: the cases (the decision's own and every case holding one of the post's snapshots), in id order, then the post.
- **File requests after the commit (7.10, section 5).** The purge and the disposal no longer request image files inside their transaction. Each deleter writes a per-id note into a new uploads directory `delete-owed/` inside its transaction (if the note cannot be written, the transaction fails and no row goes); after the commit the files are requested and the notes removed. `media/owed.ts` requests the file of any note whose row is gone and forgets notes whose row is still there after an hour; the sweeper runs it every pass, and the disposal runs it first and takes no batch while anything is owed. Owed deletions older than 10 minutes count in the overview's late-erasure figure. The worker never reads `delete-owed/`. `DISPOSAL_BATCH`'s "one lost batch per crash" sizing is now the fallback only for a lost note.
- **R4 redaction and later cases (6.4, step 5; migration 0019).** `redact_r4_body` erases only snapshots taken no later than the R4 decision (`taken_at <= decided_at`). A snapshot an open case took afterwards follows that case's own retention rule, so a later case's copy of a post, or of an R4-removed image's alt text, can outlive the redaction until that case's evidence is due. Migration `0019_redaction_scope.sql` replaces the function; a correction racing it is refused with `snapshot_erased`.
- **Mails are never sent twice (7.8).** The statement, the receipt and both kinds of update lock their pending row (`lock_timeout` 5 s) before the send and record the result in the same transaction; a database that cannot write sends nothing. "Leaves the row as it was ... it stays pending until step 6 gives it up" applies only to a failure before the send. Cost: signed-out updates hold their lines' rows for the length of the send, so a correction that changes one of those lines waits within its bounded lock wait.
- **What a statement says of its ground (section 9, `STATEMENT_WORDS.ground`).** Five wordings: a seal on a rule ("This step rests on korbat rule ..."), a seal on the law ("This step rests on the law: ..."; the database allows a seal in the category Protection of minors), a measure taken while deciding ("korbat is checking ..."), a final restriction without a seal ("It breaks korbat rule ..." or "korbat found it illegal ..."), and a review or correction ("That decision rested on ..."). A kept review reads "korbat kept its decision". The deletion screen and both deletion mails use the contracts' `ACCOUNT_DELETION_WORDS`, which never say "for good" or "gone", since korbat keeps what an open case holds.
- **The composer's forecast and smaller fixes (sections 8, 10, 11).** For a final decision that closes its case, the forecast of "no effect" ignores this case's own measures taken while deciding, as the database applies them; a second post withhold, or a withhold of an image this case already withholds, is not offered; after a final decision overtaken by a new item, the notice adds that what was done while deciding stays in force. The logger records a failed query by its statement, code, constraint, message and stack, never its parameters. The daily limits' refusals give the number and the wait (`DAILY_LIMIT_WORDS`). The urgent repeat reads at most `URGENT_REACH_PER_ROUND` (200) cases a round, cases beyond it counting as calling, oldest first. Not applied, for the owner's list: the console cannot say that an image row was already disposed (it would need a new contract member through a media interface, and a code of its own for the refusal).

## 19. Changed by 3e (2026-10-12)

`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-09-account-sanctions-3e-design.md` (account sanctions, cited here as 3e) builds what section 16 scoped for strikes, suspensions, handles and Explore, on this design's ledger and nothing beside it. Its migrations are `0020_fearless_absorbing_man.sql` to `0024_standing_retention.sql`.

- **Section 16** is superseded by 3e for strikes, retirement, suspensions, termination, the restriction of reporting and Explore, and by 3f for the rest. Where it differs: a suspension lives in the ledger (no `suspensions` or `suspension_lifts` table); a strike's column is `ends_at`; the reply window is 168 hours, not 72 (flagged for the owner); a reply holds the start and is answered by a correction, it does not cancel and return the case; the bridge is `auth.account_standing`; no step-up action is added; no account is held against its deletion (flagged; 3f may revisit).
- **Section 2, row Lock order.** The subject's profile is taken `FOR UPDATE` in the transactions 3e's section 5 lists (a decision that holds a suspension or a termination, a decision that answers the reply of an announced one, and the reply). The purge's residue is closed for a decision and a third account, and not for the purged account's own counterparts: there a deadlock is still detected, and `follow`, `unfollow` and `createPost` try once more.
- **Section 2, row Queue order and weight.** Per-account counts of reports now exist for 90 days, as the input of one measure with a statement, the restriction of reporting, never as a key of the queue.
- **Section 2, row What a report or notice holds.** "A notice or report naming an account is answered for its display name and bio" holds unless its answering decision holds an account measure (a retirement, a suspension at once on a threat, on terrorist content or on a flood, a termination at once); the reporter's line then carries `ACCOUNT_MEASURE_SCOPE` in place of `ACCOUNT_ITEM_SCOPE`. A ladder measure, a protective suspension and a reporting measure never answer a report at all (`open_reports`).
- **6.3, 6.4, 6.5.** A correction may have the outcome `kept`, to answer a reply or to withdraw all marks of a `no_violation` decision together. `review_needs_admin` is also true for a termination and for a measure taken at once; `moderation_apply`'s pin gains three columns of `posts`. `appeals_check` and `decision_final_at` read the later of two `informed_at` when a kept answer to an on-time reply started the measure, so the six months count from that answer, and a decision is not final while a reply filed on time to its measure waits. For the R4 redaction an R4 measure stays one of `remove`, `remove_media`, `reset_bio` and `reset_display_name`: the retention job's candidate rule and `erasureOverdue` name those four kinds and never read 3e's kinds of `measures_in_force`. `forget_reporter` also deletes the account's marks and tallies.
- **6.6 and 7.9, retention.** The retention job gains two steps, each one call in a transaction of its own with its audit row when it changed something: `reply_statements` (`erase_reply_statements()`: a reply's text once the reply is answered and its decision is final for its author, an unanswered one never) and `report_counts` (`forget_report_counts()`: the marks and the daily tallies of reports sent on a UTC day before the frame of 90 days). `RetentionReport` gains `replies` and `reportCounts`, and `RETENTION` gains `replyText`, `reportCountDays` and `standingRecord`, which the terms print.
- **7.3, 7.7.** A decision that holds a protective `suspend` answers the account's R2 finding and holds no seal, but it is no "answer without a seal": what the seal's case froze stays frozen, `suspicion.earlier` does not count it, and nobody may open the images because of it. Its appeal is reviewed at once; the wait for the authorities stays that of the seal's own decision.
- **7.11, the export.** The member `moderation` gains `strikes`, `standing`, `replies` and `reporting`, and the profile `retiredHandles`.
- **7.12, 7.14, 7.15 (words that named 3e).** The last sentence of the two staff mails `moderation_urgent` and `moderation_waiting` names sub-project 3f's phone alerts; so does section 2's sentence that the urgent mail is the only alert until the phone alert. The queue-age alert also counts the replies unanswered for more than 24 hours (`REPLY_AIM_HOURS`).
- **Section 9, the terms.** The sentence on forgetting who sent a report gains ", except through the mark of a report found manifestly unfounded (Reporting in bad faith)"; the terms gain the sections Strikes, Suspension and termination, Reporting in bad faith and Handles, list every measure of the ledger in How moderators decide, and explain `ACCOUNT_MEASURE_SCOPE` beside the outcome phrases; the list "Your reports" shows a mark with its outcome.
- **Section 10.** Redress version 2 holds the reply's texts; an order's redress is 3f's, in a later version. `ACCOUNT_MEASURE_SCOPE` joins `ACCOUNT_ITEM_SCOPE`. A `ReportRow`'s items gain `unfounded`. The statement's paragraph of the rule, "It breaks korbat rule ...", stays for every decision that holds no standing measure; a decision that holds one gets 3e's paragraph by its basis.
- **Section 12.** The terms' table test no longer treats a strike, a retired handle or a suspension as a measure the constants lack: it holds every number of 3e's sections to `STANDING_RULES`, `REPLY_AIM_HOURS`, `RETENTION` and `APPEAL_MONTHS`.
- **Section 13, Task 12's runbook.** A breach that lies only in a handle is decided with "Retire the handle" and no longer waits. The hard gate (the README's first line under Before going public) reads "until sub-project 3f's order path and sub-project 3d's handling of sealed images have both shipped", as it did since 3e's design.
- **Section 17, which sub-project owns what.** The record of reports of sealed images, the hand-over, the off-server copy, the phone alert and any hold on deletion are 3f's, not 3e's.
- **Not changed by 3e, noted for this design's builder.** A seal's own statement renders "It breaks korbat rule R2" whenever its decision cites R2 (`packages/rules/src/statement.ts`), beside drafts that say korbat has not assessed the images. 3e's protective statement does not repeat that sentence; whether a seal's statement should keep it is this design's question, with the same counsel flag.

## 20. Changed by 3f (2026-10-17)

`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-09-authorities-orders-3f-design.md` supersedes section 16 for orders, alerts, records and recovery, and changes the following.

- **Section 16:** where it differs: the watcher polls and reads no header, sender or subject (not IDLE with "headers only"); an order is given effect by a decision of the kind `order` that answers only its own item, so "lane 0 for an order's item" and "its outcome phrase for notifiers" have nothing to apply to, and nobody who reported the post is answered by the order; the hold's end is computed from the order's day and is never early, not "42 days", and for an order that is not a removal order it is the time the issuing authority states; the push is added to the staff mails, not put in their place; `reverse-window` and European Production and Preservation Orders are not built.
- **6.1:** `frozen_for` keeps its name and now also holds what is preserved under an order (a fourth arm). `snapshot_withheld` is not changed: it still means that a copy's content was erased, which is what the author's decision view, the export and `access-request` read it for. The staff case page's snapshot read gains a term of its own, `public.post_preserved(post)`, the fourth arm's test and nothing else.
- **6.2:** `decisions.informed_at` may be null for an `order` decision, for its correction, and for any decision about a post whose order notice is held, and for no other, under the CHECK `decisions_informed` (an untold decision has `author_mail` `none`); the dispatch mails no decision whose `informed_at` is null, and every read of `decisions` on the author's side, `chipFacts` among them, takes the one fragment `toldToAuthor` (`author_mail <> 'none'` and `informed_at is not null`); `decision_holds` has a branch for `order_effected`; a `final` decision that closes its case sets `answered_seq` to `intake_seq`. **6.3:** `appeals_check` refuses an `order` decision.
- **7.12:** "3e replaces the mails with the phone channel" reads "3f adds the phone channel beside the mails"; the last sentence of `moderation_urgent` and `moderation_waiting` is 3f's. **7.15** is not amended: the staff mails keep their words, with the staff handle where they name one; their pushes name nobody. **7.13:** `account-facts` becomes `notification-facts`; `access-request` leaves an order decision its author was not told of, its post and every decision held with it out of what is sent, and marks the case for the operator (`access.noticeHeld`); it prints `access.authorityHold` for an authority's demand; for a handle it runs in the migrate service, as the migrator. **7.14** is replaced by 3f's section 7 and the README's Authorities' orders.
- **Section 9:** the terms' "Terrorist content" is 3f's (W.6). **Section 10:** redress version 3, `MEASURES`' two kinds, the decision kind `order`.
- **Section 13**, Task 12's runbook: the R2 paragraph's off-server records of a report become the records of 3f's commands; the hard gate names 3f's order path and the owner's list. Task 9's test of `account-facts` is rewritten for `notification-facts` by 3f's Task 5.
- **Section 17**, What 3d receives: where it lists `frozen_for` and its callers, the predicate now means "frozen or preserved", and 3d's release adds its clause to the seal arm only. Which sub-project owns what: the question whether an account whose images are sealed may be deleted before the report is answered here (the address is held, the deletion is not).
